# Bug Fix Log

### FIXED [scope-mismatch]: the parallel worker's turns that ran while its tab was in the background never came back, and read as deleted (2026-10-03; bug id `worker-turns-lost-in-background`)

- **Symptom:** _"In the 'Parallel worker' tab I still cannot see its full history. Try again to recover it all, and make sure the bug that deleted it is fixed."_ (4th report on this tab; after `tab-history-erased` below.)
- **Measured:** nothing deleted. On disk, 14 archives back to the tab's creation (09-30 21:40); the gateway served all of them, and a fresh isolated page paged them all in (1,526 assistant rows in 35 s). The owner's page snapshot at 08:54 held one archive and the current turn.
- **Root cause:** two scopes disagreed about what a page holds. Archive paging assumed a page holds one contiguous stretch from its oldest row to now; the morning's `archiveFloor` (the oldest row) encoded that. But a background tab never receives its session's live turns, and the worker's session is reset before every turn, so a tab coming back holds older turns plus the current one, with the turns between only in archives newer than its oldest row: cut by the floor, skipped, never shown. Two more made it worse: archive rows were written at the top of the page, and under a live run `loadChat` deferred the merge on a page holding history, so the running turn's start was missing too. My floor change at 07:25 that morning made the hole permanent.
- **Fix:** `tinker-ui.md` §5.8W "a tab fills the turns its session ran while it was away". The floor cuts copies only; archive rows go in by time; entering a tab fills the reset gap (`app.ts fillResetGap`). A fourth part (writing missing rows by time under a live run, `writeHistoryByTime`) drew the prompt twice on a switch-back and was reverted the same day (a replay of captured frames, `tabs-cap1-A`: the copy gone in 3 of 3 runs).
- **Tests:** `history-paging.test.ts` (5 new: the hole placement, the watched run and the gap steps ran red against the unchanged code; the gap span and the live-run placement were written with their functions; plus 2 static guards), `chat.reset-archive.test.ts` (the reset-under-floor case red before the fix). Real browser `tinker-ui/e2e/chat-viewport/` 10/10; develop's build fails check 10 with `Dx | Da Dc`.
- **LESSON:** a cursor that summarises a page by one number (its oldest row) is only valid while the page is contiguous. Before adding one, list what can put a hole in the page: here, every background tab whose session moves on without it.

### FIXED [threshold-unit+scope-mismatch]: Claude-backed tabs showed older prompts without their answers, and Main showed nothing before 2026-10-02 (2026-10-03; bug id `tab-history-erased`)

- **Symptom:** _"There are still tabs where the history has been erased. Can you recover it?"_ (the morning after `reset-archives-never-served`, further down).
- **Measured (census of the 14 open tabs, served rows against the transcripts on disk):** 9 tabs tripped the import flood valve between 2026-10-02 19:00 and 10-03 07:00, among them US trip (592 imported rows served as 99), Keep between us (577 → 63), Chinese model costs (288 → 33), the laptop hunt (261 → 24), Sonnet (58 → 6) and Main (79 → 12, 314 trips). Main's store entry named a transcript started at 10-02 12:12; its earlier transcript `7dc2a690…` held two reset archives (534 messages, 08-22 → 09-22, and 2 more to 10-02) beside a path nothing named. Dumb opus never had a transcript: its store entry holds only its name, and its page snapshots at creation (09-29 23:25) and on 10-02 were empty.
- **Root causes:** (1) a threshold in the wrong unit: the valve capped imports at 3× the LOCAL rows, and a Claude-backed tab keeps its turns in the CLI transcript, so the cap always tripped and kept only the newest steps. The cost it bounded was gone: offline on the live stores the full merge took 24–341 ms against 45–623 ms with the valve. (2) A scope mismatch: archive paging listed the resets of the CURRENT transcript path, and `/new` moves a tab to a new path (a reset keeps it). (3) A retention sweep: reset archives were deleted 30 days after the reset (`resetArchiveRetention` defaults to `pruneAfter`), which already took the local transcript of Main's 08-20 → 08-22 (the claude-cli turns of those days survived).
- **Fix:** `tinker-ui.md` §5.8W "every earlier transcript of a tab pages in, under the page's floor". The valve is opt-in (`OPENCLAW_IMPORT_FLOOD_MAX_RATIO`); `listSessionArchives` follows a tab through its sessions' trajectories to every reset, left file and copy; `chat.history {archiveFloor}` serves only rows older than what the page holds, skips archives that leaves empty, serves copies only under a floor, and never re-imports a live CLI session; the reply's `archive.kind` labels the divider. Config: `session.maintenance.resetArchiveRetention: false`.
- **Tests:** `cli-session-history.flood.test.ts` (default merges 40 imports against 2 local rows; the valve's mechanics pinned under an explicit ratio), `chat-history-archive.test.ts` (lineage through trajectories, kinds and order, another key's files, a trajectory written later), `chat.reset-archive.test.ts` (a left path's resets, the floor, an emptied archive skipped, a copy only under a floor, no live CLI re-import), `history-paging.test.ts`. All 17 new or changed cases were red before the fix. Real browser `tinker-ui/e2e/chat-viewport/` 9/9; develop's build fails check 9.

### FIXED [scope-mismatch+fractal-anchor-is-live-only]: nothing the gateway sent on WhatsApp reached `whatsapp-history.db` after the whatsmeow switch (2026-10-03)

- **Symptom.** Four messages the gateway posted to a group on 10-02 and 10-03 were missing from `messages`, while the architect's phone-typed messages in the same group were there (`from_me=1`). The journal had `[whatsapp] Sent message <id>` for each. Because of this, the 15-minute edit guard in `message-sent-at.ts` could not find a send time after any restart and fell back to "send blind + warning". "Has the chat moved on" (`countLaterMessages` in `inbound/monitor.ts`, whose comment counts "the agent's own reply included") could not see our replies. And recovering what Jarvis said needed transcripts.
- **Census** (retained journal 2026-09-01 → 10-03, DB opened read-only). 26 explicit sends carry an id in the journal; 0 are in the DB. 61 auto-reply chunks and 54 done-separators were sent with no id logged, and the DB holds no matching row. Bot-prefixed outbound rows (`🤖…` or `⚡`): 947 in 2026-02 and 761 in 2026-03, all `source='live'` (Baileys). From 03-27 to 10-03 there are 8, all `live-wm`, most likely brought back by history sync. The last `live` row is 2026-03-27 07:23. The first post-switch `live-wm` row is 03-26 22:01.
- **Root cause.** `history/live-capture.ts` stores whatever the client raises as a `message` event. Baileys raised `messages.upsert` for its own sends. whatsmeow raises nothing for what this device sends, and the Go bridge forwards only `events.Message`. The backend switch landed 2026-03-26 20:04–22:32 (`0d1a12c107f`..`410e0d15b4f`) with the capture still assuming the Baileys contract. The edit guard's send time lived only in `inbound/sent-ids.ts` process memory.
- **Fix.** `69cb5f1ca5d`. `createBaileysAdapter().sendMessage` in `baileys-adapter-wm.ts` is the one function every send passes through. After the send succeeds it calls `history/outbound-archive.ts`. Text, quoted text, media (caption plus the media keys, so `download-media` can fetch it) and raw sends are written `from_me=1`, `source='gateway-send'`, at whatsmeow's send time (unix seconds, `resp.Timestamp.Unix()` in the bridge). An edit updates `text_content` and appends `{atSec,text}` to `raw_json.edits`; the original stays in `raw_json.message`. An own revoke sets `message_type='revoked'` with `raw_json.originalType` and `revokedAtSec`, and the row and its text stay. Edits and revokes only touch `gateway-send` rows, and inserts are `INSERT OR IGNORE`, so no existing row is ever rewritten. Reactions and polls are not archived: the thinking-reaction cycle alone would flood the DB. Best-effort: a failure prints `[wa-history] could not archive …` and the send still returns.
- **Also found.** `331a41cfbb8`. The bridge writes protojson, so a quote id is `stanzaID`, but live-capture read only `stanzaId`. Every whatsmeow row has `quoted_id` NULL: 0 of 82,835, while 4,357 carry a quote in `raw_json`. It now reads both spellings for new rows. The old rows are not rewritten.
- **Guard.** `extensions/tinkerclaw-whatsapp/src/history/outbound-archive.test.ts` (9 tests) drives the adapter into a temp-home DB. It covers text, quoted reply, media with caption, edit keeps the original, revoke marks and keeps the row, rows from other sources untouched, an unopenable DB warns and the send still succeeds, and a send made before a simulated restart (modules reset, DB handle closed) is found by `resolveWhatsAppMessageSentAtMs` at the archived time. Red before: 7 of 9 (the 2 that passed are guards that cannot fail on develop). Green after: 9 of 9. WhatsApp lane: the same 74 failing test names as develop `bc3396e8985`, 771 passed vs 762.
- **Still open.** No backfill of the sends from before this fix. The journal has no text for any of them, the "Sent message" lines carry only a hashed recipient, and auto-reply sends log no id, so a backfill could not reproduce the rows exactly. The 4,357 quote ids stay NULL on the old rows.
- **Files:** `extensions/tinkerclaw-whatsapp/src/baileys-adapter-wm.ts`, `extensions/tinkerclaw-whatsapp/src/history/{outbound-archive,outbound-archive.test,live-capture}.ts`.
- **LESSON:** when a transport is swapped, list what the old one did implicitly, not only what the callers ask of it. Baileys's echo of our own sends was nobody's feature, so nobody ported it, and its absence showed only as rows that were not there.

### FIXED [scope-mismatch+config-dead-code]: the WhatsApp plugin offered 18 message actions and 15 of them threw (2026-10-03)

- **Symptom.** `edit` through the agent's `message` tool logged `[tools-invoke] tool execution failed: Error: Unsupported WhatsApp action: edit`. From the CLI, `openclaw message edit --channel whatsapp` failed the same way and `openclaw message delete --channel whatsapp` answered `Message action delete not supported for channel whatsapp`. Second sighting of the class: `unsend` was advertised and missing until `0e5f1d69170` (2026-10-02).
- **Census** (contract test plus a probe of core's normalizer, 2026-10-03). Of 18 advertised actions, 3 worked: react, unsend, and poll, which core sends through `outbound.sendPoll`. 8 reached `handleWhatsAppAction` and threw at `extensions/tinkerclaw-whatsapp/src/action-runtime.ts:95`: edit, reply, sticker, renameGroup, setGroupIcon, addParticipant, removeParticipant, leaveGroup. 7 never got that far. group-create, setGroupDescription, promote/demoteParticipant, get/revokeInviteCode and getGroupInfo are not in `CHANNEL_MESSAGE_ACTION_NAMES` and have no target mode, so `normalizeMessageActionInput` throws `Action X requires a target.` whether the call carries a target or a `groupJid`. tsgo had flagged those seven `actions.add(...)` lines as TS2345 all along. The thrower was the plugin's runtime, not `src/agents/tools/whatsapp-actions.ts`, which nothing imports.
- **Root cause.** The advertised list and the handler were written separately and nothing compared them. The plugin also set no `resolveExecutionMode`, so a CLI action ran inside the CLI process, which holds no WhatsApp socket.
- **Fix.** `027aa1c5ecb`. edit, reply, renameGroup, setGroupIcon and leaveGroup now go all the way to whatsmeow (`editMessage`, a quoted `sendRawMessage`, `setGroupName`, `setGroupPhoto`, `leaveGroup`). edit refuses a message older than 15 minutes ("WhatsApp only allows edits within 15 minutes; this message is N min old — unsend and resend instead"), using the send time this process recorded or the history DB. With no known time it sends and returns a warning, because WhatsApp drops a late edit without an error. No longer advertised: sticker (whatsmeow-node uploads no stickers), add/removeParticipant (the Go bridge discards the `UpdateGroupParticipants` result, so a refused add would read as success), and the seven unknown names. `delete` maps to unsend, and every plugin action runs in the gateway.
- **Also found.** Quoted replies never quoted on whatsmeow: `monitor.ts` dropped the send options and the adapter had no quote mapping. whatsmeow-node's README spells the field `stanzaId`, but the bridge parses with protojson and rejects it (`unknown field "stanzaId"`, Go probe 2026-10-03). The adapter sends `stanzaID`.
- **Guard.** `extensions/tinkerclaw-whatsapp/src/channel.actions-contract.test.ts` takes every advertised action through `handleAction` to a fake listener, and checks that poll reaches `sendPoll`, `delete` reaches `revokeMessage`, and each plugin action runs in the gateway. Red before: 35 of 38 cases. Green after: 18 of 18.
- **Still open.** Auto-replies still never quote (`monitor.ts` `reply` takes text only). `history/whatsmeow-stub.d.ts` types every `wmClient` call as `unknown`: 16 TS18046 in the adapter now, 11 before. Advertising participant changes again needs the bridge to return per-participant status.
- **Files:** `extensions/tinkerclaw-whatsapp/src/{channel,action-runtime,send,message-sent-at,baileys-adapter-wm}.ts`, `extensions/tinkerclaw-whatsapp/src/inbound/{monitor,send-api,sent-ids,types}.ts`.
- **LESSON:** a capability list and its handler are two owners of one fact. Test the list against the handler, and the first time one entry turns out hollow, enumerate the whole list before fixing the one.

### FIXED [timeout-tuning]: `openclaw message send` said "gateway timeout" for a WhatsApp group post that was delivered (2026-10-02)

- **Symptom:** a post to a 500-member neighbours' group printed `Error: gateway timeout after 10000ms`. The gateway journal showed `Sending message -> sha256:244a0d41fd84` then `Sent message 3EB0538E32FE60312A1B0B (7558ms)`; the hash is the target group's JID, so the message WAS delivered. A retry on the error would have double-posted.
- **Root cause:** `messageCommand` (`src/commands/message.ts`) passed the runner a gateway with no `timeoutMs`, so `resolveGatewayActionOptions` (`src/infra/outbound/message-action-runner.ts:177-180`) fell back to 10 s, shorter than a send that waits in the gateway's queue and then takes 7.6 s to confirm.
- **Fix:** the CLI message command now asks for 60 s. Test `gives the gateway call 60 s, so a slow send is not reported as a failure` in `src/commands/message.test.ts` fails without the line and passes with it (run with `node scripts/run-vitest.mjs run --config test/vitest/vitest.commands.config.ts`; bare `npx vitest` on this file did not finish in 9 min).
- **Until it is built:** a timeout from this command is UNKNOWN, not failed. Grep the gateway journal for `Sent message` with the target's sha256 prefix before any retry.

### FIXED [display-misclassify+scope-mismatch]: WhatsApp got the tool narration, glued together, and once a trailing NO_REPLY (2026-10-02)

- **Symptom.** In the WhatsApp group "I've got special powers" the owner asked for a mic-drop GIF at 15:44. The GIF went out at 15:46:04; eight seconds later a 1004-char text followed: every sentence the model had written before its 12 tool calls ("Reading the gifgrep skill instructions…", "Tenor's demo key returned 403…", "Sending the mic-drop clip…"), run together, ending in the literal `NO_REPLY`. The owner deleted it at 16:04. At 16:36 the same group got "Running the draft reply to Luís through the `human-voice` checker…" in front of the joke it was meant to be.
- **Census** (journal 2026-09-01 → 2026-10-02 16:40). 47 bridge replies were delivered to WhatsApp; 45 opened with narration: 29 in the owner's self-chat, 8 in groups, 8 in DMs addressed by LID (one of the 45 had no preview line and was confirmed from its transcript). One ended in `NO_REPLY` after narration (15:46). The live transcripts on disk hold 11 bridge turns in WhatsApp sessions: 9 with narration before a tool (8 then an answer, 1 then `NO_REPLY`), 2 with no tool, none with an empty final segment. Replay of the live transcripts: the 15:46 turn now delivers nothing, the 16:36 turn only the joke, the 10:01 morning briefing loses its 2.3k chars of narration.
- **Root cause.** The narration contract (`extensions/tinkerclaw-tinker-bridge/prompts/narration-contract.md`) is loaded into every claude-code worker, channel sessions included. `stream.ts` `buildContent()` keeps all of a turn's prose in ONE text part; each tool start records `textOffset`, the chars before it. Tinker's read path (`src/gateway/session-utils.fs.ts`) slices at those offsets and shows narration beside each tool row. Channel delivery read the same text with no offsets, so it sent all of it. `isSilentReplyText` only matches an exact `NO_REPLY`, on purpose (upstream #19537), so a narration-then-`NO_REPLY` text was a real reply to it.
- **Fix.** `f91b2acaa11`. `attempt.ts` pipes the run's normalized message channel to the bridge (`__openclawMessageChannel`). On a non-webchat run the bridge splits its text at the last tool start (`decideChannelReply`, `extensions/tinkerclaw-tinker-bridge/src/channel-reply.ts`) and stamps the final message with `channelReply`: `{kind:"silent"}` when the rest is exactly the silent token (the payload builder's own `isSilentReplyPayloadText`), `{kind:"final", from}` when it is an answer. An empty rest keeps the old behaviour and logs `[channel-reply] verdict=whole source=empty-final-segment`; every chat-channel turn logs one `[channel-reply] verdict=… source=…` line. `run.ts` reads the stamp off the CURRENT attempt's message (`src/fork/channel-reply.ts`), checks `textLength` still matches, and gives `buildEmbeddedRunPayloads` the answer or the silent token. The stored text is unchanged and webchat runs are never stamped, so Tinker is untouched.
- **Guard.** `extensions/tinkerclaw-tinker-bridge/src/stream.channel-reply.test.ts` drives the real stream fn with the 15:46 narration and the 16:36 turn (red before the fix: no stamp), and checks webchat and channel-less runs stay unstamped. `src/fork/channel-reply.test.ts` puts the stamped 15:46 message through `buildEmbeddedRunPayloads` (nothing delivered) and keeps the unstamped leak as a control.
- **Still open.** A channel with block streaming on would still stream narration live through `onBlockReply`; no channel has it configured today. Cron runs with a delivery channel get the same treatment, since the stamp follows the run's message channel.
- **Files:** `extensions/tinkerclaw-tinker-bridge/src/{channel-reply,stream}.ts`, `src/fork/channel-reply.ts`, `src/agents/embedded-agent-runner/run.ts`, `src/agents/embedded-agent-runner/run/attempt.ts`.
- **LESSON:** when one text has two readers and only one of them knows its structure, the other one ships the structure as content. Hand the structure to the place that decides what leaves the house, and measure the leak before fixing it: the "one bad GIF turn" was 45 of 47 deliveries.

### FIXED [layout]: the CONTEXT WINDOW fold drew its own full-height scrollbar, for one stray pixel (2026-10-02)

- **Symptom.** The owner: "a very annoying vertical sliding bar spanning only the 'context window' panel". The rest of the rail was fine.
- **Root cause.** Two defects, each harmless alone. (1) `.budget-panel-wrapper .rpanel-body { overflow-y: auto; overflow-x: hidden; flex: 1 }` was written for `#budget-panel`. The static folds that later moved into MODELS (CONTEXT WINDOW 2026-08-06, EEG, THALAMUS) keep `.rpanel-body` for its colour context and the collapse rule, so the descendant selector made each of them an `overflow-y: auto` box. Nothing caps their height (the rail is not a flex container, so `flex: 1` is inert), and a capless scroll box only scrolls when something sticks out of it. (2) Something did: the call timeline's screen-reader announcer `.ctl-sr` is `position: absolute`, 1 px tall, appended last to `#cache-timeline` with no offsets, so its static spot hung 1 px below the host. Measured on the prod UI with real scrollbars: `#cache-panel` scrollHeight 163 in a 162 px box, 15 px scrollbar; THALAMUS and EEG had nothing sticking out, so they stayed clean.
- **Fix.** `5388746a6ec`. `base.css`: `.budget-panel-wrapper > .model-group.rpanel-body { overflow-x: clip; overflow-y: visible }`. A fold is never a scroller, and `clip` + `visible` is a legal pair (only hidden/scroll/auto turn the other axis into auto), so the horizontal clipping stays. `call-timeline-canvas.ts`: `.ctl-sr` pinned `top:0;left:0`. After: scrollbar 0 px, 162/162, nothing past the bottom edge; `#budget-panel` still scrolls.
- **Guard.** `tinker-ui.md` verify gate "a fold inside MODELS … is never its own scroller": asserts the fold rule keeps `overflow-y: visible`, `.ctl-sr` keeps its pin, and `#cache-panel` still carries the two classes the rule matches.
- **Files:** `tinker-ui/src/styles/base.css`, `tinker-ui/src/panels/call-timeline-canvas.ts`.
- **LESSON:** headless Playwright passes `--hide-scrollbars`, so a probe reports a 0 px scrollbar on a box that shows 15 px on the owner's screen. Measure with `ignoreDefaultArgs: ["--hide-scrollbars"]`, or compare scrollHeight with clientHeight. And when a block moves under a parent styled by a descendant selector, check what that selector now matches.

### FIXED [double-count]: the smart × cost map and the router priced thinking effort twice, so max drew 4x dearer than high (2026-09-30)

- **Symptom.** Found by the J19 r2 concept review (Astra): on the per-task view, Opus 5 at max sat 4x to the right of Opus 5 at high, while the token table says a max task burns 2x the tokens of a high one. The frontier, the dial's picks and the paper's Figure 2 were all built on that axis.
- **Root cause.** `frontierRungsFor` priced a rung as `relCost × EFFORT_COST_MULT[effort] × tokenRatioFor(model, effort)`, and `tokensPerTaskFor` already multiplies the base by `EFFORT_COST_MULT`. The chart did the same: its per-token x is `relCost × costMult`, and its task view multiplied that by `scTokenRatio`, whose tokens carry the multiplier again. The per-token multiplier was always a stand-in for burn; the vendor's price per token does not change with effort. A test pinned the square as intended ("once in the price and once in the burn").
- **Fix.** `6eb6abfa9d7` — `taskCostFactor(model, effort)` in `src/shared/tokens-per-task.ts` (the token ratio net of the multiplier a per-token cost already carries); the router's rung cost is `relCost × tokenRatioFor`; every per-task x in `smart-cost-chart.ts` uses `scTaskCostFactor`, so the envelope and the routed rung still agree.
- **Guard.** `thalamus-frontier.test.ts` "counts effort ONCE" (max/high = the token ratio) and `smart-cost-chart.test.ts` "per task, max costs … 2x, not 4x"; the envelope test compares router and chart through the same factor.
- **Still open.** Not deployed: the gateway and the built UI keep the old axis until the next build. `model-selection.thalamus.test.ts` fails 4 cases on develop before and after this change (`thalamusRoute` undefined) — a separate defect.
- **Files:** `src/shared/tokens-per-task.ts`, `src/shared/thalamus-frontier.ts`, `tinker-ui/src/panels/smart-cost-chart.ts`, their tests.
- **LESSON:** when a proxy (an effort multiplier on price) is replaced by the real quantity (tokens per task), remove the proxy in the same change; a unit check (€/Mtok × tokens ÷ 10⁶ = € per task) would have caught it.

### FIXED [pii-gate-case-sensitive+gate-publishes-its-words]: the leak hook let the employer's name through, and spelled out the private words it guarded (2026-09-29)

- **Symptom.** The employer's company and product names were on the public branch in dozens of files (docs, recipes, code comments, a task-panel schema enum, a plugin-manifest enum), and a company NAS skill with a private IP had been published whole.
- **Root cause.** `PII_RE` in `scripts/pii-pre-push.sh` was case-sensitive and knew the company only as a lowercase two-word phrase, so the capitalised product name, the upper-case company name and a robotics vendor all passed. Second defect: the pattern itself listed the architect's first name, a family name, the home city and the employer, so the public hook published the words it existed to protect.
- **Fix.** `831ab37836f` — the literal `PII_RE` keeps only generic tokens; the hook appends the pusher's home path and a private list from `$TINKERCLAW_PII_FILE` (default `~/.config/tinkerclaw/pii-private.re`), matched case-insensitively and word-aware (camelCase such as `browserRateLimiter` passes; the author byline stays allowed). `scripts/bible/pii-optics-clean.mjs` appends the same file. The tree was scrubbed in `385e5b5c237` (axes and calendar sources became user data), `be2942fa66c` (comments, fixtures) and `1b7450edb2c` (docs), plus the same on the public-main lineage.
- **Guard.** `pii-boundary.md` verify blocks (2/2) source the pattern from the hook and prove it fires; the private list is written in syntax both `grep -P` and JavaScript compile, because an inline `(?i:…)` group does not compile in Node 22.
- **Still open.** Git history on the public branch still holds the old occurrences; removing them needs a history rewrite and a force-push, which is the owner's call. Author bylines were left for the owner to decide.
- **Files:** `scripts/pii-pre-push.sh`, `scripts/bible/pii-optics-clean.mjs`, `TINKER_UI_DESIGN_BIBLE/pii-boundary.md`.
- **LESSON:** a privacy gate is only as wide as its matcher: test it against the real spellings in the tree (case, camelCase, possessives), not against the phrase someone typed once. And a public gate must not carry the secret list; keep the mechanism public and the words private.

### FIXED [double-count+observability]: one compaction was counted twice, while the count the panel read never saw the claude CLI's own (2026-09-25)

- **Symptom.** The CONTEXT WINDOW panel's THIS SESSION counters (context-window-panel.md F3a, F3d, F4): one manual compaction or eviction press booked **2** compactions; an engram compaction's `saved` figure came out **~61× too large**; the tally restarted at 0 on every reload; and on the cc-bridge lane, the lane that compacts most, the compactions cell stayed at **0**.
- **Root cause — one fact, two producers, and the wrong count read.** (1) _Double count:_ the `sessions.compact` RPC reply AND the A1 `end` event its executor publishes both reached the UI tally, and each incremented it. (2) _Wrong size:_ the stream consumer sized the drop as `tokensBefore − tokensAfter`, but on the engram path `tokensBefore` is a store-wide running total (7,855,029 on a 175,850-token session, as `sessions.ts` documents). (3) _Wrong count:_ once the `sessions.list` row carried both, the panel read `compactionCount`, the session entry's lifetime counter, which only gateway-side executors bump; the claude CLI's own `cli-internal` compactions never reach it, while the ledger's `compactions` counts every executor except EVICT. (4) _No owner:_ pi-auto's A1 targets carried no session key, so its ledger rows landed with no session and counted for none. (5) The CLI's `compact_boundary` never reached the gateway at all (the seams entry below). (6) _A second writer, too:_ the EVICT path (`src/gateway/session-eviction.ts`, A5, `bc5c36fb0c2`, landed while A1 was being written in a parallel group) published its compaction pair with its own `emitAgentEvent` call, a second writer of the stream that P7 gives exactly one owner, and it skipped the owner's absent-not-zero pass, so a NaN estimate went out on the wire as `tokensBefore: NaN`.
- **Fix.** `cdee07eca5d` — ONE increment path: nothing in the UI counts a compaction any more. A completed `end`, or a reply that compacted, only schedules a `sessions.list` re-read, and the row counts it. The `end` and the reply of one press are paired as two halves of ONE drop (`DROP_PAIR_WINDOW_MS`, 30 s) and sized once: the `end`'s `tokensDropped`, else before − after only on `exact` provenance, else the reply's `evictedTokens`; `saved` is integrated per drop. `e8fc009ca69` — paint the ledger's `compactions`, falling back to `compactionCount` only in the pre-seed window after a gateway start. `a23b9a53ea3` + `263ae1cf971` — both runner executors (engine-owned and leaf) carry the compaction's own measured drop (engram `tokensEvicted`) on the `end`: absent when unmeasured, never before − after. `812a9ab599d` — pi-auto's targets name the run's session. `a893546e726` — the eviction publishes through the A1 owner (`emitCompactionTelemetry`), so a non-finite figure is omitted instead of sent. `eb148250396` — the evicted × turns `saved` ratchet that B3 left reachable only from a test is deleted.
- **Guard.** `tinker-ui/src/panels/context-counters.test.ts` transcribes the pre-B3 tally (`preB3Tally`) and asserts its defects (one press → 2 compactions; the 61× saving; `0` painted before any read) as a CONTROL beside the fixed reducer. `src/gateway/session-eviction.test.ts` asserts the module never writes the stream literal and does call the owner, mirroring context-window-panel.md's gate 7 (one compaction emitter) into the unit suite.
- **Still open.** `SessionCounters.compactionsLifetime` is produced (`context-counters.ts:482`) and read by nothing: the tooltip that should say a fallback count reads LOW lives in `context-cache.ts`, outside that unit. Its own docstring says to delete the flag if that follow-up does not land.
- **Files:** `tinker-ui/src/panels/context-counters.ts`, `tinker-ui/src/panels/context-cache.ts`, `tinker-ui/src/app.ts`, `src/agents/embedded-agent-runner/compact.queued.ts`, `src/agents/embedded-agent-runner/compact.ts`, `src/agents/embedded-agent-subscribe.handlers.compaction.ts`, `src/gateway/session-eviction.ts`.
- **LESSON:** a fact with two producers gets ONE counter, owned by the side that sees every producer (here the gateway's ledger, not the page). Pair the halves of one event by identity; never let each half increment. And when a row carries two counts of "the same" thing, they are two different measurements: name which executors each one sees before painting either.

### FIXED [scope-mismatch]: two owners of one identity — a 0-based call index read as a 1-based ordinal, and one session spelled three ways (2026-09-25)

- **Symptom (a).** In the call timeline a run's second model call overwrote its first: two wire calls (`callIndex` 0, then 1) left ONE call record.
- **Root cause (a).** The producer (`src/infra/call-telemetry.ts`, `de82e7a412f`) numbers a run's calls from 0: an IDENTITY. The store (B5, `b181d0a623f`), written by a parallel unit of the same run, read the field as its own 1-based ORDINAL. The same drift was one step away across lanes: the cc-bridge kept its own counter, so a run that failed over between the embedded lane and claude-code numbered each lane from 0, and sharing the owner's counter without a lane key would have shifted the bridge's indexes by the embedded calls of the same run.
- **Fix (a).** `39947563790` (B6) — one parser, `parseCallFrame`, reads a call's identity as `lane:callIndex` (`wireKey`), and the store binds a call to that key. `23732b3cdef` — `allocateCallIndex(runId, lane)` keys its counter on (lane, run) and the bridge's own counter is deleted; a per-lane numbering test pins it (CONTROL, with the lane ignored: `expected [ 2 ] to deeply equal [ +0 ]`).
- **Symptom (b).** For some configs a session row showed no pending prompts while its turn was preparing, `run.live: false` while the turn ran, and a Stop did not reach the turn the row showed.
- **Root cause (b).** Three canonicalisers of one session identity disagree. The diagnostic state is keyed by the RAW inbound `ctx.SessionKey`; the reply-run registry by the key `initSessionState` derives (lower-cased, main aliases mapped); the store by `resolveSessionStoreKey`. With no `main` agent and a renamed `mainKey`, a legacy `agent:main:<mainKey>` is STORED as `agent:<default>:<mainKey>` but REGISTERED as `agent:main:<mainKey>`; a bare `x` is stored as `agent:<default>:x` but registered as `x`. Two registry lookups made with a store key missed (`deriveSessionPendingPrompts` and `chat.abort`'s settle), and the row builder's run-set read was a third.
- **Fix (b).** `8d31a82b88c` found the split while wiring the G6 probe. `81d238d82d2` — `src/gateway/reply-registry-key.ts` is the ONE owner of "under which key does the reply pipeline hold this session's turns?" (`deriveReplyRegistryKey`, `findReplyOperation`, `resolveReplyRegistryKeys`, `resolveReplyHolderKey`); the pending-prompt derivation and `chat.abort`'s settle read through it. `f97728bd1c1` — the row builder reads the run set under the holder key too, closing the GAP the first fix had named. Deliberately NOT routed: `sessions.reset` / `sessions.delete`, which rotate only the store entries they name.
- **Guard.** `src/gateway/reply-registry-key.test.ts` derives every registered key with the REAL `initSessionState` and every store key with the REAL `resolveSessionStoreKey`, never with a copy of either derivation.
- **LESSON:** an identifier has ONE owner and every reader goes through it. A 0 that means "the first" to one side and "no index" to the other, and a key normalised by three functions that agree on the common case, both pass every test built on the common case. Test the seam with the REAL producer on both ends, and look a key up where the registry derives it, never by a store spelling.

### FIXED [mechanism-starved+config-dead-code]: seams whose two ends never met — a router with no `system` arm, a probe nobody injected, a declared row nobody wrote, a helper nobody called (2026-09-25)

- **Symptom.** Four consumers that were complete, tested and merged, and heard nothing: the CONTEXT WINDOW panel counted **0** compactions on the cc-bridge lane; the `[diagnostic] stuck session` line never printed its `phase=` field in production; `j.route.decision` (J19) was declared in the events catalog and never written; and lifecycles.md's `stranded → surfaced to the user` edge did not exist (prompt-queue.md C4).
- **Root cause — the consumer shipped, the producer never reached it.** (1) `extensions/tinkerclaw-tinker-bridge/src/stream.ts` `handleLine` routed each stdout line by type and had no `system` arm, so every `system` line, the claude CLI's `compact_boundary` among them, was dropped although `worker.ts` forwarded every one. (2) `905dba34d9d` gave the stuck-session line a `phase=` field fed by an injected `replyPhaseProbe`; gateway startup never injected one. (3) `342b2e6ef02` declared `j.route.decision`; `44881e0859a` wired the other src-side `j.*` rows and not this one, because logging.md §4.12 named a browser-bundled module (`src/shared/thalamus-plan.ts`) as its site, where `emit.ts` cannot be imported. (4) `strandedQueuedEntries` was exported and unit-tested with zero call sites in `app.ts`. The ctx-timeline's per-call feed had the same shape (F9): a `round-start` / `round-complete` pair whose producers never had a caller.
- **Fix.** `d904a1e1e1f` — a `system` arm (`createCompactionLineReader`: a latched `compacting` status → start, `compact_boundary` → exact end, a failed status → `completed:false`) hands each event to the A1 owner. The mapping follows the CLI's own stream-json schema as the installed CLI (2.1.281) emits it, not a transcript: on the wire `compact_metadata` is snake_case, while the transcript spells the same fields camelCase, so a reader written from the transcript would have matched nothing. `8d31a82b88c` — `startGatewayDiagnosticHeartbeat()` passes `createGatewayReplyPhaseProbe(getConfig)`. `18ea97b55ac` then `5dea8d5fc15` — `emitJRouteDecisionFromPlan`, called from the node-only `thalamusPlan` caller in `src/auto-reply/reply/model-selection.ts` (its `margin` is `null`, never 0, when no rival house is on the frontier: 0 would read as a dead heat). `5c4118d6eb5` (U4) — LOST is derived on the activity clock and `strandedQueuedEntries` is finally called. `39947563790` + `568a2e20ca4` — the dead round pair and its consumers are deleted; the ctx-timeline reads the one `stream:"call"` parser.
- **Near-miss, caught before shipping — a listener API that filters silently.** logging.md named `run.completed` and `tool.execution.*` as bus events for the events bridge, but both are emitted TRUSTED, and `onDiagnosticEvent` / `onDiagnosticEventTypes` (`src/infra/diagnostic-events.ts:783-808`) return early on `metadata.trusted` (and on `log.record`): a bridge built to the spec would have written zero `run.done` / `tool.done` rows. `ae3a6995c41` writes those rows by direct producer calls instead; `d0000f8e322` confirmed that its two bridged producers emit UNTRUSTED before relying on the listener. The exposure stays open and quiet: none of the five `BRIDGED_BUS_TYPES` (`src/infra/events/bridge-diagnostic-bus.ts:82`) is emitted through `emitTrustedDiagnosticEvent` today (grep, 2026-09-25), and the day one of them is, its rows stop with nothing red. Owed, one of two: a gate asserting every bridged type is emitted untrusted (nearly free, because the enumeration already exists), or a listener signature that makes the caller choose whether trusted events are delivered instead of a default that discards them.
- **Guard that would have caught (3).** `src/infra/events/j-rows.test.ts` keeps a WIRED / UNWIRED map and source-checks both directions: every WIRED producer really calls its helper, and wiring an UNWIRED row turns the test red until the row moves (UNWIRED is empty since `5dea8d5fc15`). context-window-panel.md's finding pin F3a failed by design the moment (1) landed.
- **Open, flagged not fixed.** The producer comment at `src/auto-reply/reply/model-selection.ts:640-642` says the row "is written even when a pinned tier default wins the turn"; the helper's own contract at `src/infra/events/j-rows.ts:336-338` says to call it "only on a turn whose model the PLAN chose", because rowing a pinned turn "would credit the router with a pick it did not make". One of the two is a bug. Until a declared field tells pinned turns apart, a J19 analysis of this row includes turns the router did not decide.
- **Files:** `extensions/tinkerclaw-tinker-bridge/src/stream.ts`, `src/gateway/server.impl.ts`, `src/infra/events/j-rows.ts`, `src/auto-reply/reply/model-selection.ts`, `tinker-ui/src/app.ts`, `tinker-ui/src/prompt-state.ts`.
- **LESSON:** this is the 2026-06-02 META (scaffold-but-unwired PRODUCER) again, four times in one wave. A seam is not shipped until a test drives the REAL producer into the REAL consumer; a consumer test fed by hand proves only the consumer. When the consumer is a listener, read the listener's filter before designing the producer: "subscribed" is not "delivered". And a router with a closed set of arms must be able to say what its default branch dropped; a silent default is a data-loss surface with no error surface.

### FIXED [display-misclassify+event-ordering+fractal-anchor-is-live-only]: a terminal ended prompts it did not name, and a prompt the owner stopped was offered Resend (2026-09-25)

- **Symptom.** (1) A prompt typed during a running turn, which the gateway steered or backlogged, released EVERY deferred prompt of the session mid-turn, so they landed above the host turn's remaining answer (2026-06-19 bug C by another door; prompt-queue.md C2). (2) Once U4 derived LOST, a prompt the owner STOPPED before its user row landed read LOST, with a **Resend** of the very prompt he had just stopped; after a reload it did so even when the page had got it right; and the outbox backstop could re-send an unacked stopped prompt, running the turn he had stopped.
- **Root cause — terminals were scoped to the SESSION, not to the prompt they name (PQ-7).** (1) The gateway answers a steered or backlogged prompt with an early `final` for that prompt's own key; the UI read every terminal as "this session's turn is over". (2) A Stop is a session-scoped `aborted` that names no prompt key, and terminal facts were recorded only on prompts KEYED to a run, so the stopped host prompt got none; U4's derivation then found it acked, unproven and held by nobody, i.e. LOST. Once recorded, the stop lived only on the page's bubble, so a reload re-derived LOST, and `dueForReplay` never consulted it.
- **Fix.** `7a86de80b48` (U3) — `chatTerminalScope` classifies each terminal: a disposition `final` (gateway G2, `06985ead65b`) names one prompt and ends no run; a linked follow-up run (G3's `stream:"followup"` `promptKeys`) ends exactly its keys; only an unlabelled terminal stays session-wide. `a891a143865` — `ownRunTerminal`: `chat.send` runs a prompt under its `idempotencyKey`, so a terminal whose runId is the prompt's key speaks for that prompt (`aborted` → CANCELLED, which outranks LOST); the FIRST own-run terminal stands, because a stop can broadcast `aborted` after the run already answered (PQ-6). `6186bbdaf5a` — the stop is stamped on the durable outbox entry (`cancelledAt`), so a reload draws CANCELLED. `c801f804a65` — `dueForReplay` refuses a stamped entry; proof reads continue.
- **Same optic.** This entry closes C2. C1 and C8 have their own entries directly below, C4 is in the seams entry above and C9 in the page-clock entry; prompt-queue.md owns the C table.
- **Files:** `tinker-ui/src/queued-sends.ts`, `tinker-ui/src/outbox.ts`, `tinker-ui/src/msg-order.ts`, `tinker-ui/src/prompt-state.ts`, `tinker-ui/src/app.ts`.
- **LESSON:** a terminal must carry the identity of what it ends, and a consumer must end only what the terminal names; "something ended in this session" is evidence about no prompt in particular. And the owner's Stop is a FACT about the prompt, not page state: store it where the prompt is stored, or the next reload un-says it.

### FIXED [display-misclassify]: a rejected send read `queued` — two stacked badge lanes, and paint order picked the winner (2026-09-25)

- **Symptom.** A deferred prompt whose `chat.send` was REJECTED, so the gateway never got it, still wore the grey `queued` badge: the one thing it certainly was not (prompt-queue.md C1).
- **Root cause.** A user bubble's pending look came from a flag triple (`_queued`, `_undelivered`, `_undeliveredAcked`) read by two stacked display lanes in `renderMsg` (`queuedClass` / `queuedBadge`). `_queued` and `_undelivered` were both stamped at creation, and the `queued` lane won the precedence.
- **Fix.** `f5c5b803564` (U1) — a pure `prompt-state.ts` derives ONE lifecycle state from a prompt's facts. `2cc105eaf1d` (U2) — the triple is replaced by one field of facts (`_promptState`), and the badge is derived at render through `msg-order.ts` (`promptStateOf`): one state, one indicator (PQ-2). `11612a6e9ec` + `72b407b5b3d` — the dead code of the retired flags is removed.
- **LESSON:** stacked indicators encode precedence, and precedence decided by paint order is not evidence: the most alarming state is the one most likely to be hidden. Derive one state from facts and draw one badge.

### FIXED [session-integrity+restart-recovery]: a retry-ladder re-send lived only in page memory, so a reload during the disconnect it was retrying lost it (2026-09-25)

- **Symptom.** A recoverable-error ladder fire (`retry n/6`) that met a closed socket existed nowhere but the page: a reload during the disconnect lost it, no transcript row could ever prove it, and it went out as the raw text, without the injection `send()` applies (prompt-queue.md C8).
- **Root cause.** `retryLastTurn` sent a fresh `chat.send` under a new key and created no outbox entry, bypassing the durable outbox every other send goes through.
- **Fix (`eac696e809b`, U5).** `enqueueLadderRetry` writes each fire as its own outbox entry under a fresh key, linked by `retryOf` to the prompt the owner typed, before the first await. The fire is drawn as its own bubble and sent through `resendOutboxEntry`, the one outbox send path (preamble injected, session pins applied). A transport failure now leaves it UNSENT for the outbox's own replay tick instead of scheduling a second in-memory copy.
- **LESSON:** a recovery path runs only when the system is already degraded, so its "rare" failure modes are its normal case. A retry must be at least as durable as the send it retries.

### FIXED [cache-staleness+timeout-tuning]: the page ruled on gateway facts by its own clock, and kept a report the gateway had withdrawn (2026-09-25)

- **Symptom.** Pre-model waits of 21–49 minutes were measured live on 2026-09-23, and the tab went dark at 120 s on turns the gateway was still preparing (prompt-queue.md C9). A prompt could read LOST only after a UI age bound, however clearly the gateway's report left it out. And a prompt that a `sessions.changed` push once reported as BEHIND / STEERED / PREPARING / RUNNING stayed so after its holder was gone, until the next full `loadSessions()`; a stale `preparing` also kept lifting the pre-model glow's time bound.
- **Root cause — a clock standing in for a fact, and a snapshot that aged without being re-asked.** (1) `PRE_MODEL_MAX_MS` (120 s, `tinker-ui/src/pre-model-window.ts`) timed out the pre-model window on the page's clock, and nothing re-asked `sessions.list` during the window it was ruling on, so even a gateway that reported `preparing` was never heard past 120 s. (2) LOST required `QUEUED_STRANDED_MS` (120 s) even when the row CARRIED a G5 `pendingPrompts` list that omitted the key, and the snapshot was dated by when its reply ARRIVED, although a `sessions.list` that arrives after an ack may have been built before it. (3) Between `chat.send`'s ack and `runReplyAgent` (measured at up to ~15 s) no G5 holder named the prompt, so the page needed a liveness veto to keep that absence from reading LOST. (4) `buildGatewaySessionRow` sends `pendingPrompts` only when something is pending (never `[]`), so on a whole row its ABSENCE means "nothing pending"; `mergeChangedRow` merged it field by field, so the last report stood.
- **Fix.** `ba0db7c4655` (U6) — past 120 s the window is believed only while a FRESH report (≤ `PRE_MODEL_REPORT_FRESH_MS`, 150 s) says `preparing`, and `preModelReportDue` re-asks `sessions.list` from 20 s before the bound, at most once a minute; the report never OPENS a window. `0e7c3842d4d` — a row that carries a G5 list decides LOST without the age bound (the bound stays for an old gateway), and the snapshot is dated by when the request was SENT (`sessionsListAskedAt`). `44f2ae2d1dc` — `chat.send` marks its prompt PREPARING from the ack until the pipeline places it (holder C, a named exception to prompt-queue.md §6.4); `71e82bea101` corrects the page's note that said G5 does not read holder C, and records why the two page vetoes stay (a page cannot tell a gateway that marks holder C from one that does not). `ab2c237cf4d` — a WHOLE row that leaves `pendingPrompts` out deletes the stale field; a partial push never touches it.
- **Files:** `tinker-ui/src/pre-model-window.ts`, `tinker-ui/src/prompt-state.ts`, `tinker-ui/src/session-rows-live.ts`, `tinker-ui/src/app.ts`, `src/gateway/server-methods/chat.ts`, `src/gateway/session-utils.ts`.
- **LESSON:** a UI timer may bound how long the page waits for an ANSWER; it must never decide what the gateway is doing. If the page must rule after N seconds, it re-asks during those N seconds and dates the answer by when it asked. And the 2026-08-04 ui-state lesson again, on a different wire: when absence carries meaning ("nothing pending"), a field-level merge cannot represent it, so replace the field on a whole row.

### FIXED [event-ordering]: the context bar showed one composition live and another after a reload — two anatomy rows per call, last write wins (2026-09-25)

- **Symptom.** The last half of the owner's context-bar complaint (context-window-panel.md F5): at every turn end the live bar swapped to the POST-TURN composition (badge `· post-turn`), while a reload of the same tab painted the PRE-CALL one.
- **Root cause.** Since A9 a turn writes two context-anatomy rows for one (run, round). The panel host took every row's `contextSent`, last write wins, while the anatomy DB upsert and the ctx-timeline's `pushEvent` both keep the pre-call composition. The live bar was the one reader that followed arrival order.
- **Fix (`a4d74c8bdbf`).** A pure `keepsPreCallComposition(held, row)` in `tinker-ui/src/panels/context-cache.ts`; the host records which (run, round) the held composition came from (`compositionRunId`, `compositionRound`), on the live row and on the reload backfill alike, so a post-turn row of the same key cannot displace it. The row's model, provider and window still land. CONTROL: the old last-write-wins rule over the same rows draws the `· post-turn` badge.
- **LESSON:** when one datum has two snapshots per turn, "last write wins" makes the live view and the reloaded view two different products. Every reader of the datum applies the precedence rule the store applies.

### FIXED [pii-boundary]: the events writer stored raw session keys that producers put in label, id and field slots (logging.md L4) (2026-09-25)

- **Symptom.** A throwaway probe of 9 emits against the events writer, before the fix: `written=9 dropped={} leaked=3`. Only the read-side redaction (`query.ts` `redactRawSessionKeys`) kept those keys from a READ-scope client.
- **Root cause.** `sessionKey` itself was hashed, but the other slots were validated by SHAPE only: `LABEL_VALUE` and `ID_VALUE` both accept `agent:<id>:<rest>`, so a producer that passed a raw key as a label, `run_id`, `worker_id` or string field value stored it verbatim. A validator that checks what a value LOOKS like cannot refuse a private value that looks legal.
- **Fix (`3700115a16d`).** `src/infra/events/emit.ts` refuses the whole record (never blanks the slot: a blanked `worker.sample` would chart a row with no worker type) when any of those slots parses as a session key through the canonical `parseAgentSessionKey`, the same parser the read-side redaction uses, so there is one owner of "what is a session key" and not a second regex. A length check and an anchored `/^[\s:]*agent:/i` prefilter keep it cheap; `::agent:main:x` parses, so a `startsWith` guard would have missed it. Refusals count under `droppedByReason.session_key_in_column`. The same probe after: `written=5 dropped={"session_key_in_column":4} leaked=0`. A key nested inside a JSON object field is deliberately not walked; read-side redaction stays the backstop there.
- **Files:** `src/infra/events/emit.ts`, `src/infra/events/emit.test.ts`.
- **LESSON:** a store's privacy contract is enforced at the WRITE, by the same parser that defines the private thing (pii-boundary.md). Read-side redaction is a backstop, not a boundary: every reader it does not wrap (a backup, a raw `sqlite3` session, the next query path) bypasses it.

### META [gate-blindspot+merge-wipe+spec-drift]: evidence nobody exercised — a test dead since an upstream drop, a test directory no runner collects, a merge gate that exited 0 having run nothing, a path documented from a default the live caller overrides (2026-09-25)

- **(1) A test that threw before its first assertion, for five months.** The upstream chunk-21 drop (FORK 2026-04-28) removed `noteCompactionTokensAfter` and, with it, `getLastCompactionTokensAfter`. `src/agents/embedded-agent-subscribe.subscribe-embedded-agent-session.waits-multiple-compaction-retries-before-resolving.test.ts` kept calling the getter and died with `TypeError: subscription.getLastCompactionTokensAfter is not a function` before asserting anything. Its two `TS2339`s sat inside the `tsgo:core:test` baseline of 1,543 errors, and its red sat in an agents project that already carried other pre-existing reds. A sibling test ("calls runAfterCompaction when willRetry is false") still asserted the call chunk-21 removed and failed identically at both wave bases (`3e60ec36dd4`, `6ca4ae78846`). Fixed by `05c63097dda` (reads pi's post-compaction size off the A1 stream; `tsgo:core:test` 1,543 → 1,541) and `c44bcef86fe`. A third had been patched on its own on 2026-07-24 (`059582918fa`), without asking what else chunk-21 had orphaned.
- **(2) A directory no runner collects.** No vitest project collects the repo-root `scripts/` directory (the tooling project includes `test/**/*.test.ts` and `src/scripts/**/*.test.ts`, with `passWithNoTests: true`), and the one test file there is a `node:test` `.mjs` besides, so two misses stack. `scripts/merge-drivers/apply-fork-wiring.test.mjs` (388 lines), the suite proving that the merge driver restores the tier-1 fork wiring after an upstream merge (`a018114b747`, `2a13682aaf3`), runs only by hand: `node --test scripts/merge-drivers/apply-fork-wiring.test.mjs`. It guards the machinery whose failure mode is a SILENT loss of fork code (`merge-wipe`), and is itself silently not run. A unit brief in the same wave named `scripts/events-backfill.test.ts`; its implementer put those tests beside the ledgers they read instead (`81e79ce42c0`), where a project does collect them. **Still open:** nothing runs the merge-driver suite automatically, and when this entry was written (at `5dea8d5fc15`) the manual command was written only in the two commit bodies (unit-tests.md did not list it).
- **(3) A merge gate that exited 0 having run nothing.** `scripts/test-invariants.mjs` (the bible's `verify:` runner) decided "am I the entry script?" by comparing `path.resolve(process.argv[1])` with `fileURLToPath(import.meta.url)`. Node resolves the ESM entry's URL through symlinks while `argv[1]` keeps the path as typed, so when the script or any parent directory was reached through a symlink, `main()` never ran and the gate reported success. Found in the wave's final review, not in an incident; whether any past run hit it is unknown. Fixed by `181349a25ad` (`isEntryPoint` realpaths both sides; control: a symlink run went from no output, exit 0, to the real failure, exit 2). A milder cousin in the same wave: a nine-case eviction suite ran TWICE, byte-identical in two files, kept reachable by a vestigial re-export (`f2f6d18d021` deleted the duplicate, `b5f50c2173c` the re-export, and `b4177becf3b` re-aimed the guard at the failure that can actually recur: the re-export coming back through a tier-1 merge).
- **(4) A path documented from the writer's default instead of from the caller that sets it.** logging.md's source table gives the ENGRAM collector's output as `homedir()/.openclaw/metrics/<date>.jsonl`, the default in `src/memory/engram/metrics.ts:48-49`. Its only production caller, `src/agents/pi-extensions/compaction-engram.ts:151-154`, passes `baseDir = ~/.openclaw/engram`. Measured on the dev host on 2026-09-25: 82 day files in `~/.openclaw/engram/` (2026-04-10 → 2026-09-24), and `~/.openclaw/metrics/` does not exist. The backfill (`81e79ce42c0`) reads both. Trust `compaction-engram.ts` over the table until the table says the same. **A second instance, still open (found by the review panel, re-checked with `grep -rn '".openclaw", "engram"'` at `5dea8d5fc15`):** the ENGRAM root is spelled three ways. crons.md states `<engram>` = `$OPENCLAW_HOME/.openclaw/engram`, which is true of the cron it documents (`src/cron/jobs/engram-consolidate.ts:133`) and of `src/memory/engram/failure-tracking-store.ts:34` and `src/fork/skill-rpc.ts:61` (`OPENCLAW_HOME ?? homedir()`). The compaction writers use `process.env.HOME ?? "~"` (`src/agents/pi-extensions/compaction-engram.ts:151`, `src/memory/engram/compaction-reflection.ts:299`, `src/memory/engram/global-fts-bridge.ts:16`, `src/agents/pi-extensions/sleep-consolidation-cron.ts:105`, the runner's `src/agents/embedded-agent-runner/extensions.ts:186`), which with `HOME` unset would write into a directory literally named `~`. And `src/memory/engram/consolidate-cli.ts:22`, `extensions/tinkerclaw-total-recall/index.ts:45` and `extensions/tinkerclaw-prefrontal/index.ts:1052` use plain `homedir()`. The three agree only while `OPENCLAW_HOME` is unset and `HOME` is set; set `OPENCLAW_HOME` and the consolidation cron reads a root the compaction writers never write. memory-layout.md's `verify:` gates test the literal `~/.openclaw/engram/...`. Owed: one exported ENGRAM-root resolver that every writer calls, and both optics restated from it.
- **CLASS — the 2026-08-04 META "a gate's SCOPE is not its package", four more ways.** In each a check's EXISTENCE was read as coverage: one could not reach an assertion, one was never collected, one reported success without running, one described a default nobody runs. Reflexes: **(a)** when an upstream drop removes a symbol, grep the TESTS for it in the same commit; the type errors were there all along, but a baseline COUNT absorbs them, and a new error inside a baseline is invisible unless someone diffs the list, not the number. **(b)** a test file is coverage only once a runner is proven to collect it: the cheap gate is to enumerate every `*.test.*` file on disk and assert each matches some project's includes (the `auth-scope` entry's reflex #1: a registry keyed by an enumerable set needs a gate asserting it covers the set). **(c)** an entry-point guard compares REALPATHS; a gate that silently no-ops reports success, which is worse than no gate. **(d)** document a path from the call site that runs in production, never from a parameter default; `createMetricsCollector(` has one production caller, and it overrides the default.

### FIXED [failure-as-value]: ORCA died at merge-back because an agent that hit a usage limit RESOLVED null instead of rejecting (2026-09-25, cross-repo: jarvis-icu)

- **Symptom.** Twice on 2026-09-24 (wave-0924 runs 2b and 2d), ORCA died at the worktree merge-back with `TypeError: null is not an object (evaluating 'r.branch')`, and the whole run landed nothing although other groups had finished their commits.
- **Root cause.** A group's apply agent that hits a usage limit RESOLVES with `null`; it does not reject. `runGroup`'s fallback was a `.catch`, which never runs for a resolved value, so the `null` flowed on into code that read `r.branch`. The merge-back agent (`r.merged`) and the in-place commit agent (`r.committed`) had the same exposure.
- **Fix (`jarvis-icu@c8d2997`, a commit of the jarvis-icu repository, cited repo-qualified so this file's SHA gate does not try to resolve it here).** `runGroup` maps a rejection AND a null or non-object resolution to the same all-units-failed record (`failedGroupResult`), and the `parallel()` result is normalised the same way; a null at merge-back or commit is now a FAILED merge or commit with a note, never a crash. `parallel-implement.null-agent.test.mjs` runs the real workflow body with stubbed agents, 3/3; CONTROL, the same file against the previous script: 3/3 fail with the TypeError.
- **Files (jarvis-icu):** `docs/superpowers/parallel-implement.workflow.js`, `docs/superpowers/parallel-implement.null-agent.test.mjs`.
- **LESSON:** an error handler guards only the channel it is attached to. When a dependency can report failure as a VALUE (null, an empty record, a no-op-shaped result), normalise at the boundary: map "rejected" and "resolved to something that is not a result" to one failure record, and let one group's failure cost that group, never the run. Same family as the 2026-08-04 flow-registry and `fork.*` entries, where a failure was byte-identical to a healthy "nothing to do".

### FIXED [session-integrity+restart-recovery+gate-blindspot]: every page stopped saving its tabs after about nine writes, so each reboot brought back closed tabs and closed open ones (2026-09-24)

- **Symptom.** The architect: "I have been deleting the SharePoint tab many times, and every time I restart the machine, it appears again, and yet the tab 'Drets soci ERP' I leave open all the time goes back to being closed." This had survived three earlier fixes (09-16 rehydrate, 09-16 self-retry, 09-21 tombstones and tracing).
- **Evidence.** The server tab trace (`journalctl --user -u tinker-prod-ui | grep ui-state`) had not logged a single tab change since 09-23 20:23. Over the same period the gateway journal shows the Tinker page's own WebSocket (`conn=83fbc5a3`, 08:33–16:49) deleting the SharePoint (`muccguv1`) and Loop (`mudz6610`) sessions at 13:11. That delete path runs `closeTab`, which tombstones the tab. The file still held both tabs and neither tombstone. The previous night's page (`conn=58cac448`, one WebSocket from 19:48 to 07:52) did land its 20:23 close. Its 07:43 "Dad DNI" tab (`muf3w4lq`, minted by `createTab`) and that tab's 07:50 close never reached the file. So a page wrote for a while and then went silent, with the same bundle and the same socket throughout.
- **Root cause.** Since `0c46ffdad88` (2026-08-02) every mirror POST was `keepalive: true`, and nothing read the response. Chrome charges a keepalive body against a 64 KB per-page budget until the response has been consumed, so the budget never came back. With a ~6.7 KB snapshot, the 10th mirror of every page was rejected inside the browser (`TypeError: Failed to fetch`). Every mirror after it was rejected too, for the rest of the page's life. The rejection went to `.catch(() => {})`. The server never received a request, so the tab trace had nothing to show. localStorage kept every change, which is why the UI looked normal. Boot and a few tab switches used up the nine writes. After that, every close and open lived only in localStorage, which this Chrome profile wipes on exit. At the next boot the stale file won the hydrate. Measured headless against a private copy of prod-ui (temp state file): 9 landed, 37 rejected. The same run with the response body read, or without keepalive, landed 36/36.
- **Why the earlier fixes missed it.** Each fixed a real way for the mirror to be _skipped_: a failed hydrate gate, no retry, closes recorded as absences. All of them assumed that a mirror the page _attempted_ also arrived. The 09-21 tracing was placed on the server, which cannot observe a request that dies in the browser. The one line that could have shown it was the silent `catch`.
- **Second finding.** Chrome opens at most six HTTP connections to the UI host. `/api/ui-state` shares them with every proxied `/tinker/api/*` call, and `proxyToGateway` had no timeout. With a gateway that forwards WebSockets but never answers HTTP (a pinned event loop, as on 09-23), 6 context-anatomy/jarvis-mute calls were held, the mirror stalled at 5 landed / 12 pending, and the new tab never reached disk. A reboot inside such a stall loses the tabs.
- **Fix.** `10ece44108c`: routine mirrors are ordinary requests whose body is always read. `keepalive` is kept for the `pagehide` flush, which also re-sends when a routine POST is still unanswered. A rejected or non-2xx mirror now `console.warn`s and retries (2 s → 60 s), and the first write that lands carries `X-Tinker-Mirror-Failures`. Six tests, including a fetch double that models the budget; five fail on the old module (the budget test with 22 of 31 rejected). `f211a313028`: `proxyToGateway` answers 504 if the gateway has not started a response in 30 s (`TINKER_PROXY_TIMEOUT_MS`), and prod-ui logs `[ui-state] mirror landed after N failed POST(s)`. The stalled-gateway run then landed 16/16, with the create and close in the trace. `8762fec8208`: the Vite dev server's POST called `readState()` without its path, so every dev write was refused with a 500. Found along the way; the dev server has not run since 09-20. End-to-end on the fixed bundle: 40 tab switches plus a create and a close, 46/46 landed, three repeats clean.
- **LESSON:** a write that can fail must be observable where it fails. A trace that watches only the receiver is blind to every request the sender never got out, and a `catch(() => {})` on a durability write turns "not saved" into "looks saved". When a durable store goes quiet, count attempts on the SENDER before theorising about the receiver. `keepalive` is a budget, not a flag: use it only for the one request that must outlive the page, and read every response.
- **Files:** `tinker-ui/src/panels/ui-state.ts`, `tinker-ui/src/panels/ui-state.test.ts`, `scripts/tinker-prod-ui.mjs`, `tinker-ui/vite.config.ts`, `TINKER_UI_DESIGN_BIBLE/ui-persistence.md`.

### FIXED [detection-pattern]: every answer carrying a `**Jarvis:**` voice line was drawn twice, all of it (2026-09-24)

- **Symptom.** The architect, on the work tab (`agent:main:tinker:mue2cvin`): "every time he answers I see the response double." Snapshot `tinker-ui-snapshot.agent-main-tinker-mue2cvin-037e3c64.html` (09:10Z): every turn from 09:44 on ended `answer · fractal · answer-again · fractal-again`. The second copy was the WHOLE reply (narration + answer + fractal) in one bubble, sometimes opening with a literal `</span>`, with no `data-oc-id` — so it was written by the client from a live event, not by history. The gateway transcript held ONE assistant record per turn. `snapshot-dup-census.py` reported 0 pairs, because the two copies were not the same string.
- **Root cause.** Two things that were each harmless alone. (1) The gateway sends two chat finals per run (see `final-supersede.ts`): #1 is the raw streamed text; #2, the backstop, is built from `deliveredReplies`, whose text `buildReplyPayloads` had passed through `applyJarvisVoiceMarkup` — `**Jarvis:** x` became `**Jarvis:** <span class="jarvis-voice">x</span>`, with `x` trimmed. `[chat-deliver]` showed every pair differing by exactly 34 chars per voice label (2205→2273, 4170→4238, 8761→8829…), and recomputing #2 as `applyJarvisVoiceMarkup(#1)` reproduced every live length. (2) `supersedingAppendTail` looks for each shown bubble inside #2. It collapsed whitespace but still required it in the same places, and it could not see through the span, so nothing was credited and the whole body was appended. Two geometries on the same tab defeated a "tag = one space" rule: the trim ate the blank line before the next label, and the stream GLUES a pre-tool narration onto the post-tool answer with no whitespace at all (`…have run.**Jarvis:** *…`), exactly where the wrapper put `</span>`.
- **Fix.** `765947accb2`: the reply text leaves `buildReplyPayloads` as the model wrote it. The wrapper and its test are deleted, not guarded: the UI already paints the voice line at render time from raw text, the transcript never held the span, and no consumer outside the repo reads it (the WhatsApp copies of it stopped 2026-03-26). `03332595a1a`: the superseding comparison drops whitespace and voice-span tags entirely. The appended tail is still sliced from the raw body, so an answer only #2 carries still arrives. Replay of the tab's 8 real runs through the real module: 8 full re-appends before, 0 after, against both the span-wrapped body and the raw one. Tests: `final-supersede.test.ts` ("the voice-span double"; 4 of its cases fail on the old module), `agent-runner-payloads.test.ts`. The UI half is live on a page reload. The gateway half is live after its next restart.
- **LESSON:** two constructions of one reply must be the same string before anything compares them. Presentation belongs to the renderer. A transport that restyles text makes every downstream "is this already shown?" question unanswerable. The census could not see it either: a checker keyed on equal text is blind to the one duplicate that is not equal. When a "double" survives an exact-text census, diff the two bodies instead.

### FIXED [detection-pattern]: every Opus 5.5 thought was shown twice inside its own thinking bubble (2026-09-24)

- **Symptom.** The architect: "in nearly every thinking message, in the AI analysis tab, for example, the messages show twice." Snapshot `tinker-ui-snapshot.agent-main-tinker-muds33da-cc891373.html` (04:30Z): live bubbles #39–#48 of the running turn each held `<p>X</p><p>X</p>`; the history-drawn thinking rows (`data-oc-id="ext:…"`) above them were clean. Unlike the 2026-09-23 entry below, the doubling was inside ONE bubble, and the gateway's own transcript already held it: persisted thinking blocks in session `51f245a9` read `"X\n\nX\n\nY\n\nY…"` (rows 295, 522, 623 on 09-23).
- **Root cause.** Opus 5.5 at effort xhigh answers with TWO thinking blocks per API message: an empty signed block (`apiBlockIndex 0`), then a narration block carrying the visible text and ending in `\n\n` (`apiBlockIndex 1`). claude-cli transcript `68da72bf`: 17 of the turn's 30 thinking messages had that shape. claude-cli writes one `assistant` frame PER BLOCK, right after that block's deltas (captured live 2026-09-24: `start(0) → thinking_delta(0) → signature_delta(0) → assistant[thinking] → stop(0)`). The bridge's cumulative arm (`tinker-bridge/src/stream.ts`) counted the 2026-08-28 type-scoped ordinal inside each frame, so both one-block frames were `thinking:0`. The narration's frame was then compared with the EMPTY block, and `startsWith("")` let the whole text through. It was pushed a second time on top of the deltas, which had already streamed it under `thinking:1`.
- **Fix (`5488a4dd2d3`).** `createBlockKeyTracker` records the absolute index the delta path touched last, per kind, in the current message. `keyForFrameBlock` gives a frame holding ONE block of a kind that block's key. A frame with several blocks of a kind, or one arriving with no stream events, keeps the ordinal. `keysForAssistantFrame` is exported, and the handler and both test harnesses key frames through it. Replaying the real turn through the tracker: 5,988 of 5,988 thinking chars; the old keying gave 11,976 (exactly 2×), text unaffected. Tests: `stream.thinking-frames.test.ts`. Needs a gateway rebuild/restart to go live; past rows stay doubled in the gateway transcript, but the UI draws history thinking from the clean claude-cli import.
- **LESSON:** a dedup that compares against "what I have already seen of this block" is only as good as the key that names the block. With the wrong key, `prev` is `""`, and the empty prefix matches everything. Key a frame by what the stream just said, not by where the block sits inside the frame.

### FIXED [retry-storm]: chat-history-full-reread-loop-saturation — chat "stuck" for 5–17 minutes before the model (2026-09-23)

- **Symptom.** The owner's Tinker UI tabs read as frozen; a typed prompt took 5–17 minutes between dispatch and reaching the model; `sessions.json` write-lock warnings ("`session-write-lock … held for`") fired roughly every 3 minutes, each held 40–53 s; `tinkerclaw gateway call diagnostic.cpuProfile` would not connect on the first try.
- **Root cause.** Gateway event-loop ELU=1 from about one minute after boot. `chat.history` ran ~22 calls/min against open tabs holding 10–24 MB transcripts; each call did a full `readFileSync` + `JSON.parse` of the WHOLE transcript, then handed it to pi's `SessionManager.open`, which re-read the same file a second time. The single biggest caller was the Tinker UI outbox backstop (`app.ts` `flushOutbox`, a 20 s `setInterval`): it fetched `limit:1000` for EVERY session holding any outbox entry, before checking whether anything was actually due — an acked or unprovable entry kept its session polled forever. Every UI read additionally asked `limit:1000` regardless of how much had changed; a background tab's wake replaced its whole in-memory row list; and every delta rebuilt the entire transcript's HTML.
- **A second, separate profile — 14:40, AFTER a 14:43:36 restart (Task 15 below), with the transcript-read cost already being addressed in flight** — surfaced two further main-thread costs the 12:00 measurement had not named, because they had been smaller than the noise floor until the larger cost was cut: total-recall's `refreshPackInBackground` → `ftsSearchChunked` at ~35–42% of main-thread time (15–239 s of wall time per refresh), and a whole-session-store `structuredClone` inside `readSessionStoreCache` (reached from `buildSessionEventSnapshot`, `chat.history`'s `loadSessionEntry`, and task-registry maintenance) at ~20–23%. Transcript parsing itself was down to only ~2–3% post-restart, small enough that the originally planned worker-thread cold-index task (plan task 10, a `transcript-index.worker.ts`) was DROPPED as YAGNI, and the two newly measured costs were added to the plan as tasks 13 and 14 in its place.
- **Fix, six groups of commits, branch `feat/chat-history-incremental` cut from `develop` at `6ca4ae78846`:**
  - **Outbox backstop + read caching (plan tasks 1–2).** `8f323073453` — outbox backstop fetches history only for sessions actually needing proof, at `limit:200` not `1000`. `62e8965040f` — a stat-keyed `(ino, size, mtime)` result cache for `readSessionMessages` (satisfies failures.md M20's rule for the session's OWN transcript, distinct from the claude-cli import path M20 originally fixed in 2026-09-14).
  - **Incremental TranscriptIndex (plan tasks 3–4).** `54c2b2c64c5` — a per-file index that tail-parses only appended bytes (two-probe rewrite detection, not a naive tail-only check). `4b634fc3741` — serves the tree-transcript `chat.history` path from the index (39-case parity against pi's own reader). `9449048d684` — epochs unique across a registry eviction/restart (R19). `fb2354dfc6d` — registry bounded by bytes as well as count (R20).
  - **`chat.history` seq cursors + client window (plan tasks 5–9).** `136b684a6b9` — protocol adds `afterSeq`/`beforeSeq`/`epoch`. `5fc8e672451` + `836c0297424` — paging/import-floor review fixes (R23). `43724fefcc3` + `b140a10ddb9` — push events carry the matching seq; usage reads the transcript tail only. `dac11b84b2b` — per-tab `HistoryWindow` cursor state. `10c157aa0ea` … `9ba8bc2db27` (22 commits) — cursors wired into `loadChat`/`hydrateTab`/older-page-scroll/outbox, bounded per-tab memory, 3 review-fix rounds (rulings R24–R28). `fede4722de2` … `d540ed58002` (5 commits) — keyed DOM rendering, so a delta repaints only the changed bubble instead of the whole transcript. See flows.md F8 and tinker-ui.md §5.8W/§5.8X.
  - **Session-store clone elimination (plan tasks 11, 13 — closes the 14:40 profile's SECOND finding).** `540982f9ab6` — content-keyed hashing memo for skills-snapshot externalization. `4fbb75c1209` + `261ea0e02de` — single-entry save paths (`updateSessionStoreEntry`, `updateLastRoute`, `recordSessionMetaFromInbound`) adopt the just-saved store instead of cloning it. `8051e8fd7a6` + `8db0d954536` + `f605b16237f` + `1baba2e0cc5` + `5e0b4d09a4c` — single-entry read accessors (`loadSessionStoreEntry`, `hasSessionStoreEntry`) routed into `chat.history`'s target lookup, task maintenance, and the per-event verbose gates, none of which ever needed the whole store. See failures.md M21.
  - **Total-recall FTS off the main thread (plan tasks 14, 16 — closes the 14:40 profile's FIRST finding).** `cce322c416b` — single-flight, coalesced refresh scheduler (60 s minimum interval per session; a cold session bypasses it). `c40484a7f5d` + `030ab9b4e1a` + `ae626f7b4ae` + `1aded85b0ee` — exact trigram prefilter (roughly halved CPU per search; still short of the 2,000 ms target on its own). `c1a9112072d` + `c8542ef8133` + `c97f2b637a6` + `092239aba3d` — a persistent FTS worker thread (kill switch `OPENCLAW_TOTAL_RECALL_FTS_WORKER=0`; `gateway_stop` shuts it down) — cut main-thread CPU per refresh from 3.3–6.3 s to 70–110 ms, about 98%. See memory-layout.md.
- **Investigated, NOT fixed (plan task 15, stopped deliberately under its own STOP RULE — zero code changed).** The three sessions the 14:43:36 restart's `stuck session` diagnostic flagged as `sessionId=unknown` were not orphaned workers or dead processes: each had been dispatched and accepted into the reply pipeline but had never reached `llm_input` — two sibling sessions with the identical signature completed normally 21 and 29 minutes later, with no command-lane wait recorded for any of them. The restart killed turns that were still waiting BEFORE the model, not hung ones. A real, separate gap the investigation did confirm: `sessions.delete`/`sessions.reset` cancel a REGISTERED embedded run, but do nothing for a turn that is still queued or waiting on the reply-run registry — a soft-deleted session's pending turn could still run afterward, against a transcript that is simultaneously being archived. A concrete fix is written up (cancel the reply-run registry entry and the chat-abort controller inside the same delete/reset handler) but intentionally left unimplemented pending the owner's decision among three existing abort primitives, each with a distinct trap (an authorization failure that would newly break today-working deletes; a false `abortedLastRun` stamp bleeding onto the next session; or an unproven "exactly one terminal event" guarantee).
- **LESSON.** The same failure shape — an operation that costs O(transcript size) or O(store size) on a per-poll or per-save hot path — recurred independently in four different subsystems that had each been profiled and fixed before in isolation (M20's 2026-09-14 fix covered only the claude-cli import reader, not the session's own transcript reader; the session-store clone and the total-recall FTS cost were both invisible in the 12:00 profile because the transcript-read cost was large enough to hide them). A CPU profile only shows the costs that are large THAT DAY — re-profile after a big fix lands, because the next-largest cost was always there, just under the noise floor until the one ahead of it was removed.

### DECISION [config-dead-code]: TinkerClaw owns the moral code; Claude Code only reads what TinkerClaw publishes (2026-09-23)

- **Decision (the architect).** "TinkerClaw is … something bigger that survives token windows because it can switch models, so the functionalities should be native in TinkerClaw but then used as extensions from Claude Code only, like symlinks."
- **Root cause of the question.** Asked "what happens if I run out of Claude tokens?": the `tinkerclaw-moral-code` plugin had been **disabled since it shipped** (not in `plugins.allow`, zero `[moral-code] delivered` lines), so Grok/Sol and every fallback turn got no moral code, while Claude — via the bridge and the Claude Code SessionStart hook — read a pack file frozen since 2026-09-22 13:09. The Claude side worked on its own while TinkerClaw's side was off.
- **Fix (`cc8a8626161`).** `src/moral-code/contract.ts` (via `plugin-sdk/state-paths`) is the one home of the marker, the published pack path, its reader and the transcript test. TinkerClaw decides WHAT and WHETHER; Claude-side code decides only WHEN Claude's own context restarts (session start, internal compaction, bridge resume). Gateway startup withdraws the published pack when the plugin is known and not loaded. Test: a Claude attempt gets nothing from the gateway; the fallback attempt of the same turn gets the pack. Plugin enabled 2026-09-23 10:37 via `config.patch` (`tinkerclaw plugins enable` hung >20 min without writing).
- **LESSON:** a feature with a Claude-side reader and a TinkerClaw-side owner must fail symmetrically. If the owner is off and the reader keeps working, the asymmetry is invisible until the one model that carried it is gone.

### FIXED [detection-pattern+gate-blindspot]: kit-content and save-file let `<root>/../..` escape the kit roots over HTTP (2026-09-23)

- **Symptom.** `/tinker/api/kit-content` (read) and `/tinker/api/save-file` (write) took an ABSOLUTE `path` as given and checked it with `startsWith(root + sep)`. `<root>/../../.ssh/authorized_keys` starts with the root string and escapes it. With `gateway.dangerouslyDisableDeviceAuth` set, these routes answer without a token, so any page able to POST to the loopback port was a plausible writer (inferred, not exploited).
