- **Fix.** `resolveInsideRoots()` in `extensions/tinkerclaw-tinker/index.ts` resolves the path (collapsing `..`), realpaths the nearest EXISTING ancestor so a symlink inside a root cannot point out, compares against realpath'd roots, and rejects empty/NUL input; a root that does not exist yet still counts. 7 regression tests (`path-traversal.test.ts`).
- **LESSON:** a prefix test on an unnormalised path is not a containment test. Resolve, then compare — and resolve symlinks, because `path.resolve` alone does not.

### FIXED [bridge-leak]: WhatsApp history prefetch pulled old-style group chats into a DM with the group's creator (2026-09-23)

- **Symptom.** Thread, rhythm and contact-card prefetch matched history with `chat_jid LIKE %digits%`. Old-style group JIDs embed the creator's number, so a DM with that person carried context from their groups — 24 such groups in the live history DB.
- **Fix.** Exact match on up to two JIDs (`chat-jid-match.ts`); prefetches open the history DB read-only and never create it; DB + WAL/SHM kept 0600. Contact research on unknown numbers and the cross-chat hint became opt-in (`OPENCLAW_WHATSAPP_CONTACT_RESEARCH=1`, `OPENCLAW_WHATSAPP_CROSS_CHAT_HINT=1`) — a deliberate, privacy-positive behaviour change.
- **LESSON:** a substring match on an identifier is a join on everything that contains it. Match identifiers exactly.

### FIXED [spec-drift]: develop kept calling the stock dashboard a week after it was deleted (2026-09-23)

- **Symptom.** `4f88e6fb0b8` (09-19) deleted `ui/` and the `ui:*` scripts but left their callers: the update runner ran a non-existent `pnpm ui:build`, prepack required `dist/control-ui` artifacts nothing builds, two tests imported the deleted `ui/`. The cleanup existed only as uncommitted work in the shared checkout — and even that half-done: prepack still `readdirSync`'d `dist/control-ui/assets`, which threw and made prepack exit 1 every time.
- **Fix.** Callers removed; routing serves only the bootstrap JSON and 404s the rest; tests rewritten to the new truth (404s) or deleted with the feature they tested; prepack no longer reads the dashboard dir.
- **LESSON:** a deletion is finished when nothing references it — grep for callers in the same commit. Work that lives only in a shared working tree is not landed; land it or it rots.

### FIXED [display-misclassify]: system-written "user" turns (moral code, compaction summary, triage brief, reminders) rendered as the architect's own prompt (2026-09-23)

- **Symptom.** In the work tab a 65k-char bubble opening with the ethical rules sat under the architect's real prompt, in his voice; other tabs showed similar long "quasi prompts".
- **Root cause.** The model's input is user/assistant turns, so everything the system tells it is stored as a USER turn. Two paths put those on screen: (1) the tinker-bridge prefixes the moral-code pack to the next forwarded message when it resumes a claude-cli session that lacks it (`worker.ts` `pendingMoralCodePrefix`); the claude-cli copy then no longer text-matched the local prompt row, so `cli-session-history.merge.ts` kept both (agent:main:tinker:mtshq738 rows 940 local / 941 claude-cli, 08:56). (2) Every other system-to-model turn — census of the bridge's forwarded messages over 7 days: 88 cron reminders, 35 fractal-triage briefs, 34 `[reply-mode]` frames, 11 `<task-notification>`s, 6 compaction summaries — rendered through the plain user-bubble path; only `[System]` had a card (system-notice.ts).
- **Fix.** Gateway: `stripBridgeDeliveredContext` drops a leading `<moral_code source="tinkerclaw">…</moral_code>` before the user-row dedup comparison (the prefix twin of the existing `<!-- TINKERCLAW -->` suffix rule), so the import is recognised as the same prompt — needs the backend rebuild. UI: `tinker-ui/src/injected-context.ts` recognises each producer by its fixed opening and renders a labelled, collapsed "sent to the model" card (same `msg-system-auto` styling as the restart notice); role and stored text unchanged.
- **LESSON:** anything a harness adds to the model's input lands in the transcript as the user's voice. Every producer needs a fixed, recognisable opening, and the renderer needs a card for it the day it ships.

### FIXED [event-ordering+merge-wipe]: a turn the page joined mid-run was drawn twice, and thinking grew in one bubble at the top (2026-09-23)

- **Symptom.** The architect: "most of the time, the messages show repeated … if I refresh the page, the duplicate goes away" (the second copy sometimes renders raw html instead of the card), and "sometimes I see a thinking bubble up high being filled up with text, instead of appearing at the bottom".
- **Root cause.** Text deltas and `stream:"thinking"` are CUMULATIVE per run (cc-bridge concatenates across tool calls; `server-chat.ts`, `tinker-bridge/src/stream.ts`), and `chat.history` serves the rows of a run that is still running (cc-bridge imports the CLI transcript incrementally). A page that received history mid-run — a tab switched to, opened, reloaded or reconnected, and above all a background tab hydrated by `hydrateTab` / `refreshBackgroundTabsFor` — already showed the start of the run, and the live writer then began at offset 0 (`lastDeltaLen = 0`) and drew the whole run again. Evidence: snapshot `tinker-ui-snapshot.tinker-mub3arnj-e04305ed.html` (2026-09-21) — 23 history rows of one turn, then live bubbles holding the full 2,174-char answer buffer and the full 7,262-char reasoning buffer. The thinking consumer kept ONE bubble per run, created at the first thought and grown in place while text and tool rows were appended below it.
- **Fix (`1a371604e8a`).** `tinker-ui/src/live-continuation.ts` (pure, tested): a run's writer continues after what the page already shows of that run (`runTurnStart` → `runShownTexts` → `shownPrefixEnd`; scoped to the run's turn, and the first match must open the buffer, so a neighbouring answer can never anchor it). Thinking is segmented exactly like text (`nextSegmentWrite`): a text delta, tool call or block break closes the open segment and the next thought opens a new bubble at the bottom. Whole-body finals push only the unshown rest. `history-reconcile.ts` starts a run's watched window at its prompt (`_watchedFrom`), so a later gap-fill does not re-append rows the continued bubble already shows. Cursors reset whenever a page is swapped or rewritten from history.
- **Resurrected code removed again.** `mergeSentenceContinuations()` / `findSentenceEnd()` (tinker-ui.md §5.8a records them REMOVED 2026-03-20; they had come back) moved text between finished bubbles and spliced bubbles out at turn end; the >5 s pause split that fed it, the `[duprep-ui]` console log and the unused `sentence-continuation.ts` went with them.
- **Not fixed here (separate cause).** The "not delivered · will retry" copy of a session's FIRST prompt: `appendPromptKeyMarkerForChatSend` (`server-methods/chat.ts`) skips the prompt-key marker on a transcript with no assistant reply yet, so the outbox can never prove that prompt and `reinjectOutboxBubbles` redraws it on every load. **Fixed 2026-10-01** by `f81c15e456d` (the runner keys the first prompt) and `bdf1c2751ba` (an answered prompt is never redrawn); see `[chat-divergence]`.
- **LESSON:** a cumulative stream and a row-based history are two writers of the same run. Give the run ONE writer with a cursor that knows where the page's paper ends, instead of a dedup after the fact — and check the bible's REMOVED list before trusting that deleted code stays deleted.

### FIXED [spec-drift]: only Claude received the moral code, and it re-rode every spawn's system prompt (2026-09-22)

- **Symptom.** Ethical rules, objectives and the five starter-kit files were appended by the Claude bridge alone (`worker.ts` `buildEthicalRulesBlock` / `buildObjectivesBlock` / `buildStarterKitBlocks`). A Tinker chat on Sol, Grok or any non-Claude provider ran with no moral code at all, and a native Claude Code chat had none either. On the bridge, the ~40k-char pack was re-sent in the system prompt of every spawn (every turn).
- **Fix.** New gateway plugin `extensions/tinkerclaw-moral-code` owns the 7 sources (moved from the bridge's `prompts/`, now declared in `STATIC_EXTENSION_ASSETS`; the bridge's prompts never were, so fresh installs only worked via the dev-clone fallback). It builds one pack wrapped in `<moral_code source="tinkerclaw">`, materializes it at `<stateDir>/moral-code/moral-code.md`, and for non-Claude providers `before_prompt_build` prepends it whenever the session's messages lack the marker: first turn, first turn after a compaction, pre-existing sessions. Stateless, restart-safe. Claude side: `claude-plugins/tinkerclaw-core` (marketplace in `claude-plugins/`) has a `SessionStart` hook (startup | clear | compact) that emits the materialized pack as `additionalContext`. The bridge loads it with `--plugin-dir`, drops the three builders, and prefixes the pack once for resumed conversations whose transcript lacks it.
- **Trap caught by a test.** Claude Code transcripts are JSONL, so the marker's quotes are stored escaped (`source=\"tinkerclaw\"`). A raw `includes()` never matched, and every resumed turn would have been re-prefixed. `transcriptHasMoralCode` matches the JSON-escaped form too; verified against a real transcript.
- **Verification (live `claude -p`, Haiku):** the full 40,329-char pack arrives (last sentence quoted verbatim; rule 11 = bystanders); resume adds nothing (marker count 5 → 5); `/compact` re-delivers (5 → 8); the plugin loaded twice delivers once. Tests: `tinkerclaw-moral-code` suite + `moral-code-delivery.test.ts`; bridge suite 242/242.
- **LESSON:** a rule that only one runtime carries is a runtime's rule, not the agent's. Deliver foundational context from one source to every model, and check the storage encoding of the store you grep before trusting a marker search.

### DECISION [spec-drift]: the system prompt opens with the agent's own name and TinkerClaw, not "running inside OpenClaw" (2026-09-22)

- **What changed.** `buildAgentSystemPrompt` no longer emits upstream's `You are a personal assistant running inside OpenClaw.` It opens with `agents.*.identityLine`, which onboarding now sets from a new "What should your agent be called?" step (`--agent-name` non-interactively): `You are <name>, an agent running TinkerClaw, a Claude Code UI enhanced with ideas from other harnesses.` Unset, the default is `You are a personal assistant running inside TinkerClaw.`
- **Why (maintainer decision, stated on the record).** The sentence is false for this product: TinkerClaw is being turned into a Claude Code plugin — native tools, native updates — which is the integration Anthropic's terms describe. The replacement names the harness the agent really runs in; nothing about the client is concealed. Which strings Anthropic uses to classify traffic is not known, only conjectured, and is not the basis for this change either way; compliance is handled by reading the terms and following them.
- **Supersedes** the 2026-09-20 standing rule below ("ADD our own identity, NEVER SUBTRACT harness identity") for this sentence. The part of that rule that stays: never shape outbound content _in order to_ change how a provider classifies or bills a request, and state the harness truthfully. `extensions/tinkerclaw-harness-id` targets the old sentence and is now inert.
- **Verification:** `system-prompt.identity-line.test.ts` (7) green; wizard/onboard suites green apart from the 2 `onboarding.model-strategy` reds that fail identically on `develop`.

### FIXED [layout+flex-weighting]: tab-strip-width-followed-title-length-so-short-tabs-hit-the-floor-and-the-tail-was-clipped (2026-09-16)

- **Symptom:** the owner: _"The tinker ui tabs don't share the horizontal space evenly. Apparently those with more text push more and some with small text show none. They should all share the horizontal space equally except if the name is so short it does not need to waste it and can remain narrow."_
- **Evidence:** `.tab` in `tinker-ui/src/styles/base.css` was `flex: 0 1 auto; min-width: 64px; max-width: 200px`. With `flex-basis: auto` the flex algorithm scales each item's shrink by its own basis (content width), so a 32-char title kept most of its width while `📄 Latest posts` was driven to the 64 px floor; once the floor sum exceeded the strip, `.tab-bar-scroll { overflow: hidden }` clipped the tail and the last tabs showed nothing until the scroll arrows were used. Measured in headless Chromium on a fixture built from the real rules, 8 tabs in an 800 px strip: old `[88,88,140,88,125,88,88,88]`, new `[88,114,114,98,114,88,88,88]`.
- **Root cause:** shrink weighted by content width is the opposite of "share the space equally"; the only equal-share primitive is `flex-basis: 0` + equal `flex-grow`.
- **Fix:** `.tab { flex: 1 1 0; max-width: max-content }` — every tab grows equally from zero, but a tab whose title is shorter than its equal share is capped at its own content width and hands the slack back. The readable cap for very long titles moved to `.tab-title { max-width: 155px }` (it bounds the tab's max-content; 200 px outer as before). `min-width: 64px` and the `has-overflow` scroll arrows are unchanged.
- **Lesson:** `flex: 0 1 auto` is "content-sized, shrink proportionally to size" — a Chrome-style tab strip wants "equal, but never wider than needed", which is basis 0 plus an intrinsic max-width, not a shrink factor.
- **Files:** `tinker-ui/src/styles/base.css`.

### FIXED [ui-state-clear+restart-recovery]: background-tabs-frozen-until-clicked-and-detached-tabs-blank-forever (2026-09-16)

- **Symptom:** the owner: _"When I switch from one tab to another, sometimes it does not refresh fast enough. Sometimes I have to wait for around 5 seconds and the new answer appears. It seems like the responses are not sent to the UI until I select the tab. On the other hand, sometimes (mainly when I restart the computer) I switch to a tab and it is just empty (like the 'Latest posts' one). This emptiness gets sometimes restored within 30 to 60 seconds, sometimes only restored when refreshing, sometimes it never gets restored."_ Two defects, one page.
- **Evidence (the 5 s):** `onEvent` in `tinker-ui/src/app.ts` drops every chat delta AND final for a session that is not on screen (`if (!isViewedMain && !isViewedSubagent) … return`, with the comment "loadChat re-fetches … when that tab opens"). `hydrateTab` refuses to touch a tab that already has messages. So the per-tab cache is frozen at the moment the tab was left; `applyTabSwitch` paints that snapshot instantly and fires an un-awaited `loadChat()`, whose `chat.history` was measured live at 4.7–5.1 s from the CLI that afternoon (independent of `limit` — 31 rows cost the same as 1000; the gateway parses a 14.6 MB `sessions.json` per call) and 276–3,778 ms per call in the gateway journal. That fetch IS the wait.
- **Evidence (the blank tab):** `📄 Latest posts & nav` = `tab-mtzgnvmqq992` → `agent:main:tinker:mtzgnvmq`, stored with `isAttached: false`; its store entry carried `deletedAt` = 2026-09-15 22:02:33 (the only writer of that stamp is `sessions.delete`), yet its transcript (505 KB, 295 lines) was intact and `chat.history` served 440 KB for it. Two more of the 14 tabs (`mtzjjto8`, `mu26xr7a`) were stamped at 15:51:14 / 15:51:24 the same day by `sessions.delete` calls from the live page (14–19 s each). `sessions.list` hides deleted entries → `loadSessions()` detaches the tab → and BOTH transcript readers were gated on attachment: `hydrateTab` (`!tab.isAttached → return`) and `applyTabSwitch` (`if (tab.isAttached) loadChat()`). A detached tab never asked for its history at all. The diagnostic snapshot `tinker-ui-snapshot.agent-main-tinker-mtzgnvmq-ed7d9ff0.html` at 15:51:13 is a 364-byte empty `#messages`. The "30–60 s" recoveries were the OTHER path: boot prefetch is strictly serial behind a 60 s `req` timeout per call, so tab _k_ waits for tabs 1…k−1 on a cold gateway.
- **Evidence (why after a reboot):** the durable file was last written 12:52 while the page (loaded 12:17, one WebSocket connection all afternoon) opened and closed tabs until 15:51 — tabs created 09-04 and 09-14 lived in localStorage but not in the file; a direct POST of the live snapshot returned 200 and updated the file, so the server path was fine and the page had simply stopped mirroring (the fail-shut gate again: boot hydrate failed, the single `hello` retry failed, nothing else ever asked). Boot hydrate then REPLACES localStorage with the file, so a reboot resurrects tabs whose sessions were deleted hours earlier — as detached, blank, unreadable zombies.
- **Root cause:** (1) a background session's turn end was not a trigger to refresh that tab's cache — freshness was only ever bought on the click, at chat.history's full price; (2) `isAttached`, which exists to gate SENDING, also gated every READ of a transcript, so a tab whose session left the list could never fill; (3) the ui-state hydrate retried once per reconnect and otherwise stayed silent, so the file went stale for hours and the next boot restored the stale file.
- **Fix:** `app.ts` — `refreshBackgroundTabsFor(sessionKey)` runs from the background branch of `onEvent` on `final`/`error`/`aborted` and calls `hydrateTab(tab, { force: true })`, one in-flight read per tab, writing only that tab's `TabState` (no splice into `messages`); `hydrateTab` no longer requires `isAttached`; `applyTabSwitch` always calls `loadChat()` when the tab has a key; the reconnect path sets the viewed `sessionKey` from the active tab whether attached or not; the boot prefetch includes detached tabs; a tab that RE-attaches with an empty cache is hydrated at once. `panels/ui-state.ts` — a failed hydrate arms a self-retry ladder (2 s, 5 s, 10 s, 30 s, then every 60 s, 4 s budget per try) that stops the moment the file has been read; `hello` still retries immediately; success logs `console.info`. Three unit tests in `panels/ui-state.test.ts`: self-retry without a reconnect then mirror; the ladder tops out and never doubles a timer; a clean boot never arms it.
- **Lesson:** a flag named for one purpose (attachment = "may I send") silently acquires a second (= "may I read") when it is the only bit lying around; name the gate for the effect, and a read-only path must never wait on a write-side precondition. And the same lesson as the entry below, one day later: a retry that runs once is not a retry.
- **Files:** `tinker-ui/src/app.ts`, `tinker-ui/src/panels/ui-state.ts`, `tinker-ui/src/panels/ui-state.test.ts`.

### FIXED [session-integrity+restart-recovery]: a-page-that-failed-its-boot-hydrate-mirrored-nothing-for-eight-hours (2026-09-16)

- **Symptom:** the owner, after a reboot: _"I just restarted the computer and Jarvis does not have the tabs open the same way they were when I turned it off."_ The tab bar came back as it was the previous evening, not as it was at shutdown.
- **Evidence:** `~/.openclaw/data/tinker-ui-state.json` (rewritten 08:18:55, after the reboot, by the freshly loaded page) held 14 tabs whose titles were OLDER than the server's own `sessions.json`: `mtshq738` was `🗺️ Work` in the file but `🏷️ Work` on the server (auto-named 23:57), `mt79j0oy` was `🧪 HR` in the file but `🧳 Castilla test vs relocate` on the server; and `agent:main:tinker:mtsv97f2` (`🧠 Second-agent login memory`, last turn 00:41) was in no tab at all while sitting FIRST in the "just left the tab bar" segment of `sessions:panel-order`. Every tab the file did know matched the sessions touched before 22:22. The gateway crash-restarted three times that night (23:26, 00:11, 00:18 — `status=1/FAILURE`, `write EPIPE`). Server-side title patches (`sessions.patch` over the WebSocket) kept landing all night; only the HTTP mirror stopped.
- **Root cause:** `tinker-ui/src/app.ts` awaits `hydrateUiState()` exactly ONCE, as the module's first statement. When that one GET fails — the gateway mid-restart, or the 1500ms timeout on a cold machine — the mirror gate in `panels/ui-state.ts` (FORK 2026-08-04) correctly records `failed` and refuses every POST, so the page cannot wipe the file with its defaults… and then **nothing ever re-reads the file**. The page keeps working from its localStorage cache, looks perfectly normal, and for the rest of its life every tab opened, closed, reordered or renamed reaches the server session store but never the durable file. The gate was designed as fail-shut; it had no recovery path, and it reported the state as one `catch {}`. The cold start after the reboot then faithfully restored the file: the evening's state, minus everything after the failed hydrate.
- **Fix:** `rehydrateUiState()` in `panels/ui-state.ts` — retried from the WebSocket `hello` handler in `app.ts`, BEFORE `loadTabs()`, whenever `uiStateHydrateOutcome() !== "ok"`. A `hello` that just landed proves the gateway is up, so this is the natural retry point, and it runs on every reconnect. It MERGES the file into the live store (local entries win, the file fills the gaps; tabs by `id`, local first, file-only appended — `mergeUiStateSnapshot`), flips the outcome to `ok`, and schedules a mirror so the merged truth lands on disk without waiting for a user action. A page that hydrated cleanly at boot takes a synchronous early return: no second GET, no timing change on the common path. The boot failure now also logs a `console.warn` instead of nothing. Five unit tests in `panels/ui-state.test.ts` cover: no-op when boot succeeded; failed boot → merge + mirror unblocked (GET then POST); empty cache takes the file wholesale; a second failure keeps the mirror blocked; a `degraded` answer is still no answer.
- **Lesson:** a fail-shut gate with no retry is a slow leak, not a safety. If a page can enter "not durable", something must (a) say so out loud and (b) try again at the next proof of life. The forensic trick that dated the freeze: diff the durable file's tab titles against the server's `sessions.json` titles — the server kept moving, the file did not.
- **Files:** `tinker-ui/src/panels/ui-state.ts`, `tinker-ui/src/panels/ui-state.test.ts`, `tinker-ui/src/app.ts`.

### OPEN [display-misclassify]: `openclaw message send --dry-run` prints "✅ Sent via gateway (whatsapp). Message ID: unknown" (2026-09-12)

- **Symptom:** a `--dry-run` to a real neighbours' WhatsApp group (`34687775277-1584864244@g.us`), run only to confirm the command shape before an owner-approved post, printed a success line indistinguishable from a real send. Proving that nothing reached ~a whole village took several tool rounds.
- **Evidence nothing was delivered:** `src/infra/outbound/message.ts:253` returns `{ via: "gateway", dryRun: true }` before any delivery; `src/infra/outbound/outbound-send-service.ts:76` and `src/infra/outbound/message-action-runner.ts:381` both return early on `dryRun`; `~/.openclaw/data/whatsapp-history.db` held no row with the test text; `/tmp/openclaw/openclaw-2026-09-12.log` held no outbound send.
- **Root cause:** `src/commands/message-format.ts:299-319` — the `kind: "send"` branch routes `send.via === "gateway"` through `formatGatewaySummary` (`src/infra/outbound/format.ts:113`), which defaults `action` to `"Sent"` and `messageId` to `"unknown"`, and never reads `result.dryRun`. The plugin fallback at `:319` and the poll branch at `:322-344` have the same gap.
- **Fix (not applied — bookmark):** at the top of the send and poll branches in `formatMessageCliText`, `if (result.dryRun) return [theme.muted(\`🧪 Dry run — nothing sent (${resolveChannelLabel(result.channel)})\`)];`plus a`message-format` test asserting a dry-run result's text never contains "Sent".
- **Until fixed:** the only trustworthy discriminator is `--json`, whose envelope carries `"dryRun": true`. Do not infer a send from the human-readable line, and do not start distrusting real "Sent" lines either.

### FIXED [spec-drift+display-misclassify]: the-voice-script-authored-the-purple-line-a-second-time (2026-09-06)

- **Symptom:** the owner, in the torrent-scout tab: _"In the torrent skill tab I am using right now, I get more than one answer most of the time. This is a problem we've been trying to fix multiple times. What I recall from those times is that we have not detected double tokens being used, so we presume it is basically a front-end bug, but we've been unable to locate its origin."_
- **The measurement.** Session `agent:main:tinker:mtocsumz` (`54d1b065-….jsonl`): **14 user messages, 21 assistant messages.** Seven user turns carry TWO assistant siblings under one `parentId` — one `provider:"claude-code"` / `model:"claude-opus-5"` with thousands of output tokens, and one `provider:"openclaw"` / `model:"gateway-injected"` with `usage.output: 0`. The injected texts are in the spoken register ("thirty-two consecutive", "forty-four times", "two thousand seventeen") because they are the string handed to TTS. They fired on exactly the seven turns where the model ran `jarvis "…"` as a Bash call — one-to-one, with a consistent 5.3–5.8 s lag from exec start to inject timestamp. Five of the seven duplicated a `**Jarvis:**` line the model had already written in its own reply; not one was the rescue the block existed to provide.
- **Why "most of the time" and not "every time" — a second, silent bug on the same line.** The inject was built as `setsid bash -c "openclaw gateway call chat.inject --params '$PARAMS' …"`, nesting a single-quoted JSON blob inside a double-quoted `-c` string. Any apostrophe in the spoken text broke the quoting and the command died without a trace: the 2026-09-05 13:46:59 voice call, whose text contained "Sasha's", produced no bubble at all. So roughly thirteen turns called `jarvis` and only seven surfaced a duplicate — the rate was set by how often the persona happened to use a contraction. The same line also raced the transcript's creation once (`createIfMissing`), writing a duplicate session header and a `parentId: null` orphan bubble. Both are fixed alongside the gate: the call no longer goes through `bash -c`, so the opt-in path actually works.
- **Root cause — the identity line had two authors, and only one of them was still supposed to exist.** `~/.local/bin/jarvis` (the TTS script, untracked, in neither repo) ran `openclaw gateway call chat.inject` whenever `$TC_SESSION_KEY` was set — which `tinkerclaw-tinker-bridge/src/worker.ts:847` sets for every Tinker turn, and whose channel gate (`VOICE_CHANNELS="main tinker"`) explicitly passes `tinker`. The gateway handler (`src/gateway/server-methods/chat.ts:3105-3167`) then does both halves of the damage: it persists a real `role:"assistant"` record via `chat-transcript-inject.ts`, and it broadcasts that record to the UI as `state:"final"`. Meanwhile doctrine had been reversed on 2026-08-31: `workspace/IDENTITY.md` (`speak_on_home_session`) and `jarvis-voice/VOICE.md` both say **"Purple is in the reply. Audio is extra."** and, verbatim, "Do **not** wait for the script to `chat.inject` the bubble." The script was never updated to match, so both authors ran unconditionally and the line was written twice. The script's own comment recorded the obsolete trade it was still honouring: _"Duplicate bubbles are cheaper than a missing identity line in the reply."_
- **Why every previous hunt failed — three independent decoys.** (a) `usage` is hard-coded to all-zero in `chat-transcript-inject.ts`, so no second model call was ever billed and every "are we paying twice?" check came back correctly clean, which is what fixed the front-end hypothesis in place. (b) The producer is a **bash script outside both git repos**, so no amount of grepping `tinkerclaw` or `.openclaw` could reach it — "unable to locate its origin" was literally true. (c) A 2026-07-31 fix DID teach the UI about these sentinels, and it looked like the relevant fix: `tinker-ui/src/transcript-only-models.ts` exports `isTranscriptOnlyModel`, and the file's own header is about `gateway-injected`. But it is consulted at exactly two call sites, both inside `renderThinkingIndicator()`, where it only picks the spinner's model name and colour. It has never touched bubble rendering. A second decoy fix, FORK 2026-08-05 ("duprep — the 'I see my answer twice' report", `src/gateway/server-chat.ts`), was real but addressed a different mechanism entirely: client cursors indexing into a server-cumulative delta buffer.
- **Fix:** the inject is now behind `TC_VOICE_BUBBLE`, default off (`if [ "${TC_VOICE_BUBBLE:-0}" = "1" ] && [ -n "${TC_SESSION_KEY:-}" ]`). Gated rather than deleted so the rescue case — a turn where the model omits the inline line — stays one env var away. The audio path is untouched. No rebuild and no gateway restart were required: the producer is a shell script, and the UI at :18790 is a Vite **dev** server (pid 1558, cwd `tinker-ui`) that transpiles on request.
- **Verified:** with a stubbed `openclaw` on PATH — default with a live ticket → **0** `chat.inject` calls; `TC_VOICE_BUBBLE=1` → exactly 1, with correct params; `agent:main:whatsapp:*` + opt-in → 0 (the pre-existing channel gate still holds). With the real unmuted config and only `paplay` stubbed, TTS still produced a wav and called `paplay` while making 0 injects — the audio path is intact.
- **Files:** `~/.local/bin/jarvis`, `~/.openclaw/workspace/skills/jarvis-voice/SKILL.md`, `~/.openclaw/workspace/skills/jarvis-voice/templates/VOICE.md`, and the new `verify:` gate in `tinker-ui.md`.
- **Rule:** **when a policy flips from "the tool does it" to "the agent does it", the tool must be made to STOP — a policy that only adds an author never removes one.** The 2026-08-31 change correctly told the agent to write the purple line and correctly told it not to wait for the script; it never disabled the script's half, so the system quietly acquired two authors for one line. And the second author was invisible to every instrument pointed at it: zero tokens hid it from billing, a path outside both repos hid it from grep, and a real, well-written fix for the same sentinel in a neighbouring function hid it from code review. **A duplicate that costs nothing is not cheap — it is unmeasurable.**

### FIXED [display-misclassify+event-ordering]: thinking-bubbles-promoted-to-answers-by-a-partial-narration-stamp (2026-09-02)

- **Symptom:** the owner, hours after the entry below was written and its fix shipped: _"You say the reasoning compaction was fixed and yet this same chat shows me a bunch of thinking messages as final answers. What is worse, it seems to have grouped all the tool calls in one single section, which is correctly compacted, which to me it hints towards not having respected the time of arrival of each message."_ **That second sentence is the whole diagnosis** — the class split was real, and it was visible from the outside without reading a line of code.
- **The measurement.** Run 68→278 of `agent:main:tinker:mtievarb`: 210 messages — **86 `toolcall+tool_result`, 63 `thinking`, 61 `text`** — and the SERVED history is perfectly interleaved (`h T C h T C …`), so nothing upstream reordered anything. Running the REAL `narrationIndices` over that run returns **60 narration + exactly 1 answer**, and the surviving answer is the bubble that opens `**Jarvis:**` and closes with the html summary. The rendered DOM showed the opposite: one `reasoning-group` holding **86 tool-rows** followed by **61 loose `msg assistant` bubbles**. Data right, classifier right, stamp wrong.
- **Root cause — a freeze that could be PARTIAL, and a read-back that treated "unclassified" as "answer".** Both gates asked `some(...)`: `if (!runMsgs.some(m => m._narration !== undefined) && settled)` to stamp, `if (runMsgs.some(m => m._narration !== undefined))` to read. So a stamp taken while the run held only its first few messages left every later member at `_narration === undefined` — and the read-back only adds `=== true` to `thinkingSet`. `undefined` is neither, so those messages were never classified, fell through to the answer branch, and stayed answers for the life of the tab. The chronology damage the owner spotted is a SECOND-ORDER effect, not a sorting bug: the renderer emits all intermediates and then all answers, so once 60 narration bubbles are misfiled as answers the run is displayed **by class instead of by time**.
- **My own contribution to it, on the record.** The entry below widened `runSettled` with `|| nothing-is-streaming` to unblock a stranded `_temporary`. That was correct for the bug it targeted but it made the stamp fire in MORE situations, including on runs that were still growing — so it raised the odds of exactly this partial stamp. The first fix was real and stays; it was half the story, and the half it left open is the half the owner hit next.
- **Fix:** the freeze is now all-or-nothing per run. `fullyStamped` requires `stampedCount === runMsgs.length`; anything short of a complete stamp is treated as stale and re-classified rather than half-read. The 2026-08-05 guarantee is untouched — a run that IS fully stamped still reads its stamp and is never re-shuffled — but "classified" and "unclassified" can no longer coexist inside one run.
- **Reproduced and verified deterministically on the real transcript** (stamp taken after only 6 of 210 messages had arrived): OLD gate → narration=1, **60 bubbles rendered as answers**; NEW gate → narration=60, **1 answer**. 60 wrongly-promoted + the 1 real answer is exactly the 61 loose bubbles counted in the DOM. `vite build` clean; `vitest run tinker-ui/src` → **47 files / 1,660 tests passed**.
- **The owner's offered heuristic, and why it was not needed.** He suggested keying on the reply's shape (`**Jarvis:**` opening, html summary closing). Useful as corroboration and it matched perfectly — the structural classifier independently picked that exact bubble as the single answer — but it was deliberately NOT implemented: a content marker is the `💬 ANSWER` failure mode this file already records twice, and it would bind the fork's renderer to one persona's wording. Structure decided it correctly; the marker only confirmed it.
- **Files:** `tinker-ui/src/app.ts`.
- **Rule:** a memoised verdict over a COLLECTION must be all-or-nothing. When the write is gated on "has anyone been stamped?" and the read treats a missing stamp as a default value, growth after the first stamp silently reclassifies every new member — and the default is invisible precisely because it looks like a decision.

### FIXED [display-misclassify+event-ordering]: reasoning-never-collapsed-because-a-\_temporary-flag-outlived-its-stream (2026-09-02)

- **Symptom:** the owner: _"the last response you sent is preceded by a lot of reasoning, that did not collapse correctly"_ — and the automatic reasoning compaction had been _"failing lately"_ in general.
- **The measurement.** On his own live tab (`agent:main:tinker:mtievarb`), the run ending in the 3,941-char answer renders as **~30 loose `msg assistant` bubbles with no `reasoning-group` at all**. Replaying the real classifier (`normalizeHistoryRenderBlocks` → `isRunBoundary` → `narrationIndices` → the grouping pass) over that run's SERVED history classifies **229 intermediates / 2 answers** — a clean collapse. The pure decision was never wrong; something downstream of it refused to use the answer. Across the tab, 9 `reasoning-group`s exist for the older runs and none for the recent ones, which is the shape of a defect that arms itself over a session's life rather than one that is always on.
- **Root cause — a streaming mark with no owner left in the session.** The render gate read `hasTemporaries = intermediateIndices.some(j => view[j]._temporary)` and folded it into `isStreaming`, which gates the collapsed branch. `_temporary` is cleared in exactly two places and BOTH match on the run id: `ownsTempMsg` promotes only a temp whose `_runId === p.runId`, and the frozen-reasoning loop clears only one whose `_reasoningRunId === p.runId`. A history reload then does `messages = incoming`, which **destroys every `_runId` stamp** — while `_isReasoning` IS in msg-order's `CLIENT_ONLY_FLAGS` and `_temporary` is **not**, so a live reasoning bubble is preserved across the reload verbatim, `_temporary` and all. The flag now names a run that can never recur, so nothing can ever clear it, and one stale temp pins its whole run to `isStreaming` **permanently**. The `_narration` freeze is gated on the same `!some(_temporary)` test, so the run could not even be classified and re-collapsed later — the two gates failed together, which is why no repaint, and no amount of waiting, ever fixed it.
- **Why it looked intermittent:** a tab that has never reloaded mid-life has no stranded temps and collapses correctly, which is most short sessions. The defect accumulates with reconnects — and the reconnect/retry machinery that performs those reloads without a manual refresh is itself recent (2026-08-23 / 08-28 / 08-30), which is exactly the window in which "lately" starts.
- **Fix (three edits, all in `tinker-ui/src/app.ts`):** (1) the preserve loop in `loadChat` strips `_temporary` from every bubble it carries across the reload and marks it `_reasoningFrozen`, the same freeze turn-end applies — safe because that loop is only reached after `liveWriter` has been ruled out, so a surviving temp is stale by construction; (2) the render gate asks the honest question — `hasTemporaries` now also requires `streamRunId !== null || streamMsgUid !== null`, i.e. that something is ACTUALLY streaming; (3) the `_narration` freeze accepts "nothing is streaming" as an alternative proof that the turn ended. During a live turn both stream globals are set, so the streaming path is bit-for-bit unchanged.
- **Not the blinking-chat-text bug:** that regression (removed twice, 2026-03-26 and 2026-05-29, bible §5.8) came from an `isCurrentRun` guard inside the narration CLASSIFIER, which made settled bubbles flip style on every delta. This guard sits only in the "has the turn ended" test and never touches classification.
- **Files:** `tinker-ui/src/app.ts`.
- **Verified:** `vite build` clean; `vitest run tinker-ui/src` → **47 files / 1,648 tests passed**. The UI is served by the Vite dev server from SOURCE (`:18790`), so the fix is live on the next tab reload — no gateway restart involved.
- **Found alongside, NOT fixed (separate defect, needs a call on what the user should see):** `normalizeHistoryRenderBlocks` rewrites any assistant message that carries `thinking` but no `text` into `rec.content = [{type:"text", …}]` — **replacing the whole array and destroying the `tool_use` blocks that lived in the same message**. Measured on this session: **86 tool blocks destroyed** in a 400-message window, **68 of them in the single failing run**. Compounding it, reloaded tool results arrive as role `toolResult` with blocks of type `text`, so `globalResultMap` indexes none of them and every reloaded tool row renders a permanent "running" spinner. Fixing it properly needs `renderMsg`'s early-returning `_isReasoning` branch to emit tool rows too, so it is a deliberate change to what reaches the screen rather than a one-liner.
- **SUPERSEDED IN PART, same day — read the entry above this one.** The diagnosis and fix here are correct but INCOMPLETE: the owner reported the same symptom hours later, from a different cause (a PARTIAL `_narration` stamp). The `runSettled` widening described above also made that second bug more likely by letting the stamp fire on runs that were still growing. Do not cite this entry alone as "reasoning compaction is fixed".
- **Rule:** a flag that means "this is happening right now" must be answerable from the thing that is happening, not from a mark left on the data. When the only clearers of a mark are keyed on an id that another code path is free to erase, the mark is not state — it is a leak with a timer.

### FIXED [display-misclassify+detection-pattern]: injected-instructions-wore-the-architects-voice (2026-08-28)

- **Symptom:** the architect: _"sometimes, upon what I can assume was the use of a recipe, the whole text of the recipe was shown as if it was one of my prompts. This is a bug."_
- **The measurement.** His turn of 2026-08-28 12:06 is two typed lines — _"You seem stuck again … Also create a BROCA-recipe with the present strategy we are using for chosing a name for a product"_ — and is stored as **15,753 characters**: his words plus the entire fractal doctrine. Across 1,114 sessions and 5,795 user turns (2026-08-01 → 08-28), **676 further turns** carried a large appended block that matched no sentinel at all.
- **Root cause — an allowlist where a rule belonged.** Several subsystems APPEND instructions-to-the-model to the typed text before sending (fractal doctrine, morning briefing, a matched BROCA recipe). The gateway persists the concatenation as the USER turn, because that is what was actually sent. `reconstructInjectionFields` (app.ts) was supposed to split the two back apart, but it decided _whether a turn contained an injection_ by testing it against **four hard-coded sentence fragments**. That is an allowlist, so it is wrong by construction: every injector not on the list renders in the human's own voice, and a matched recipe was never on the list. The bug is not that a wording was missed — it is that the design required every future injector to remember to add itself.
- **Why nobody caught it:** the two injectors that ARE on the list are the two that fire on nearly every turn, so the mechanism looked like it worked. The failure only surfaces for the rare injector — which is exactly the one a human notices, because an unfamiliar wall of text suddenly appears above their own sentence.
- **Fix:** the allowlist is replaced by a structural split in the new pure module `tinker-ui/src/injected-prompt.ts`. It finds the `---` rule separating typed text from an appended block and classifies the block by what it _is_ (`briefing` / `fractal` / `recipe` / `system` / `directive`); known sentinels survive only as LABELS, never as the admission test. Candidate rules are tried last-first so the human keeps as much of their own text as the evidence allows — safe because all 342 fractal-injected turns of 2026-08 contain exactly one `---`. The renderer now labels the fold by what was appended, so a recipe injection no longer offers a link to `fractal-prompt.md`.
- **The constraint that shaped it:** never mislabel a human. Folding a machine block is a visible improvement; folding a sentence the architect actually typed is a worse regression than the bug. Every heuristic returns null when unsure. Verified against the live transcripts: of 5,795 real user turns, 1,682 fold (1,006 `fractal`, 676 `directive`) and **every one of the 676 is the same machine family** (`# Finished-turn digest`) — zero human turns folded. The reported turn now shows 256 characters instead of 15,753.
- **Shipped alongside:** the architect also asked to _"see a particular message in the chat, just a short reminder that we are using it with a link to its md"_ whenever a BROCA recipe is used. The `matched`/`merged` trail event already fires at exactly that moment; it now carries `recipeTitle` + `recipePath` (the matcher's index already resolved the absolute path for lazy step parsing), and the chat draws a one-line 🍳 notice under the prompt that matched, linking the recipe's own `recipe.md` via the standard `.fs-link` convention. **Design record (added 2026-09-01, the day the owner asked where the bible put this):** `tinker-ui.md` §5.8N is the owner. This paragraph is forensic, not the spec. The 2026-08-29 skill-announce extension lives in workspace `AGENTS.md`, not as a second UI chip.
- **Files:** `tinker-ui/src/injected-prompt.ts` (new, + tests), `tinker-ui/src/app.ts`, `tinker-ui/src/panels/prefrontal-tree.ts`, `tinker-ui/src/styles/base.css`, `extensions/tinkerclaw-prefrontal/{index,recipe-matcher}.ts`.
- **Rule:** when code must recognise "did another subsystem do X to this data?", an allowlist of known strings is a bug with a delay fuse. Decide it from STRUCTURE, and let the known names degrade into labels. A list that every future contributor must remember to extend is not a mechanism — it is a hope.

### FIXED [spec-drift+session-integrity]: model-picker-Auto-cleared-the-label-but-not-the-routing (2026-08-06)

- **Symptom:** the architect selected **Auto** in a Tinker tab's model picker and the turn ran on **qwen** anyway — a qwen thinking indicator, on a tab whose picker read Auto. It looked like Auto had "improvised" mid-turn after starting on opus.
- **Not what it looked like.** No run ever changed model mid-turn: every `runId` in the forensic dumps carries exactly one model. Model changes happen strictly BETWEEN runs, and a chunk of the qwen runs in that tab were **fractal-reflection child runs** (their run ids match the `forensic-agent_main_fractal-reflection_*` files one-for-one). The thinking indicator re-renders each tick from the newest run belonging to the viewed session, so it relabelled from the turn's opus run to a qwen child run. Same family as "panel glow is not traffic": the row shows you _a_ run, not _your_ run.
- **Root cause — an asymmetric control.** `chat.send`'s `model` param does not set a per-turn model; the gateway persists it as a durable `modelOverride`/`providerOverride` on the session entry (`modelOverrideSource:"user"`). Auto, by design, **omits** the param — `app.ts` only deleted the client-side `modelPinBySession` entry and wrote localStorage. So the picker could SET a server pin and had **no way to clear one**. A model-less send then resolved against the surviving override, never against `agents.defaults.model.primary`. Two of three live Tinker tabs were carrying `modelOverride: qwen/qwen3.8-max` while their pickers read Auto.
- **Why it stayed hidden:** the failure is silent and looks like a routing decision. Auto is _supposed_ to hand model choice away, so a non-default model under Auto reads as the allocator working, not as a stuck pin. (`agents.defaults.model` is `{primary: claude-code/claude-opus-5, fallbacks: []}` — there is no chain that could have produced qwen.)
- **Fix:** the picker now patches its selection through on every press — `sessions.patch { key, model: <id> | null }`. `null` reaches `applyModelOverrideToSessionEntry({isDefault:true})`, which deletes `modelOverride`/`providerOverride`. The webchat guard grew a second narrow carve-out, `isModelOnlyPatch`, beside the 2026-06-10 `isDisplayNameOnlyPatch`: `model` is the only mutable field allowed through, and only when it is the whole patch. **This grants the client no new authority** — it could already WRITE an override via `chat.send`; it just could never clear one.
- **The old comment was half-right and that is what cost the time.** `app.ts` asserted webchat "can't patch metadata" and that `sessions.update` is not a real method. The second half is true; the first is not. The method is **`sessions.patch`**, it has existed throughout, and its schema already accepted `model: null`. A correct sentence sitting next to a wrong one reads as one verified claim.
- **Verification:** differential, both directions — with the carve-out removed the new test fails (`expected false to be true` on the clear), with it in place it passes. Pre-existing failures in the same file (2 in the gateway suite, 4 in tinker-ui) were baselined on a clean `develop` worktree at the same commit and are unchanged. `tsgo:core`: 293 errors before and after, identical error sets, none in the changed files.
- **Files:** `src/gateway/server-methods/sessions.ts`, `src/gateway/server.sessions.gateway-server-sessions-a.test.ts`, `tinker-ui/src/app.ts`, `TINKER_UI_DESIGN_BIBLE/right-rail-interaction.md`.
- **Still open — the EFFORT axis has the identical shape and no fix.** `thinkingLevel` is not in the carve-out, `chat.send` omits `thinking` when unpinned, and the command layer only assigns when truthy. Auto on the effort slider is still a client-only clear with the server level surviving. `right-rail-interaction.md` §6 already described BOTH traps before this fix; the model half is now closed, the effort half is not.
- **Rule:** a control that can only SET is not a control. Whenever a UI writes durable state through a side channel (a send param, a directive), check that its "off"/"default" position has a path to **unset** — omitting the field is not clearing it.

### FIXED [answer-was-in-memory-but-not-in-context]: agent-decided-a-rule-was-pointless-without-knowing-why-it-existed (2026-08-05)

- **Symptom:** the agent was asked whether an inherited lint rule (extensions must not import core by relative path) still applied to this fork. It answered confidently that the rule bought us nothing, proposed ~65 file edits to satisfy it "if we care", and then proposed the opposite — exempting our own extensions from the gate entirely. Both answers were wrong. It took four rounds of the architect asking plain questions to correct it.
- **What made it wrong, in one sentence:** we publish plugins to ClawHub, ClawHub installs them into a **plain OpenClaw that does not contain our code**, and an import like `../../src/foo.js` points at a file that only exists on our machine. On a user's machine the plugin simply fails to load.
- **Root cause — this is the part worth remembering.** The agent was _not missing information_. That fact was already written down, correctly, in its own memory (`project_clawhub_inbound_marketing_plan`). It was never pulled into the conversation, because memory is fetched by resemblance to the question, and **"does this lint rule apply to us?" does not resemble "ClawHub is how we get downloads."** The two sentences share no words and no topic. The rule's _purpose_ lives in the business plan; the _question_ lived in the code. Nothing connects them except knowing both at once.
- **Why it looked fine from the inside:** every individual step checked out. The agent read the checker, traced its git history to upstream, confirmed nothing is published to npm, confirmed there are no more upstream merges, and concluded correctly that _upstream's_ reason does not apply here. The reasoning was sound. The conclusion was wrong because a premise was missing, and a missing premise leaves no trace — you cannot notice the absence of something you never had.
- **Fix:** the rule is now enforced inside ORCA in both forms — stated in the drafting prompt for any unit touching `extensions/**`, and appended to that unit's verify command so a violating patch **fails** instead of being advised. Both copies of ORCA updated (the shared cross-project copy was 55 lines stale — a rule set in one of two copies is not set).
- **Files:** `docs/superpowers/parallel-implement.workflow.js` and `~/src/.claude_global/workflows/parallel-implement.js` (jarvis-icu repo); memory `feedback_plugins_must_run_on_vanilla_openclaw.md`.
- **Rule:** before deciding an inherited constraint no longer applies, ask whether **we** have our own reason for it. _"Their reason doesn't apply to us"_ is not _"no reason applies to us."_ The two feel identical from inside the analysis and only one of them is a conclusion.
- **Second rule, from the near-miss:** when you do exempt something, define the exemption from something **checkable**. The agent's first attempt matched directory names starting `tinkerclaw-`; three of the five affected extensions don't use that prefix, so the exemption would have shipped as a green gate covering the wrong files. `does this path exist in upstream/main?` is a command, not a guess.
- **Open:** the general version of this problem — keeping the bigger picture available when the question doesn't mention it — is written up as J11 note 7 (standing premises: a small always-resident context region for objectives and constraints, and a `premise-contradiction` trigger for the AMYGDALA gate). Not built.
- Failure classes: `[answer-was-in-memory-but-not-in-context]`, `[locally-sound-globally-wrong]`, `[exemption-predicate-guessed-not-checked]`.

### FIXED [ui-state-clear+event-ordering+cleanup-race]: stop-lands-then-thinking-starts-again (2026-08-20)

- **Symptom:** Stop on Grok returns in ~118ms (`chat.abort` ✓), then the thinking bar comes back. Four clicks, same dance. Session status showed `Think: off`. A sibling tab (`tinker:mt16qwe0`) stayed `processing` for 6+ minutes with `queueDepth=1`.
- **Root cause — Stop aborted the controller, not the turn.** Three independent resurrections, all after a successful abort RPC: (1) `chat.abort` never drained the followup queue / embedded runner / children — `/stop` text already did; the UI button did not, so a queued followup or leftover tool-call started a fresh turn the moment the controller died. (2) A leftover delta from the aborted runId deleted `sessionEndedAt`, so the next `sessions.list` snapshot claiming live re-lit the bar; a snapshot taken AFTER Stop of the SAME dying run (`run.since < endedAt`) also lifted the 2026-08-06 veto. (3) `process.poll` ignored its abort signal and slept out the wait (up to 90s), so the runner stayed `processing` long after Stop. Compaction-on-timeout also ran after a user abort (`timedOut && !externalAbort` was not gated), which is how a Stop became a failover.
- **Fix:** `settleSessionAfterAbort` on every session-scoped `chat.abort` (drain queue, abort runner, kill children, stamp `abortedLastRun`). Client: leftover deltas of a terminated runId no longer clear the Stop stamp. Run-set: a run that began before Stop stays idle even if the snapshot is newer. `process.poll` returns on abort. Timeout-compaction skipped after user abort.
- **Files:** `src/gateway/server-methods/chat.ts`, `tinker-ui/src/app.ts`, `tinker-ui/src/run-state.ts`, `src/agents/bash-tools.process.ts`, `src/agents/embedded-agent-runner/run.ts`.
- **Rule:** Stop means the TURN is over, not the HTTP request. Abort the controller AND the queue AND the children AND the leftover stream. A snapshot of the dying run is not a new turn.
- Failure classes: `[ui-state-clear]`, `[event-ordering]`, `[cleanup-race]`.

### FIXED [ui-state-clear+event-ordering]: stop-thinking-indicator-re-lights-from-server-fallback (2026-08-05)

- **Symptom:** the architect stops the Grok thinking indicator; it keeps coming back / keeps going. Session status shows `Think: off`, model is `xai/grok-4.5`, but the chat thinking row reappears after Stop.
- **Root cause:** two independent "is this session live?" lanes, and Stop only closed one of them. (1) `abort()` deleted the viewed session's `activeRuns` rows, so the rich client-side thinking row went away. (2) `renderThinkingIndicator` has a second path: if no fresh client run remains, it asks `sessionHasActiveRuns` → `resolveSessionRunState`, which can re-light from `sessions.list` (`row.run.live` / legacy status) unless `sessionEndedAt` is newer than the snapshot. Manual Stop never stamped `sessionEndedAt` and never called `rememberTerminated(runId)`, so a still-live (or still-stale) server claim resurrected a "Grok working" indicator on the next tick / sessions refresh. `chat.final` / `chat.aborted` already stamp the end; Stop did not.
- **Fix (source, HMR-live if Vite is serving this tree):** in `abort()`, set `sessionEndedAt.set(sessionKey, Date.now())` before deleting local runs, and `rememberTerminated(runId)` for every deleted viewed run so a late delta cannot re-create a ghost under the same runId.
- **Files:** `tinker-ui/src/app.ts` (`abort`).
- **Not yet proven live against a full gateway-owned long Grok turn after rebuild; the local Stop → no server-fallback re-light path is closed in source.**
- **Rule:** every terminal user action that means "this turn is over" must leave the same end evidence as `chat.final` / `chat.aborted` — clear client runs, stamp `sessionEndedAt`, mark runIds terminated. Closing only the client map is half a stop.
- Failure classes: `[ui-state-clear]`, `[event-ordering]`.

### OPEN [plugin-load]: linked WhatsApp channel exits silently and leaves history blind (2026-07-29)

- **Repro:** `openclaw channels status --probe` reports `WhatsApp default: enabled, configured, linked, stopped, disconnected ... error:channel exited without an error`; `whatsapp-history.db` has no captured messages after 2026-07-28 23:58, so inbox checks can falsely resemble a quiet channel.
- **Restart escalation (16:16):** after a gateway restart intended to recover the listener, `openclaw status --deep` reports `WhatsApp ON / SETUP / UNLINKED — not linked`; `message action=send` fails with `No active WhatsApp Web listener`, while resumed agents may not expose the channel-specific `whatsapp_login` tool and interactive `openclaw channels login` is blocked through `exec`. A formerly linked channel can therefore degrade into a state the agent cannot repair or complete an already-authorized send from the same surface.
- **False health + crash loop (16:49–16:53):** after another restart, `openclaw status --deep` transiently reported `WhatsApp ON / OK` and Health `LINKED`, yet an immediate `message.send` still failed with `No active WhatsApp Web listener`. The authoritative probe then reported `linked, stopped, disconnected, error: channel exited without an error`. Journal evidence: provider starts at 16:52:41, `wm-session init()` returns a JID, then the channel exits without error at 16:52:46 and enters auto-restart attempt 6/10. `LINKED` is therefore credential presence, not live transport health; sends must gate on running/connected listener state.
- **Instrumentation added, not yet deployed:** `src/gateway/server-channels.ts` now logs unexpected `startAccount` resolution with lifetime plus `running`/`connected`/prior-error state while preserving the existing public `lastError`. Targeted `gateway-server` regression: 25/25 passed; `git diff --check` passed. `vet` produced no verdict before its 180 s timeout. Do not call this fixed: the next instrumented reproduction must identify why the WhatsApp adapter resolves after ~5 s.

### OPEN [bundler-trap]: targeted prefrontal Vitest rebuilds unrelated plugins for minutes (2026-07-29)

- **Repro:** `pnpm exec vitest run extensions/tinkerclaw-prefrontal/recipe-rpcs.test.ts` spends more than four minutes in repeated `inject-file-scope-variables` / `externalize-deps` plugin work without reaching test output; a single-extension recipe test needs a scoped no-whole-repo-build path.

### OPEN [session-integrity]: unsent composer drafts and pre-forensic-dump user messages unrecoverable after gateway restart (2026-07-29)

- **Repro:** User types a correction in Tinker composer (or send dies before run-dump); gateway restarts; `~/.openclaw/forensic-sessions/forensic-agent_main_tinker_*.json`, session jsonl/trajectory, and UI snapshot contain no copy of the text — rescue is impossible without user re-paste. Need durable draft buffer and/or pre-LLM client-side persist of submitted prompts.

## 7. Bug Fix Log

## Failure-class taxonomy (added 2026-05-11)

Every entry below now carries one or more `[tag+tag]` chips after `FIXED`.
Tags let an AI scan for recurring patterns ("how many `auth-token` bugs
have we seen?") without re-reading each prose entry. When adding a new
fix, pick from this list — extend it only if no tag fits.

| Tag                                       | Meaning                                                                                                                                                                  |
| ----------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| `auth-token`                              | OAuth tokens — refresh, content-type, scope-downgrade, refresh-failed                                                                                                    |
| `auth-scope`                              | Scope/permission gate dropped legitimate clients                                                                                                                         |
| `billable-noop`                           | A paid LLM turn fired that nobody consumes (skip-gate failed / was dead code)                                                                                            |
| `bridge-leak`                             | Cross-channel state bleed (real or suspected)                                                                                                                            |
| `bundler-trap`                            | tsdown/onlyBuiltDependencies/\_\_filename/native-deps wiped or misconfigured                                                                                             |
| `cache-staleness`                         | TTL not invalidated after dependent change                                                                                                                               |
| `cleanup-race`                            | Drain deadlock, orphan processes, stuck resurrection across restarts                                                                                                     |
| `config-dead-code`                        | Config key looked live but didn't actually apply                                                                                                                         |
| `mechanism-starved`                       | A complete, tested, deployed mechanism was handed an EMPTY input, so it ran faithfully and did nothing                                                                   |
| `crash-on-startup`                        | Bad SDK call / missing artifact prevented plugin or gateway boot                                                                                                         |
| `detection-pattern`                       | Substring/regex/startsWith assumption broke under prefix change                                                                                                          |
| `display-misclassify`                     | UI rendered system as user, error as raw object, etc.                                                                                                                    |
| `think-clamp`                             | A requested thinking level exceeded a model's ceiling and was REJECTED (hard error) instead of clamped down to the nearest supported level                               |
| `event-ordering`                          | text_end before tail-recover, lifecycle dropped, race on stream state                                                                                                    |
| `lid-routing`                             | WhatsApp LID rescue / sister-DM trigger class                                                                                                                            |
| `merge-wipe`                              | Upstream merge dropped fork code/config/scope (often combined with another)                                                                                              |
| `outbound-drop`                           | Outbound message lost / queued without delivery                                                                                                                          |
| `plugin-load`                             | Plugin failed to register — manifest missing field, wrong SDK call, name mismatch                                                                                        |
| `timeout-tuning`                          | Idle watchdog / request timeout fired prematurely or wrongly                                                                                                             |
| `ui-state-clear`                          | File-watcher / event handler cleared UI state too aggressively                                                                                                           |
| `workspace-shadow`                        | workspace/ override of bundled/ with stale content                                                                                                                       |
| `answer-was-in-memory-but-not-in-context` | A fact the agent already had on record was never retrieved into the turn, because the question did not resemble the memory that answers it                               |
| `dist-gap`                                | Source is right but the SHIPPED artifact is not — an asset never staged into dist/, or the loader reads a copy the build never wrote                                     |
| `double-count`                            | Two producers (or two paths) of ONE fact each count it, so one event is booked twice and every total built on it is inflated                                             |
| `early-return-drops-attribute`            | A render path returns before the line that stamps a datum, so every feature keyed on it silently mounts nowhere and reads as unbuilt                                     |
| `failure-as-value`                        | A failure arrives as an ordinary value (null, an empty or no-op-shaped record), so the error handler never runs and the caller trusts it                                 |
| `flex-weighting`                          | Flex grow/shrink weighted by each item's content basis, so its share follows text length instead of the equal split the UI promises                                      |
| `fractal-anchor-is-live-only`             | State written only by a live socket handler and never persisted, so whatever depends on it dies on every page load and every reconnect                                   |
| `gate-blindspot`                          | A check that cannot fail meaningfully — it matches a comment, sits red and ignored, or enumerates far less than it appears to                                            |
| `layout`                                  | A sizing, overflow or clipping defect: the geometry hides or crowds content the user needs to see                                                                        |
| `name-collision`                          | One name covers two unrelated surfaces, so a feature was built on the one the architect was not looking at and read as never shipped                                     |
| `observability`                           | A gauge under-reads the very quantity it exists to warn about, because its measurement scope is narrower than the volume billed                                          |
| `panels`                                  | A panel derives its numbers from a frozen literal, so it ages in one direction and ends up arguing for the more expensive choice                                         |
| `pii-boundary`                            | A private identifier (a raw session key, phone digits) is stored or served where its contract says none may ever be                                                      |
| `restart-recovery`                        | Restart/resume path lost work or forced a manual re-prompt                                                                                                               |
| `retry-storm`                             | A retry / backfill loop with no liveness precondition floods the transport or drowns the journal                                                                         |
| `schema-migration`                        | ADD-COLUMN cannot relax a constraint; migration ordering; fresh-install DDL differs from the deployed schema                                                             |
| `scope-mismatch`                          | Two sides of one path disagree about scope / allowlist / identity — the caller's assumption is not the callee's                                                          |
| `session-integrity`                       | Session state (drafts, queued sends, pre-dump user text) unrecoverable across a restart                                                                                  |
| `session-naming`                          | A session key lies about what the session IS, so grouping, retention, attribution and recovery are all corrupted at once, and silently                                   |
| `spec-drift`                              | Shipped code contradicts its own shipped spec, often landed in the very same commit                                                                                      |
| `tab-bleed`                               | Messages or state from one UI tab surfaced in another (attribution leak)                                                                                                 |
| `type-gap`                                | A type/field missing from an upstream interface broke the fork's build or a contract                                                                                     |
| `unverified-state`                        | Code RECORDS a measurement it never TOOK — a cached scalar or persisted meta gates the check that would catch it                                                         |
| `chat-divergence`                         | The live chat shows something different from the history a reload serves: lost prompts, stale copies, repeated answers                                                   |
| `gate-publishes-its-words`                | A guard that blocks private words prints those same words in its own message, log or test                                                                                |
| `pii-gate-case-sensitive`                 | A privacy filter matches only one letter case, so the same name in another case gets through                                                                             |
| `restart-drain`                           | Work held by a restart drain is never resumed after the gateway comes back                                                                                               |
| `pool-sweep-kills-working-child`          | A cleanup sweep stops a child process that is still doing work because it looks idle to the sweeper                                                                      |
| `target-normalization`                    | An outbound address is rewritten into a different, valid-looking address (a WhatsApp LID turned into a phone number)                                                     |
| `id-reuse-across-restarts`                | A counter kept in process memory restarts with the process, so ids made after a restart collide with ids from before it                                                  |
| `threshold-unit`                          | A cap counted in a unit that does not measure the risk it guards, so it trips on healthy input: 3x a tab's LOCAL rows, for tabs whose turns live in their CLI transcript |
| `mixed-coordinates`                       | An offset measured in one buffer is read as an offset into another, so a slice lands at an arbitrary point, often mid-word                                               |

**Recurring patterns visible from the chips:**

- `merge-wipe` shows up across `auth-scope`, `bundler-trap`, and standalone — the highest-leverage discipline gap.
- `ui-state-clear` — 21 entries at the 2026-09-25 recount (7 when this list was written on 2026-05-11). Clearing state on file-watch events without preserving error chips is a known anti-pattern.
- `event-ordering` — 27 entries at the 2026-09-25 recount (7 on 2026-05-11): async race conditions around stream lifecycle / button-state / session-resume, and, since 2026-09, terminals that end state they do not name and readers that let arrival order pick between two snapshots.
- `auth-token` — 4 entries, the latest dated 2026-03-21. It was the largest single class when this list was written; at the 2026-09-25 recount the largest are `event-ordering` (27), then `ui-state-clear` and `display-misclassify` (21 each). A recount reads the chips of every `###` entry header, whatever its status (FIXED, OPEN, META, DECISION, struck-through).
- **the 2026-08-04 sweep** (new chips `dist-gap`, `gate-blindspot`, `retry-storm`, `schema-migration`, `spec-drift`, `unverified-state`) — six independent production defects, each dead for weeks-to-months, all ONE shape: **a failure that reports below the threshold anyone reads, on a path nobody measures.** Four surfaced only as `log.warn` / `log.debug` on hot paths; two were invisible because a refusal rendered identically to a healthy no-op. If a subsystem can be DOWN, something must be able to say so out loud.
- **the wave-0924 sweep** (2026-09-24/25; new chips `double-count`, `failure-as-value`, `pii-boundary`) — eleven entries, and most of them are ONE shape: **two owners of one fact.** Two producers incremented one count; three functions canonicalised one session key; a 0 was an identity to one side and an ordinal to the other; a terminal was scoped to the session while its fact belonged to a prompt; a page clock ruled on what only the gateway knew; two stacked badges, and two anatomy rows per call, each decided by arrival or paint order. The cure was the same each time: name the ONE owner and route every reader through it. The other shape, four times in one wave, was the seam that shipped with its consumer and without its producer.

### FIXED [dist-gap+bundler-trap]: fractal prompts never reached dist — 2,379 ledger rows, ZERO successes, for eight weeks (2026-08-04)

- **Symptom:** Fractal Reflection had never produced a single successful result. The feature was on, the toggle worked, nothing ever came out of it.
- **Evidence:** the fractal ledger held **2,379 rows and ZERO successes** across its entire history (first row 2026-06-11, last 2026-08-04). **2,067** of them carried one headline, `triage-prompt.md missing or unreadable in the extension dir`; for August it was **245 of 245**. Those counts are recorded in-tree at `scripts/runtime-postbuild.mjs:49-57`, beside the fix, so they stay re-checkable after the ledger rolls.
- **Root cause:** all three prompt files were present in the extension dir **in the repo**, but `STATIC_EXTENSION_ASSETS` in `scripts/runtime-postbuild.mjs` declared only `fractal-prompt.md`. Undeclared assets are never copied, so `dist/extensions/tinkerclaw-fractal-reflection/` — and the `dist-runtime/` mirror the gateway actually loads — contained just that one file. `loadTriagePrompt` (`extensions/tinkerclaw-fractal-reflection/src/fractal-run.ts:300-313`) catches the read failure and returns `null`, so the run aborted one line later at `fractal-run.ts:548` with `errorRow("triage-prompt.md missing or unreadable in the extension dir")` — before any work started. **The repo copy was never the deployed copy.**
- **Fix (commit `9bf4ec00e26`):** declare `triage-prompt.md` + `fix-prompt.md`, and mirror the same entries into `scripts/merge-drivers/apply-fork-wiring.mjs` so the next upstream merge cannot drop them again (with a top-up branch for trees still carrying the old single-entry fork block, which the idempotency marker would otherwise skip). Verified by invoking the real `copyStaticExtensionAssets` against the real `dist/` destination: **1 file staged → 3 files staged**.
- **Files:** `scripts/runtime-postbuild.mjs`, `scripts/runtime-postbuild.d.mts`, `scripts/merge-drivers/apply-fork-wiring.mjs`, `extensions/tinkerclaw-fractal-reflection/__tests__/scaffold.test.ts`.
- **CLASS — a test that asserts an asset exists IN THE REPO is blind to whether it SHIPS.** `__tests__/scaffold.test.ts` asserted repo presence and stayed green for eight weeks while the runtime saw one of three files. Any asset consumed at runtime needs its gate on the STAGED path (`dist/`, `dist-runtime/`) or on the staging registry itself — never on the source path. And every staging registry needs a merge-driver mirror, or the next upstream merge silently re-opens the hole. Direct sibling of `[chrome-extension-cli-ships-the-stale-twin]` (2026-08-03): **the question is never "is the file here?", it is "is the file where the loader looks?"**

### FIXED [unverified-state]: vector index silently dead — 5,258 chunks, ZERO vectors, reported only as a warn (2026-08-04)

- **Symptom:** semantic memory search returned nothing useful. Retrieval had silently degraded to keyword-only (FTS) and no surface said so.
- **Evidence:** measured on the live 1.5 GB index (`~/.openclaw/memory/main.sqlite`) — `chunks` 5,258 rows, `chunks_fts` 5,305 rows, **`chunks_vec_rowids` 0 rows**, `chunks_vec` declared `FLOAT[3072]`, `meta.model` = `mxbai-embed-large` (ollama), which emits **1024**. The only console symptom was `memory sync failed … Expected 3072 dimensions but received 1024` at **WARN**, 198 times in three days — buried under a quarter-million WhatsApp backfill lines (the next entry; the two incidents hid each other).
- **Root cause — a two-part trap:** (1) `dropVectorTable()` swallowed its own failure into `log.debug`, invisible; (2) `ensureVectorTable()` then set `this.vector.dims = dimensions` **UNCONDITIONALLY**. `CREATE VIRTUAL TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` is a no-op when the table survives a failed drop, so the old 3072 table stayed while the manager RECORDED 1024 — and the `this.vector.dims === dimensions` early return made that lie **permanent**: every subsequent insert threw, forever, and the migration could never retry.
- **The second half, found only by deploying (`b5d18a37b46`):** that early return bails BEFORE any verification, and `this.vector.dims` is seeded from **persisted meta**. Measured after the first deploy: meta said `vectorDims=1024` while `chunks_vec` was still declared `FLOAT[3072]` — so `ensureVectorTable(1024)` returned instantly, never looked at the table, and the new verify-after-CREATE never ran because no CREATE was ever attempted. A fix that trusts the same wrong stored measurement inherits the same bug.
- **Fix (commits `bc16efe811c`, then `b5d18a37b46`):** `readVectorTableDims()` (`src/memory/manager-sync-ops.ts:238`) reads the declared dimension back out of `sqlite_master` AFTER the CREATE — only a table that is genuinely the right shape updates `this.vector.dims` (`:293-302`); a wrong one now DEGRADES HONESTLY (vector search disabled, keyword search still working, logged at ERROR with the remedy) instead of throwing on every insert and calling it a warning. `dropVectorTable()` now returns whether the table is actually gone, warns instead of debug-logging, and clears the `vec0` shadow tables (`_chunks`/`_info`/`_rowids`/`_vector_chunks00`) that survive a partial drop and make the next CREATE fail. The follow-up reconciles `this.vector.dims` against the table's real schema ONCE per manager (`:259-265`), before the fast path at `:268` can be taken.
- **Re-measured on 2026-08-04 while writing this entry:** `chunks_vec` now declares `FLOAT[1024]`, `chunks_vec_rowids` = **24,284** and climbing, `chunks` = 26,978, DB 2.76 GB. The declared dimension is the durable proof — the row count is a snapshot and will have moved by the time you read it.
- **Files:** `src/memory/manager-sync-ops.ts`.
- **CLASS — code that RECORDS a measurement it did not TAKE** (design-principle #20 — a measurement must carry its provenance and proof it ran; FOUNDATION #5 — no silent failure, no silent loss). Two sub-reflexes worth having separately: **(a)** a cached scalar or persisted meta that gates an early return must be RECONCILED against the real artifact at least once per process, or the cache makes the bug unreachable by its own fix; **(b)** a per-write failure that affects EVERY write is not a `warn` — it means the subsystem is down and must say so at ERROR. Anything logged at warn on a path that runs thousands of times a day is, operationally, not logged at all.
