Fix: rebuild (`node scripts/tsdown-build.mjs`) + gateway restart, then `scripts/repair-cron-board-bodies.mts` refilled the bodies IN PLACE from the immutable report files using the plugin's own `parseReport`/`parseBullet` (exact title-line match on either key the old bundle could produce, so it can only restore a body our parser dropped, never invent one; never creates/deletes/reorders/retires an item, never touches `status`/`pinned`/`runs`/dismissal reasons). Restored **123 bodies across 10 boards**; 116 of 221 open items now render prose title + body + correct skim tag, verified through the live `cronpanel.board.get` RPC. The remaining ~105 predated the voice adoption, had no body anywhere on disk, and auto-resolve needs 3 missed runs — they only had 2, so they sat on the card as title-only junk. Follow-up same day (`scripts/retire-prevoice-cron-loglines.mts`): resolved those 100 in place (pinned/dismissed untouched) and re-tagged 9 leftover `FOUND:`/`BROKE:` prefixes the old map had filed as `note`. Census after: **121 open, 116 with a title/body split, 5 genuine one-liners** (2026-08-20 wind-down bullets that were written without a body). Spiritual-tech is now clear. **Prevention:** `scripts/check-runtime-staleness.mjs` compares every built artifact's mtime against its newest source file; negative-controlled against this incident, it reports an **11.1h** gap on the cron-panel target, i.e. it would have fired. Wired into the wind-down cron brief so it RUNS nightly and reports STALE as a BROKE bullet — a checker nobody runs is the decoy failure recorded in `[fractal-prompt-orphaned-decoy-files]` above. **Same root cause is named as a suspected cause in `[tinker-api-arbitrary-file-read]` and `[fractal-action-claim-detector-missed-recurrence]` (both this week): one stale Aug-19 bundle was silently gating at least four separate features.** LESSON: when output quality does not improve after the PRODUCER was fixed, verify the artifact that READS it was rebuilt before blaming the producer — a stale consumer destroys good input and presents as an unimproved producer. Second, honest miss: I ran the build and only afterwards checked `git status`, which had compiled **12 uncommitted source files** from peer sessions into the live runtime; all were fixes awaiting deploy so the outcome was benign, but the blast radius has to be known while it is still a choice. (Jarvis)

## OPEN [skills/outlook-hack] 2026-08-22 — reply-draft splice buries new text inside the quoted thread

`scripts/outlook-mail-fetch.mjs --patch-draft --keep-signature` splices before the FIRST `<div id="Signature">`. On a draft produced by Graph `POST /me/messages/{id}/createReply`, that first match is the QUOTED message signature, not the new reply, so the new body is inserted below the quote header (`<div id="divRplyFwdMsg">`) and the recipient opens the old thread first. Repro 2026-08-22 on "RE: Consulta LOPD": my text landed at offset 1987, quote block at 268 — PATCH returned 200 and the tool reported success. Fix: when `divRplyFwdMsg` is present, splice before IT (or before the leading `<hr>`), and only fall back to the signature div otherwise. Second gap: the SKILL.md advertises "Create email drafts" but the script has no create/reply function — `createReply` had to be hand-rolled against Graph. Add a `--create-reply <msgId>` verb (still send-safe; createReply only ever produces a draft).

### FIXED [mechanism-starved+config-dead-code]: `agents.defaults.model.fallbacks` was `[]`, so every rate limit ended a turn that four other supplies could have served (2026-09-04)

- **Symptom, in the architect's words (2026-09-03):** _"we are experiencing too many rate limitations (among other causes) that stall our thinking."_ A 429 on the primary produced a dead turn, not a slower one.
- **Root cause:** `src/agents/model-fallback.ts` holds a complete failover machinery — `runWithModelFallback`, `resolveFallbackCandidates`, auth-profile rotation, `FallbackSummaryError`, with tests — and `~/.openclaw/openclaw.json` sets `agents.defaults.model.fallbacks` to **`[]`**. `resolveFallbackCandidates` resolved that empty list faithfully every turn, so the ladder had exactly one rung (the primary) and there was nothing to fall back TO. Nothing was broken and nothing logged: the config applied exactly as written, the code ran exactly as designed, and the feature was inert.
- **Why it survived review.** This is not `config-dead-code` — that tag is for a key that _looked_ live and did not apply. Here the key applied perfectly; its VALUE was empty. Code review sees a mechanism, tests see a mechanism, and only the runtime config says the mechanism is fed nothing. Hence the new tag: **`mechanism-starved`**.
- **Fix:** THALAMUS now emits a PLAN rather than a model (`src/shared/thalamus-plan.ts`), whose `chain` is a recovery ladder of at most one rung per OTHER supply, and that chain is threaded to `fallbacksOverride` (`model-selection.ts` → `run.thalamusChain` in `queue/types.ts`, set in `get-reply-run.ts` → `resolveModelFallbackOptions` → `resolveEffectiveModelFallbacks`). Precedence is owned by ONE function: a user pin keeps its empty ladder; an agent-level `fallbacks: []` is an explicit decision not to fall back; an empty chain is byte-for-byte today's behaviour. Against the live 2026-09-04 board the ladder went from nothing to `grok-4.6 → gpt-5.6-sol → gemini-3.8-flash`.
- **Prevention, and it is the part worth copying.** The THALAMUS panel renders an EMPTY chain as an explicit **warning line**, not as blank space — the whole class is invisible precisely because "nothing there" and "nothing wrong" look identical. `routing-rationale-thalamus-v2.test.ts` pins the warning, and bible §5.86 records the decision and `auth-routing.md`'s verify block asserts the wiring survives. Absent renders as silence; empty renders as the alarm.
- **LESSON (generalise it):** when a feature is "implemented, tested and deployed" but has never demonstrably fired, check its INPUT before its code. The J-series registry (2026-08-02) had already recorded five instances of this exact shape — `when:`/`onError:`/`invoke skill:` directives present in the runtime and declared in ZERO recipe files — and nobody connected them to a config array. Same class, sixth sighting. The cheap detector is a census of the input, not a reading of the code: _how many production inputs actually reach this branch?_ A uniform zero is the signature. (Jarvis)

### FIXED [display-misclassify+merge-wipe]: seven "Turn timing" blocks under one prompt were seven OLD turns restored to the tail — the client-row anchor was an ordinal in a list the bridge keeps rewriting (2026-09-08)

- **Symptom, in the architect's words:** _"In the clawhub tab I am theoretically spinning multiple parallel calls, and the EEG is not able to visualize it properly. In the chat I see multiple TURN TIMING, which indicates the amount of parallelism initiated, except it is a bug to see it so many times."_
- **Measured, not eyeballed:** the tab's DOM snapshot (`~/.openclaw/data/tinker-ui-snapshot.agent-main-tinker-mtr04ruw-*.html`, 07:23) held 25 `.msg-phase-group` blocks: SEVEN complete four-stage blocks (`sending · recalling memories · starting claude-opus-5 · preparing context`, spans 8.7–26 s, six distinct `_uid`s) stacked under the prompt "You can do the 11 in parallel", seven more ABOVE the first prompt, and none under prompts 3–31. The gateway log had exactly one `llm_input` for that prompt and zero new runs in the window; the sibling bridge tab showed the same shape (19 blocks). Every block was a real measurement of an earlier turn, in the wrong place.
- **Root cause:** `client-rows.ts` persisted only `turn = turnAnchorOf(list, row)` — an ORDINAL. `reinsertByTurnAnchor` flushed a row with turn N before the (N+1)-th user message of whatever list it was handed, or at the tail when no such message existed. A Claude Code bridge tab's served list is not the list the row was born in: `[cli-history] import` rewrites it on every ~20 s history reload, the import flood valve truncates it (`raw.user` wandered 81↔84 within minutes), and `<task-notification>` / auto-resume rows count as user messages. Ordinals past the served count went to the tail; ordinal 0 went to the top.
- **Why it read as parallelism:** each restored block is complete and distinct, so a reader sees N finished turns under one prompt — the exact picture N parallel calls would paint. `[display-misclassify]`: the number was true, the placement was the lie.
- **Fix:** `describeTurnAnchor` captures the prompt text (bounded prefix, gateway `[stamp]` stripped), the row's time and the ordinal; `stampTurnAnchorOn` writes `_anchorAt`/`_anchorPrompt` ON the row and the store keeps them beside it; `resolveTurnAnchor` trusts the prompt first (time breaks ties between repeated "keep going"s), the ordinal only when its prompt's time agrees (a steered prompt is persisted ~50 s after the tab sent it), the time alone when the prompt is gone, and the raw ordinal last. Because the anchor rides on the row, every caller that maps a stored row back as `{ m, turn }` — the reconnect preserve loop, the reload restore, the new history-reconcile module — resolves correctly unchanged. Legacy rows on disk get their capture time as the anchor, so the piles clear on the next reload. Specs: `msg-order.anchor.test.ts` (12) and `client-rows.anchor.test.ts` (4) — the pile shape is pinned as "the tail holds no block". Design record: `ui-persistence.md`, "The anchor is the PROMPT, not an ordinal".
- **Second finding, the EEG half (NOT fixed here — a design decision):** the parallel work was eleven `fork.subagents.spawn` calls at 07:22 from `openclaw-spawn-subagent` (ten accepted, two rejected at `max active children 11/10`). The CLI passes no `--parent` (deliberate since 2026-08-04), so the children's requester is the headless sink `agent:main:orchestrator`, and no emitter in `src/` or `extensions/` stamps `data.parentRunId` on their events. `recordSubagentOwner` can therefore never resolve an owner, and with TWO bridge tabs attached `subagentBelongsToViewedTab` refuses the loose root match exactly as §5.8L specifies — the EEG draws nothing. One open tab would have shown them. The bridge exports `TC_SESSION_KEY`, so the CLI could default `--parent` to it, but that also routes the children's announces into the tab (the 2026-08-04 complaint in reverse); the cleaner path is a gateway-emitted `spawnedBy` on the child's lifecycle start plus UI ownership from it. Either is the architect's call.
- **LESSON:** when N identical rows appear under one prompt, count the gateway's runs for that prompt before believing N; and never anchor a client row by its position in a list that another writer rewrites. (Jarvis)

### FIXED [event-ordering+session-integrity]: Auto ran an exhausted Sol, then Grok — the picker's Auto lost a race to a slow session patch, and a failover's own persisted override counted as a user pin (2026-09-08)

- **Symptom, in the architect's words:** _"I have Auto model selected, and I see a Sol thinking indicator. It is not only wrong because opus is available, but also because Sol has reached a token limit."_ Forty minutes later: _"Now Grok is triggering in Auto, this is insane. Thalamus should select opus first, right?"_ (a chat tab, `agent:main:tinker:mtjwloe0`.)
- **Timeline, from the gateway journal.** 07:40–07:53 Claude session limit; the architect typed `/model openai-codex/gpt-5.6-sol …` four times in that tab, which persisted a USER pin. 08:26 Sol hit the ChatGPT limit (`decision=surface_error … next=none`). ~11:26 he pressed Auto: `sessions.patch{model:null}` responded at 11:28:22 after **146.8 s**, two more at 11:32:51 / 11:32:56 after 81–85 s — sessions.json is **12 MB** (148 entries × an ~84 KB `skillsSnapshot` each; the 2026-09-04 de-dup has not taken) behind one lock. Meanwhile his sends (`/think xhigh keep going …`) went out with no `model` param and resolved against the still-stored pin: Sol at 10:54 and 11:34, both dead on the usage limit. 11:33:44 a live model switch chained `opus → next=xai/grok-4.6`; 11:36 grok answered and the runner persisted it as `modelOverride: grok-4.6, modelOverrideSource: "auto"`. From then on every turn read that override as EXPLICIT (`Boolean(storedOverride?.model)`), so the quota veto and THALAMUS never ran and grok stuck, with opus available and the dial at balanced (`orca-bias.json` biasIdx 3).
- **Two root causes.** (1) The 2026-08-06 fix above made the picker able to CLEAR a pin, but as a fire-and-forget patch racing the next send; correct when the store answers in milliseconds, wrong when it answers in minutes. (2) `agent-runner-execution.ts` persists a failover candidate with `modelOverrideSource:"auto"` so the next turn keeps it (sensible upstream, where nothing routes); this fork's `createModelSelectionState` then treated that override exactly like a `/model` pin — the very thing the THALAMUS comment warns about ("a written override would be indistinguishable from a user pin next turn"), written by the runner instead of the router.
- **Not the cause:** the quota veto itself. Replaying `windowExhausted` on the live `chatgpt-usage.json` (5h window `remaining_requests: 0`, `resets_at 10:49:40Z`) vetoes Sol. It was never consulted. No gateway run used Sonnet in that window (0 journal matches); the Sonnet the architect saw was inside the Claude Code bridge run (its own subagents), not a gateway choice.
- **Fix, gateway:** `/model auto` (new `auto-reply/reply/model-directive-auto.ts`). In `get-reply-directives.ts` it is never resolved as a model and never persisted; it becomes `createModelSelectionState({ resetStoredModelOverride: true })`, which clears the stored pin INSIDE the turn (same write as the allowlist reset, `resetModelOverride` stays false so no "not allowed" notice), starts from the configured default, and routes as Auto. `stored-model-override.ts` now returns `overrideSource`; an `"auto"` one is a starting point but not an explicit selection, so the quota substitution and THALAMUS run over it (a user pin and a legacy pin with no source stay explicit — `agent-scope.ts` already drew that line). `chat-command-body.ts` prepends the model directive to a message the user began with `/think` (half his turns), instead of treating it as a command that owns the turn; his typed level wins over the per-turn `thinking` param.
- **Fix, UI:** while an Auto press is unconfirmed (`autoAssertedSessions`), `chat.send` carries `model: "auto"`. A confirmed Auto sends nothing, as before; a displayed server pin the user never cleared is left alone — the reset follows the PRESS, never the absence of a client pin.
- **Verification:** `model-directive-auto.test.ts` (3), `model-selection.auto-reset.test.ts` (5), `model-selection.auto-override-not-a-pin.test.ts` (5), `chat-command-body.auto.test.ts` (4) green; the existing model-selection / quota-aware / thalamus / directive / command-body suites green; `tinker-ui` vite build green. Pre-existing reds baselined identical on a clean `develop` at the same commit: `model-selection.inherit-parent` (1), `get-reply-directives.target-session` (1), `chat.directive-tags` (2).
- **Still open, deliberately:** the 12 MB session store. `sessions.patch` at 82–147 s and `sessions.delete` at 237 s are the lock, and the `skillsSnapshot` de-dup shipped 2026-09-04 has not shrunk the file. This fix removes the dependency on that patch for Auto; it does not make the patch fast.
- **LESSON:** a fire-and-forget write that the next request depends on is a race, and "it lands in milliseconds" is a fact about today's store size, not about the design. Carry the intent on the request that needs it. And any override written by machinery — not by a hand — must not be read back as a hand's choice. (Jarvis)

### FIXED [detection-pattern+spec-drift]: the cc-bridge shortened its own system prompt to change how Anthropic billed it — removed, deliberately and on the record (2026-09-20)

- **What it did.** `extensions/tinkerclaw-tinker-bridge/src/worker.ts` sliced `systemPromptAppend` at the sentinel `"You are a personal assistant running inside OpenClaw"` and dropped everything after it, keeping only the persona. The behaviour was default-on for every bridge turn since 2026-04-24.
- **Root cause of the removal — the stated purpose was the problem, not a defect.** The code's own comment recorded it plainly: the appended harness catalog is _"exactly the fingerprint Anthropic's server-side classifier uses to route requests to the metered overage pool"_, and stripping it _"makes Jarvis bill the subscription again"_ (confirmed there by bisect across `4a6a289d5a` / `3db21784ad` / `378684e4f5`). That is removing truthful harness identity in order to change how the provider classifies and bills the request. It is evasion, not privacy, and relabeling the switch would not change its causal purpose.
- **Why now.** An adversarial legal review (2026-09-18→20) of publishing the bridge established: Anthropic Consumer Terms §3(7) bars automated/non-human access except via an API key or explicit permission; attended, human-triggered automation is still automation; and §3's preamble also binds a party against _"help[ing] another person to access or use"_ the Services in the prohibited ways. The mechanism was the single least defensible artifact in the fork.
- **Fix.** `personaOnly` is now `systemPromptBody` verbatim — nothing is subtracted. The `## Your Role` re-extraction (added 2026-06-11 to rescue a spawned subagent's task from the strip) becomes a no-op empty string, because with nothing stripped the block is already present and re-appending it would duplicate the task. `OPENCLAW_SYSPROMPT_CUTOFF`, `openClawCutoff` and `extractSubagentRoleBlock` are gone. README's "✅ Lawful use: driving your Claude subscription interactively" and "Your account is on the line, not the author's" claims were withdrawn in the same commit and replaced with an accurate, unresolved-permission notice.
- **Standing rule established.** The bridge may **ADD** its own identity (the persona block); it may **NEVER SUBTRACT** harness identity. Billing follows whatever Anthropic's classifier honestly determines from what is actually sent. A genuine privacy feature redacts the _user's_ data — PII, secrets, local paths, hostnames — never the client identity that bears on authorization, enforcement or billing.
- **Verification.** `oxlint` clean on the changed file (0 warnings, 0 errors); no remaining references to the removed identifiers.
- **Deliberately NOT done: scrubbing the history.** The prior behaviour is reachable in the public fork's history — in code, and in self-describing commit subjects (`fix(cc-bridge): deliver subagent task through the billing-safety prompt strip`, `docs(bible): §5.73 cc-bridge subscription-billing boundary`) — and is documented across several bible files. Rewriting that history would be incomplete (forks, clones, archives keep the objects; the provider's own request logs are the real record), maximally conspicuous, and would convert a defensible posture — an engineer working a grey area in the open and correcting it voluntarily — into concealment. The correction is published as loudly as the original.
- **LESSON:** when a comment has to explain how a transformation changes a _vendor's billing classification_, the comment is describing the violation, not documenting the feature. Write the rule as a direction, not a threshold — _add identity, never subtract it_ — because a threshold invites the next engineer to tune it, and a direction does not. (Jarvis)

### FIXED [merge-wipe+ui-state-clear]: Ctrl+Shift+R brought back tabs the user had closed or deleted — a close was only an ABSENCE from a list, and four layers read absence as "no information" (2026-09-21)

- **Symptom.** A hard reload re-opened closed tabs, including tabs of sessions he had deleted. Measured: `~/.openclaw/data/tinker-ui-state.json` (written 12:15) held 4 tabs whose sessions carry `deletedAt` in `sessions.json`, one deleted 2026-09-17; a deletion at 12:16:37 never reached the file.
- **Root cause.** The durable file WINS the boot hydrate, and four independent paths could leave a closed tab in it: the 250 ms debounced mirror dies with the page on reload; a page whose hydrate failed never mirrors; `mergeUiStateSnapshot` re-added "file-only" tabs by design; and the server is last-writer-wins, so any second window still holding the tab posts it back.
- **Fix** (`0cf642d38ab`). Closed-tab tombstones (`tinker.closedTabs`, tab id → closedAt) recorded by `closeTab` (so session delete is covered too), unioned in cache, hydrate, merge, and on every POST in `tinker-prod-ui.mjs` and the Vite handler; every tab list is filtered through them. `pagehide` flushes a pending mirror.
- **Verification.** 8 new cases in `ui-state.test.ts` (stale writer, hydrate from old file, file tombstone over old local list, rehydrate merge, union-not-replace, tab-main exempt, cap, pagehide flush) — 117/119 green, the 2 red are pre-existing (`sessionStorage` undefined in this vitest env, red on develop too). Server replayed on a spare port: close lands, then a stale POST of the old list — GET returns the list WITHOUT the closed tab.
- **LESSON:** in a store where the file wins, a deletion must be a positive fact that only ever grows; "absent from my list" is indistinguishable from "I never heard of it", and every stale writer will read it the second way. (Jarvis)

### OPEN [panels]: the €/Mtok↔€/task crossfade on constellation lines never animates — a higher-specificity `transition: opacity` rule erases the 1.3 s `stroke-opacity` fade (2026-09-23)

- **Symptom.** Toggling the SMARTNESS × COST chart between €/Mtok and €/task glides the circles and triangles over 1.6 s, but every constellation line, rail, API line and bridge SNAPS to the new axis instead of crossfading.
- **Root cause (read off computed style on the live page, not inferred).** `base.css` gives `.sc-line-cost, .sc-line-task` (and the est/rail/api/bridge siblings) `transition: stroke-opacity 1.3s ease`, but the hover/dim block gives `.sc-svg .sc-line-cost` … `transition: opacity 0.22s ease`. Same property, higher specificity (0,2,0 > 0,1,0): the computed transition on those lines is `opacity 0.22s` only. Measured on `.sc-line-api-cost` 2026-09-23: `transition: "opacity 0.22s"`.
- **Why it is not a two-minute fix.** `stroke-opacity` is now ALSO the hover-light channel (`bcf429af32d`: a hovered model's path goes 0.34→0.90). Restoring `stroke-opacity 1.3s` would make hover light slow and leave a 1.3 s after-glow; the per-model stagger (parked on lines as an inert `--sc-delay`) would add up to ~1.1 s more. The honest fix splits the channels: crossfade on one property (e.g. `opacity` of a per-axis wrapper group, or `visibility`+`opacity` of the hidden copy) and hover on another — so each keeps its own clock.
- **Repro:** open the chart, `getComputedStyle(document.querySelector('.sc-line-cost')).transition` → `opacity 0.22s`; toggle €/task and watch the lines jump while the dots glide. (Jarvis)

### FIXED [double-count]: MORE MODELS listed Astra a second time at its API price (366× Grok beside the 25× seat row), out of II order (2026-09-26)

- **Symptom.** The architect: astra6 in SMART MODELS at 25× Grok, and again inside MORE MODELS at the same Intelligence Index for 366× Grok, sitting below Copilot (43) although it scores 52.7.
- **Root cause.** `AA_INTELLIGENCE_INDEX` keeps a metered `openai/` twin of every `openai-codex/` seat model so the SMART × COST chart can plot the list price. MORE MODELS unions that map's keys, gated only on "provider is known", and `openai:default` sits in authOrder, so each twin counted as reachable. Census on the live config: 7 twins (astra6, sol6, sol5.6, terra5.6, gpt-5.5, luna5.6, luna6); three of them scored above the SMART cut, which is why they looked misplaced.
- **Fix** (`3e3c726a14f`). `withoutRouteTwins` in `panels/model-surface-catalog.ts` drops a baked id when a configured id carries the same model under another provider prefix; configured ids are never dropped. The chart is untouched.
- **Verification.** 7/7 in `model-surface-catalog.test.ts`. Headless render of `:18793/tinker/`: MORE MODELS 69 → 62 rows, no GPT twin left, top row terra5.6 at 42.1 under Copilot's 43; astra6 appears once, SMART, 52.7, 25× Grok.
- **LESSON:** a map that feeds a chart AND a selectable list will leak the chart's evidence rows into the list; the list needs its own "one model, one row" rule. (Jarvis)

### FIXED [mechanism-starved+display-misclassify+gate-blindspot]: [chat-usage-and-outcome] usage chips fired in 91/370 turns; 349 failures rendered as answers (2026-09-29)

- **Symptom, in the architect's words (2026-09-29):** _"Every time a plugin, skill or recipe is used, I want to see it in the tinker ui chat in a distinct color … I don't want to have to rely on the models to announce what they are using, not reliable."_ And: _"Any rate limit, refusal to answer, or any other output other than a proper answer, should be displayed properly."_
- **Evidence (census 2026-09-29, three read-only investigations over the local transcripts; plan `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-29-chat-usage-chips-and-typed-outcomes.md` in jarvis-icu):** a chip in **91 of ~370** turns that used a skill, recipe or plugin; recipe chips **0**; plugin chips **0**; **99 of 152** cc-bridge turns that used one showed nothing; bridge turns read `SKILL.md` through Bash **167** times and recipe `.md` files **120** times, and **84** turns used a skill only through its scripts; **5** prompt locations still told models to announce. **349** real failures rendered as plain answers after a reload; the CLI transcript import alone dropped the typed error on **76** rows (rate_limit 40, auth 14, billing 13, safeguards 6, model_not_found 3); **16** real answers were stored as errors.
- **Root cause — chips. The renderer worked; its producers were starved:**
  1. **The `:main` gate.** Prefrontal's recipe matcher returns early unless the session key ends `:main` (`extensions/tinkerclaw-prefrontal/index.ts`), so no `:tinker:<id>` or `:dashboard:<id>` tab ever emitted `matched` / `merged`, the §5.8N producer; only the pinned `agent:main:main` tab passes.
  2. **The recipe tag never reached the transcript.** `<active_recipe title path>` rides in `prependSystemContext`, the system prompt, which is not persisted, so the reload reconstruct (`recipeNoticeFromInjected`) had nothing to read.
  3. **Exec reads were excluded by rule.** §5.8O said "do not fire on `exec cat`", which hid every Bash read and every script run (the 167 / 120 / 84 above).
  4. **The bridge's `Skill` result has no base-directory line.** It is only `Launching skill: <name>`, so `skillNoticeFromTool` returned null for it by design, and the chip depended on the injected skill body being served.
  5. **The tree projection has no `tinker-bridge-tool` arm.** Only the flat path served the bridge's tool rows, and 68 of 70 recent transcripts are tree-format, so after a reload the page held none of a bridge turn's Bash / Read calls.
  6. **Prompt residues.** Five places still told models to announce, so a chip that did draw was duplicated in prose or buried in the folded reasoning.
- **Root cause — outcomes. The failure lived in fields nothing read:**
  1. `renderMsg` never read `stopReason` / `errorMessage` on a history row (334 turns).
  2. The UI's text-match list missed the gateway's own wording (63 turns).
  3. The backstop `final` sent after an error was taken for a success: it cancelled the §5.8j ladder and deleted the saved error bubble.
  4. The cc-bridge persisted every turn with `stopReason: "stop"`, appended the error envelope to streamed answer text, and `src/agents/cli-runner.ts` hardcoded `refusal: false`.
  5. The Claude CLI import dropped `isApiErrorMessage` / `error` (the 76 rows above) and refusal stop reasons.
  6. The inverse: `chat.ts` joined every final payload into ONE `isError` message once any payload failed, storing 16 real answers (each followed by a trailing `⚠️ 📝 Edit … failed` line) as errors.
- **Fix** (branch `feat/chat-usage-outcome`, staged for the next gateway start; decision D5, the owner restarts). ONE gateway attributor (`src/fork/usage-attribution.ts`, `ea4401f9c7d`) turns tool calls into usage marks, shipped live on tool start events and in history on the turn's user row; the UI draws one chip row from them (recipe green, skill yellow, plugin cyan) with the old producers as fallback (`tinker-ui.md` §5.8AA). ONE gateway classifier (`src/fork/turn-outcome.ts`, `c7a0d274d95`) types every non-answer at each producer: the display projection, the cc-bridge final, the CLI import, `chat.ts`'s injected messages and backstop final, the prompt-error rows. The UI draws one bubble from `outcome`, or from `stopReason` + `errorMessage` on a pre-restart row, and a final that carries an outcome is never a success (`tinker-ui.md` §5.8AB). The announce line is gone from every prompt location (`6cd6a8878a4` for the bridge helper). Chips may open `~/.claude/skills/**` and `~/.claude/plugins/cache/**` (`9ee6c90a173`).
- **Not fixed here, named:** the `:main` gate itself (a recipe the agent reads or runs now draws its chip; a recipe it is only matched to still draws none on a Tinker tab); a plugin tool registered without declared names has no chip in history; WhatsApp shows no chip (D3); the saved-bubble half of "a final with an outcome is not a success" lives in `app.ts` with no test.
- **Why it survived — the gates checked names, not behaviour.** §5.8N's and §5.8O's `verify:` asserted that `renderRecipeNotice`, `skillNoticeFromTool`, `recipeNoticeFromInjected` and their `↗` labels EXISTED in source. They did, and the gates stayed green while the producers feeding them fired in 91 of ~370 turns and never for a recipe. The new gates RUN the attributor's, the classifier's and the UI's tests, plus a wire check that the gateway imports both modules. §5.8O also carried an inline `verify:` in the body that the runner never executed (it reads frontmatter only).
- **Verification:** the two gateway modules' suites, 67/67 green under `--config test/vitest/vitest.unit-src.config.ts` (measured 2026-09-29 while writing this entry; the root config ran past 180 s with no output on the loaded box); every other unit's tests run in its own commit. Live: **UNVERIFIED** until the gateway restart and a UI rebuild.
- **LESSON:** a gate that greps a renderer proves the renderer exists, not that anything feeds it. For a chat-visible feature, count the turns that show it against the turns that should: 91 of ~370 is the number no string check can see. And a failure stored in a field the renderer does not read is, to the reader, an answer.

### OPEN [restart-drain]: a cron run held by the restart drain is never resumed — `model-rank-refresh` dropped 3 times in 45 min (2026-09-30)

- **Symptom:** GPT-6.1 Sol stayed off the picker and the public AI-analysis page. The 05:45 refresh was the first run that could add it, and it never finished. jobs-state kept `runningAtMs`, then filed `lastError: cron: job interrupted by gateway restart`; the Layer-1 report stayed at "still running". The next try is the next schedule, 24 h later.
- **Evidence (journal, 2026-09-30):** runs `7247472c` (05:45), `3fca6d30` (manual 05:58) and `f4c0efee` (manual 06:07, held 06:27:39) were each logged `restart drain: held at a model-call boundary`, then the gateway stopped (05:49:33, 06:04:17, 06:30:14; the first two for tinker-rebuild upgrades, the third for the amygdala J11 Jev guard). No resume line follows any of them. Census of `jobs-state.json`: 1 of 20 jobs affected (the others were idle).
- **Known:** boot recovery continues held CHAT turns (lifecycles L4/L4b, `continueFromTranscript`). **Not known:** why a held cron session is skipped — not yet traced in code.
- **Workaround:** after a restart, `openclaw cron run <id>` for any job whose state still carries `runningAtMs` or that errors "interrupted by gateway restart". While restarts keep coming, put the one change that matters in place by hand.
- **Proposed fix (not applied):** treat a held cron run like a held chat turn at boot, or have the restart skill re-enqueue such jobs once the new gateway is up. (Jarvis)

### FIXED [pool-sweep-kills-working-child]: the worker pool's idle sweep stopped claude children that were still working — a background Workflow after its turn, and a master turn the gateway was not tracking (2026-10-01)

- **Symptom:** at 14:29:33 a WhatsApp message made the bridge spawn a worker, and the same second two other workers got SIGTERM. One was the AcmeVision build worker's process holding a background Workflow (six agents, all ended mid-build; systemd stopped its unit and the test stack it had started). The other was the master tab's own process, killed in the middle of a command (exit 137); that master turn never delivered an answer.
- **Known (journal):** `[tinkerclaw-tinker-bridge] spawning claude … tinker-sp-f48f12de` at 14:29:33.380, then `claude exit[tinker-sp-a84fe190] signal=SIGTERM` and `claude exit[tinker-sp-d94892e0] signal=SIGTERM`. `SessionWorkerPool.sweep` (runs on every `getOrCreate`) evicts a worker when `now - lastUsedAt > idleTtlMs` (15 min) and `!isBusy()`. `lastUsedAt` is set only when a turn is handed to the worker, and `isBusy()` only sees a turn the bridge is tracking. Both children were past 15 minutes since their last tracked turn (13:57 and 13:53) and both were printing.
- **Fix (2026-10-01, `worker-pool.ts` + `worker.ts`):** the worker records when its child last printed a stdout line (`lastActivityAt()`); the sweep and the LRU cap count idleness from the later of the last turn and the last line. A child that has been silent past the TTL is still reaped. Tests in `worker-pool.test.ts` (3 cases; the first fails on the old pool) and `worker.early-empty-result.test.ts`. Running from the next gateway build (`tinker-rebuild full`), not before.

### FIXED [event-ordering+cleanup-race]: an "empty response" retry on a cc-bridge turn spawned a second worker while the first was still working — one send, two Claude processes on one session (2026-10-01)

- **Symptom:** the first send into a long-running product-build worker tab (`agent:main:tinker:muoi4s4c`) after its previous turn was killed by the 3 h run limit started TWO `claude` processes on the same CLI session (`fc1a5b67`). Both made tool calls. The prompt sat in the transcript twice, 10 s apart. In 70 s the first one created two git worktrees (`sv2/i1b`, `sv2/i1c`), and the second, seeing them, nearly stood down from the turn, believing it had a twin that was ahead.
- **Known (journal, 2026-10-01 02:44):** bridge session `tinker-sp-b45a39b0` spawned unit `tinkerclaw-worker-87662-muotatqf-qlm17o` at 02:44:42.43. First stream line after 1305 ms, then `turn result subtype=success num_turns=0 duration_ms=220 output_tokens=0 final_text_len=0`, `[tail-recover] verdict=no-result`, `agent_end success=true`. Then `[agent/embedded] empty response detected … retrying 1/1 with visible-answer continuation` re-ran the prompt on a NEW bridge session `tinker-sp-3ab03bf1` (unit `…-muotb23i-7fre9r`, 02:44:53). The first unit's `meta.json` read `turn: null` while its CLI went on working the real prompt. The wake-on-finish watcher also saw the false finish, but its "empty finish, re-check in 45 s" guard held it.
- **Assumed:** a `--resume` of a session whose last turn was SIGTERM'd makes the CLI emit an empty `result` before it processes the new input, and the bridge treats it as the end of the turn. The retry path never checks that the first worker's CLI has exited before spawning another.
- **Not known:** what the 220 ms result line actually carried (the unit's `out` file went with `systemctl stop`). Whether any resume after a killed turn does this, or only this one.
- **Workaround (used):** count `claude` processes per session key for 30 s after a send (master-worker-coding Step 2). If two are live and the transcript has two prompt copies, stop the unit whose `meta.json` says `turn: null`, then tell the survivor by SendMessage that it owns the turn.
- **Proposed fix (not applied):** (1) log the raw `result` line and whether the CLI had acknowledged the new user message when a result arrives with `num_turns=0`; (2) before the empty-response retry on a claude-code turn, stop the first worker or confirm it exited, never leave it running beside the retry. Logs first, so the next one names the cause. (Jarvis)
- **Second sighting (2026-10-01 14:01):** same worker tab, and this time no turn had been killed. The previous turn ended normally while its CLI (pid 617447) still ran a background Workflow and a Monitor. The next send's `--resume` (unit `…-muphhhdz`) printed `turn result subtype=success num_turns=0 duration_ms=189 output_tokens=0` at 14:01:45.428, 369 ms after its first stream line; `[tail-recover] verdict=no-result`; `empty response detected … retrying 1/1` at 14:01:47; a second unit (`…-muphhukt`) at 14:02:00, while the first went on working the prompt. Three processes on one CLI session, two prompt copies. The master stopped the untracked one at 14:02:47 before it changed anything. So the trigger is not the SIGTERM: it is resuming a session whose last turn left work pending, and the CLI's first `result` is not the new prompt's.
- **Fix (2026-10-01, `extensions/tinkerclaw-tinker-bridge/src/worker.ts`):** a `result` with `num_turns === 0`, no error and no text that arrives before any `assistant`, `user` or `stream_event` line of the turn is held, not taken as the turn's end. Turn activity drops it and the real `result` ends the turn; a turn that stays silent still ends with the held line after `EARLY_EMPTY_RESULT_HOLD_MS` (45 s), so a genuinely empty answer still reaches the empty-response retry; an exit while holding answers with it, as before. Each step logs `[early-empty-result] held / dropped / released`. Test `worker.early-empty-result.test.ts` (6 cases; the first fails on the old worker). The retry path (2) is unchanged: with the hold, the false finish no longer reaches it. Running from the next gateway build (`tinker-rebuild full`), not before.

### FIXED [chat-divergence]: the live chat drifted from the history a reload serves — LOST first prompts, stale copies at the bottom, retries nobody asked for, a restart that repeats an answer, raw `[x:end]` rows (2026-10-01; causes 1–6 fixed, 8 fixed 2026-10-03, 7 and 9 open)

- **Symptom (owner, 2026-10-01):** _"Sometimes it shows 'not in history' messages, repeated answers or simply misplaced prompts (among others), and then when I refresh the page it all resets and corrected."_
- **Evidence:** the owner's 10 🐛 reports in `~/.openclaw/data/bug-reports/` (09-26 to 09-30), 206 page snapshots (`~/.openclaw/data/tinker-ui-snapshot.*.html`) compared with the transcripts on disk, and the gateway journal. Read-only; four parallel investigations, the decisive lines re-checked by hand.

**Known (confirmed):**

1. **A new session's first prompt can never be proven, so it is redrawn LOST** (6 of the 10 reports). `appendPromptKeyMarkerForChatSend` (`src/gateway/server-methods/chat.ts`) skips the `openclaw.prompt-key` marker when the transcript does not exist yet or holds no assistant row (pi buffers appends until then), and nothing writes it later. The outbox only accepts a keyed proof (`keyedProofExpected`, `historyMatchesEntry` in `tinker-ui/src/outbox.ts`). Once a history merge replaces the live bubble (`messages = incoming` on a fresh page), `reinjectOutboxBubbles` draws the entry LOST beside the served row: one prompt, two bubbles, and a Resend that would re-run an answered prompt. The entry sits in localStorage with no expiry, so every load redraws it, and each stuck entry re-reads 1,000 history rows every 120 s per open page. prompt-queue.md's "an unprovable first prompt is never called LOST" holds only while the live bubble survives.
2. **A redrawn copy lands at the bottom.** `reinjectOutboxBubbles` splices the copy into `messages` at its typed time, but the page draws by `_seq` (`stampOrder` / `renderOrder` in `tinker-ui/src/msg-order.ts`), and an already-numbered page gives the newcomer the next number. All 13 order inversions in the snapshot census are such copies; reports ed93657b, 33ab89ad and abd81ba2 show the copy numbered 8 to 24 places after its served twin. A reload numbers the array from scratch, so the copy jumps to its own time, at the top of a long chat and out of view. That is why a refresh looks like a fix.
3. **The retry ladder fires on runs this page never sent.** `retryLifecycleAction` (`tinker-ui/src/retry-lifecycle.ts`) decides on session key, state and error text; `runId` appears nowhere in the module. In `mugmkh6p` on 09-26 (10:43 to 10:58 UTC), three subagent announce runs (`announce:v1:…`) failed on an openai-codex rate limit, and each failure re-sent the owner's last prompt, answered 75 minutes earlier, under a new key. Those fires died before replying, so they stay LOST (3 reports). With the model up, each fire re-runs the task, which is a real repeated answer.
4. **The seamless restart repeats an answer.** The resumed turn replays from its start under a new runId. The live bubble gets no `_watchedFrom` (`runWatchedFrom` finds no prompt for that run), so the older-page fill later inserts the turn's served rows above it. Work tab `mue2cvin`, 2026-10-01 11:11: the same answer twice (the page's last two long bubbles), one copy served.
5. **Raw `[x:end]` rows.** `KNOWN_STREAMS` in `tinker-ui/src/app.ts` lacked `call`, `item`, `command_output` and `patch`, so their end frames fell through to the unknown-stream fallback and painted raw rows. A structural census of 212 saved pages counted 76 `[item:end]`, 44 `[command_output:end]` and 7 `[call:end]` (an earlier text count, which also matched chat prose, said 83 `[call:end]`). They exist only on the live page; a reload drops them.

**Plausible (code and timing, not caught on a page):**

7. **Served identity flips when a tab's claude-cli worker respawns.** chat.history imports only the newest bridge-map file (`src/gateway/cli-session-history.ts`), so an older turn switches from its `ext:` rows to the coalesced `oc:` row and a tail read appends it again. Within 5 minutes of the new file's first text row, `dropImportCoveredLocalAssistants` can drop it from both, leaving an answer missing until a reload. 9 such switches in the last 24 h.
8. **The watched window ends 15 s after a bubble started** (`historyRowTime` prefers `_bubbleStartedAt`), so an answer that streamed longer is appended again under its live copy. 21 of 165 long text blocks in 3 days streamed past 15 s. **FIXED 2026-10-03 (`8450820d539`, `ddaa32ebbd2`):** caught on a page (the October-plan tab drew its whole answer twice, 2026-10-02 22:52); a run's window now also covers its closed bubbles until their last write (`_lastWriteAt`). Entry `[mixed-coordinates+chat-divergence+early-return-drops-attribute]` below.
9. **Steered and deferred prompts.** Late host-run output draws under a steered prompt, and a deferred prompt the gateway runs at once trails below its own answer (`pendingQueuedSends` is released only on terminal events).

**Server-side duplicates, which a reload keeps:** 22 prompts persisted twice within 10 minutes over 7 days (13 answered twice), mostly a retry saving the prompt again; the `:assistant-media` second assistant row; and the cc-bridge two-worker retry in the entry above.

**Workaround:** Dismiss a LOST bubble whose text you can see answered above it. A reload hides most of the rest.

**Fixed (2026-10-01; an ORCA run plus one companion commit, every fix test-first):**

- Cause 1, server half, `f81c15e456d`. The embedded runner writes the `openclaw.prompt-key` marker itself (`appendMissingPromptKeyMarkers`), just before the prompt and after the session is prepared, so a new session's first prompt is keyed in pi's first flush. Deviation from the plan, measured: written right after `SessionManager.open`, the marker is lost on a pre-created transcript, because `prepareSessionManagerForRun` resets the instance to its header.
- Cause 1, client half, `bdf1c2751ba`. The prompt's own successful final stamps `answeredAt` on its outbox entry (`markAnswered`). An answered entry is never redrawn, replayed, proof-read or derived LOST; it still retires only on keyed proof or Dismiss.
- Cause 2, `dc6f18b8d3c`. `placeByTime` (msg-order.ts) places a redrawn copy by render order, before the first row drawn with a later `timestamp`.
- Cause 3, `280ff61508c`. `isOwnRun` is a required dependency of `retryLifecycleAction`: a run this page never sent neither starts nor cancels the ladder, and a fire re-sends the failed run's own prompt (`retryPromptForRun`).
- Cause 4, `b646682dc8b`. The worker records the turn's runId in meta.json; the run that takes a frozen turn carries `resumesRunId` and `resumesTurnStartedAt`, and the page anchors that run to the frozen turn's prompt, so the gap-fill skips the turn's rows. The first restart after this deploy replays meta written by the old gateway (no runId) and relies on the carried start time; the second restart is the real check.
- Cause 5, `f1c5aefdcc2`. `KNOWN_STREAMS` gains `call`, `item`, `command_output` and `patch`.
- Cause 6, `ed608a01501` and `4d7a8792a8f`. The drain gets a `preparing` participant: it waits within its budget for acked prompts to leave preparation and logs `restart drain (preparing): UNFINISHED key=…` for any still preparing. On its own that only made the loss loud: the 09-30 prompt waited behind a memory flush that the drain held at the flush's first model call. A flush no longer starts while a drain is active.

**Still open:** causes 7 and 9 (re-measure now that the confirmed causes are gone; 8 was fixed 2026-10-03, entry below). A prompt queued behind a cold first turn (followup-runner.ts calls runEmbeddedPiAgent without `promptKeys`) and the claude-cli `runCliAgent` path still write no marker. A flush already running when a drain starts still blocks the prompt behind it. `WorkerTurnMeta` in worker-transport.ts does not declare the new `runId` field. Outbox entries already on the owner's page stay LOST until dismissed. (Jarvis)

### OPEN [scope-mismatch+timeout-tuning]: no ORCA unit got a cross-provider panel or critic on 2026-10-01 — two spawn defects (2026-10-01)

- **Symptom:** in the 7-unit `[chat-divergence]` ORCA run (quality `ultra`), every panel and every critic from another provider failed, so each landed patch is its own builder's draft, checked by tests only.
- **Known:** (1) `fork.subagents.spawn` rejects read-only spawns with "invalid agent params: unexpected property 'allowTools' / 'maxToolCalls'", so `openclaw-spawn-subagent.mjs --allow-tools read` cannot spawn. (2) `scripts/openclaw-spawn-subagent.mjs` stops waiting after a hard-coded 30 s watchdog (~L204) that ignores the `--timeout` it parses (~L68); it prints "timeout after 30s" while the gateway logs the spawn as ok, so the children run on unwatched (three were left stalled with no progress signal). (3) The conductor routed TypeScript units as domain `math`.
- **Not known:** whether the schema rejection comes from the gateway's spawn handler or from the parameters the CLI builds.
- **Proposed fix (not applied):** accept `allowTools` and `maxToolCalls` in the spawn schema, or stop sending them; honour `--timeout` in the watchdog; classify a unit by the files it writes before its prose. (Jarvis)

### FIXED [outbound-drop]: a turn the claude CLI starts on its own was dropped — a background task or Monitor that fired while the worker was idle never reached the chat (2026-10-01; bug id `monitor-notify-idle-session-lost`, open since 2026-07-20)

- **Symptom:** a worker that launched a background job could not end its turn and be woken when the job finished. The worker of a long-running product build (master/worker tabs) therefore waited in-turn: 167 sleep-and-check loops over its first 20 hours, 768 minutes, each call carrying about 500k tokens of context, about 30 % of the 1.8 billion cached tokens used in 24 hours (the master's review, 2026-10-01). On 2026-07-20 a Monitor's event reached nobody until the architect prompted four hours later.
- **Known (live repro, 2026-10-01 20:05, `/tmp/bgwake-repro`):** a `claude -p --input-format stream-json --output-format stream-json` process (CLI 2.1.286, the bridge's flags) ran `sleep 6` with `run_in_background`, answered "started" and printed its `result` at 5.3 s. At 9.9 s, with no input, it printed `task_notification` (status completed), a fresh `init`, the answer "WOKE" and a second `result`. A Monitor event did the same at 15.2 s, with no `task_notification` before the `init`. In the bridge, `worker.ts` `onStdoutChunk` emitted those lines as `stream_line` events, but `stream.ts` attaches its listener only for the length of a turn (`worker.on` before `send`, `worker.off` after), and a `result` with no `currentTurn` was skipped.
- **Root cause:** the bridge modelled the CLI as answer-only: output was read only inside a turn the bridge had started. A turn the CLI starts by itself had no listener and no run, so it was dropped whole. Two side effects: the pool's idle sweep took a worker whose CLI still ran a Workflow or Monitor for idle (`isBusy()` saw only the bridge's own turns), and a prompt that arrived while the CLI was in its own turn was answered by that turn's `result`.
- **Fix (2026-10-01, `61af1b2fb70`; `worker.ts`, new `unprompted-turn.ts`, `stream.ts`, `worker-pool.ts`):** an `init` with no turn of ours open and nothing queued opens a kept turn; its lines are buffered to its `result`. The worker asks the owning chat for a run with `chat.send` (the lane longjob and wake-on-finish use), pinned to the worker's model, with `[bg-turn <id>]` in the message. The run that sees the marker takes the kept turn (`takeUnpromptedTurn`: replay, then live) and writes nothing to the CLI; a run that finds it gone answers one fixed line, never an empty one (an empty answer would start the runner's retry, which writes to the CLI). A prompt that arrives mid-way joins the CLI's turn. `steer()` refuses a wake whose turn is still kept and swallows one whose turn was joined. `isBusy()` also counts live background tasks (`background_tasks_changed`) and a kept turn (30 min once ended). Design: `lifecycles.md` §L2. Every step logs `[unprompted-turn]`.
- **Tests:** `worker.unprompted-turn.test.ts` (14 cases: the first 12 ran against the old worker and its 9 worker cases failed, the other 3 test the new message helpers; the 2 `steer` cases came with the fix), `stream.unprompted-wake.test.ts` (3 cases; 2 fail on the old `stream.ts`, which sent the wake text to the CLI). All 31 tinker-bridge test files green (261 tests).
- **Not covered:** a kept turn lives in the gateway's memory, so a gateway restart between the CLI's turn and its run loses that turn's replay (the CLI's own transcript still has it). A turn the CLI starts with no `init` first is not kept; it is logged once. The wake is a user-lane row in the chat (`⟦AGENT:⏱ Background⟧ … [bg-turn <id>]`).
- **Running:** merged into develop; live from the next gateway build (`tinker-rebuild full`), not before.

### FIXED [cleanup-race+observability]: a fan-out died with the worker that held it, and nobody asked whether it was alive (2026-10-01; bug id `fanout-dies-with-its-worker`)

- **Symptom:** at 14:29 on 2026-10-01 the pool's idle sweep stopped a long-running product build's worker process and the six-agent Workflow it held in the background. The master noticed 43 minutes later: its waiter checked only whether report files existed. The build lost about 11 % of its wall time to incidents of this family (that sweep, the 3-hour run limit, a reboot, three duplicate-worker starts).
- **Known:** Claude Code's `Workflow` tool runs inside the worker's CLI process; the sweep part is `[pool-sweep-kills-working-child]` above. The platform's own runner, `openclaw-orchestrate.mjs`, blocked on one WebSocket call (`prefrontal.recipe.orchestrate`), so it also lived and died with whatever process started it, and it had no handler for a closed connection: a gateway restart left it waiting until `--timeout`. On this machine that RPC answers `prefrontal.recipe.orchestrate is disabled` (`plugins.entries.tinkerclaw-prefrontal.config.orchestration.allowScripts` unset), seen in the smoke test below.
- **Root cause:** nothing hosted a fan-out apart from the process that launched it, and nothing exposed whether it was alive; result files were the only signal, and a dead fan-out writes none.
- **Fix (2026-10-01, `a95d74aec85`; `scripts/openclaw-orchestrate.mjs`, new `scripts/lib/fanout-unit.mjs`):** `--detach` runs the plan as systemd user unit `tinkerclaw-fanout-<id>` (like the longjob skill), with `job.json`, `plan.js`, `run.log`, `result.json` and `done.json` under `~/.openclaw/fanout/<id>/`, and wakes the owning chat with `chat.send` when it ends (retried 8 times, 15 s apart). `--status <id>` answers `running` / `done` / `failed` / `dead` / `unknown`, exit 0 / 0 / 1 / 3 / 2; `dead` = unit gone and no `done.json`. A closed gateway connection now ends the run at once as a failure. The CLI `Workflow` half is covered by L2's busy rule (fix above): a worker whose CLI lists live background tasks is never reaped. Design: `subagents-and-recipes.md` § A fan-out that outlives its worker.
- **Tests:** `test/scripts/fanout-unit.test.ts` (9 cases; the CLI `--status` case fails on the old script, exit 2 instead of 3). Live smoke 2026-10-01 20:30: `--detach` of a plan with no agents started the unit, wrote `done.json` (failed: the RPC is disabled here), `--status` answered `failed` with exit 1, and the wake landed in the launching chat on the first attempt.
- **Not covered:** the plan itself runs inside the gateway, so a gateway restart still ends it (now reported at once). A per-turn lock against a duplicated resume (the fourth item of the build review) was not built: the two known causes of duplicate workers are fixed (`[event-ordering+cleanup-race]` above, and a prompt now joins a CLI turn already running), and a refusal in the turn path could start the runner's model fallback.
- **Running:** merged into develop; the CLI change works as soon as it is on disk, the bridge half from the next gateway build.

### FIXED [merge-wipe+cleanup-race]: `sessions.reset` refused with "still active" after any finished turn, until a gateway restart (2026-10-02; bug id `reset-refused-after-a-turn`)

- **Symptom:** a long-running build resets its worker tab's session before every turn. On 2026-10-01 23:52 and 2026-10-02 02:40–02:43 the reset failed 9 times in a row, each after 15 s: `UNAVAILABLE Session agent:main:tinker:… is still active; try again in a moment.` `sessions.list` showed the session `done`, `run.live: false`; the worker's turn had ended normally at 02:31:30 (`agent_end success=true`). Only a gateway restart cleared it.
- **Known (journal + code):** `endSessionTurns` (`src/gateway/session-reset-service.ts`) waits on the reply-run registry for every key form, and only then on the embedded run. The embedded wait logs `[diagnostic] wait timeout: sessionId=…` when it gives up, and diagnostic warnings do reach the journal; that line never appeared in either window (nor since 2026-09-25). So the reply-run registry held: the wait gave up there and returned before the embedded wait ran. Of the 12 `sessions.reset` answers since 2026-09-25, 9 were this refusal and 3 succeeded, two of them minutes after a restart with no turn in between.
- **Root cause:** `runReplyAgent` (`src/auto-reply/reply/agent-runner.ts`) creates the turn's reply operation and never ended it on success: only error paths in `agent-runner-execution.ts` call `fail()`. The entry stayed registered with phase `running` after the turn. The reset's abort clears only a `queued` operation, so `waitForIdle` waited out its 15 s on a turn that had already finished. The next prompt hid the leak, because `createReplyOperation` force-clears a stale entry (FORK 2026-04-20, "an upstream edge case we haven't pinpointed yet"). Upstream added `replyOperation.complete()` to the runner on 2026-04-05 (`71a389129ba`, an ancestor of develop), but develop's own history of the file never carried it: a merge from upstream dropped the line (which merge is not pinned down). The followup runner always completed its own.
- **Fix (2026-10-02, `src/auto-reply/reply/agent-runner.ts`, `src/auto-reply/reply/reply-run-registry.ts`, `src/gateway/session-reset-service.ts`):** the runner ends the operation it created in its `finally`, on every path, as `followup-runner.ts` does (`complete()` keeps a result already set). A finishing operation clears registry state only while it still owns the key, so one that a newer operation replaced can no longer delete the newer entry or wake its waiters. When `endSessionTurns` gives up it now logs `[gateway/session-reset] session-reset: not ended — reply operation still active … for <key> (phase=<before>→<after>, age=…)`, or the embedded run's `sessionId`.
- **Tests:** `agent-runner.misc.runreplyagent.test.ts` "runReplyAgent ends its reply operation" (4 cases: answered, reset right after an answered turn, empty answer, failed turn; the 3 registry cases fail before the fix), `reply-run-registry.test.ts` "lets an operation that was replaced end without clearing the one that replaced it" (fails on develop's registry: the new entry is deleted), `session-reset-service.give-up-log.test.ts` (2 cases).
- **Running:** live since 2026-10-02 05:28 (gateway build `1f9d34688de`). Verified 07:50: the AcmeVision build's worker turn ended at 07:45, its idle worker unit was stopped, and `sessions.reset` succeeded at the first try with no restart since 05:28 (new sessionId `104c24ce`). The same sequence was refused 9 times out of 9 before the fix.

### FIXED [tab-bleed+ui-state-clear]: a tab opened in the middle of its conversation, reloads forgot where every tab was, and the turn-end fold hid what had been opened (2026-10-02; bug id `chat-opens-mid-conversation`)

- **Symptom:** _"When I switch tabs, particularly when I go back to the AcmeVision tab, the first thing I see is in the middle of the conversation, and I have to scroll down."_ Also asked in the same message: keep the stickiness (follow while the last line is seen, stay put otherwise), never collapse an opened fold when new rows arrive, and keep the place each tab was left across a tab switch, a gateway restart and a computer restart.
- **Root causes (six, measured in code and in a real browser):** (1) the follow latch `chatFollow` and the pane's `scrollTop` belonged to the one `#messages` pane, not to the tab, so the first paint of a switched-to tab used the previous tab's latch and pixel offset; (2) markdown images have no dimensions, grow on load after the pin, and nothing re-pinned for them (the AcmeVision tab carries 11 frames, 8 images, 52 inline cards); (3) nothing persisted a tab's place, so every reload — every UI rebuild — landed at the bottom; (4) a scroll event equal to the last programmatic write was dropped as its echo however old, so scrolling up and back down to an unchanged bottom never re-armed follow; (5) a reading paint restored the raw pixel offset, so a row re-parsed above the viewport at a new height slid the text; (6) a finished run folds its rows into a "▸ Reasoning" group created closed, hiding a row opened during the turn, and five smaller folds (prompt toggles, system-notice detail, outcome and envelope details, the amygdala's sent-back chip) had no persisted open state at all, while a turn-timing row re-opened by hand was still closed by auto-disclosure at turn end.
- **Fix:** design and rules in `tinker-ui.md` §5.20 rework (V1–V11, with the inter-dependency map). Per-tab `TabState.viewport` (FOLLOWING or READING at an anchor), saved on leave, restored on enter, persisted as the ui-state choice `chat:scroll:<tabId>` (ui-persistence.md); pure half in `tinker-ui/src/chat-viewport.ts`; four new latch events in `scroll-follow.ts`; units named for the anchor in `chat-render.ts`; the Reasoning group asked once whether it forms open (`chat-units.ts openGroupOnForm`); keyed folds everywhere else; the idle background stub skips a tab left reading.
- **Found while proving it:** the e2e reload check brought back the right row 112 px off: a loading image without dimensions lays out its alt text (about 14 px each, 8 images above the row), and the first growth rule assumed zero. After measuring the height at paint it came back 4 rows off: the remembered row's own image had not loaded, the row sat wholly above the pane, and the growth rule anchored on the row below it. Hence the 4 s hold on a just-restored row (V7b) and the row-based growth rule (V7).
- **Tests:** unit — `chat-viewport.test.ts` (28, incl. app.ts wiring read off the source), `scroll-follow.test.ts`, `chat-render.test.ts`, `chat-units.test.ts`, `amygdala-ui.test.ts`, `outcome-bubble.test.ts`. Real browser — `tinker-ui/e2e/chat-viewport/` (mock gateway, two tabs, slow uncached images): 8/8 on the fix, twice; the pre-fix build failed 5 of 8, its switch opening the second tab 8,232 px above its bottom.
- **Not covered:** open folds are not persisted across a reload (keys are per page load); a persisted anchor resolves to the nearest transcript row, so a reload of a tab whose recent turns were all written live can drift; the restore search stops after 5 older pages or 30 s; the fractal dock, the inline file viewer and the compaction banner's open state are still dropped by a re-parse of their row.

### OPEN [target-normalization]: an outbound WhatsApp target `<digits>@lid` is rewritten to the phone number `+<digits>` (2026-10-02; bug id `lid-target-becomes-fake-phone`)

- **Symptom:** `openclaw message send --channel whatsapp --target 96447819206751@lid --dry-run --json` printed `"to": "+96447819206751"`. A LID is an opaque WhatsApp id, not a number: that `to` is an Iraqi phone number nobody meant. The real send was refused only because the fake number is not on `channels.whatsapp.allowFrom`.
- **Known (code):** `normalizeWhatsAppTarget` → `extractUserJidPhone` in `extensions/tinkerclaw-whatsapp/src/normalize-target.ts` treats `WHATSAPP_LID_RE` like a phone JID and returns its digits through `normalizeE164`. `normalizeWhatsAppMessagingTarget` (the plugin's `messaging.normalizeTarget`, `channel.ts:150`) uses it, and so do `src/infra/outbound/target-normalization.ts:37` and `outbound-policy.ts:70`.
- **Why it can bite past the allowlist:** `allowFrom` itself holds the architect's LID `66752499155100@lid`; if both sides are normalised the same way, a send to that LID becomes `+66752499155100` and passes the check.
- **Don't know (material to the fix):** whether replies into an inbound LID chat (the architect's self-chat is a LID) go through this normaliser or through the send API's own LID mapping. A blind "refuse LIDs" patch could silence Jarvis on WhatsApp, so no patch was made.
- **Next:** add a test in `resolve-target.test.ts`: `normalizeWhatsAppMessagingTarget("96447819206751@lid")` must not return `"+96447819206751"`. Log which path the self-chat reply takes (`source` param), then resolve a LID through the real LID→phone mapping (`jidToE164` with the auth store) or refuse it, outbound only.
- **Workaround:** send to the contact's phone number (`+1408…` for Sasha), never to a `@lid`.

### FIXED [detection-pattern]: the amygdala named the wrong files for 85 % of shell steps, because it never followed `cd` (2026-10-02; bug id `amygdala-targets-ignore-cd`)

- **Symptom:** a day of shadow data showed 102 `excess-scope` asks and 133 `weakens-own-check` holds, nearly all on the SV2 build worker. Samples named files under `~/.openclaw/jarvis-workspace/` for commands that ran in `/tmp/sv2-*`, plus "targets" like `SV2_REDIS_URL=redis://…` and lines of Python.
- **Root cause (code):** `resolveTargets` in `extensions/tinkerclaw-amygdala/src/effect-class.ts` resolved every relative path against the session's starting folder. Agent commands start with `cd <repo> &&`, so the path after it pointed at the wrong place. `export NAME=value` kept the setting as an argument with a `/` in it, and heredoc bodies (`python3 - <<'EOF'`) were scanned as more commands.
- **Measured:** replaying the old and new parser on the same 10,963 pre-tool shell steps of 2026-10-01/02: 9,283 changed, including 90/102 excess-scope asks, 133/133 weakens-own-check holds and 93/112 level-1 danger notes.
- **Fix:** `4814aeca4c3`: track the folder each `cd` moves to (an unknown `cd "$(…)"` keeps the old one), drop `NAME=value` words, strip heredoc bodies before scanning; `cd` itself is no longer a target. The command text Jev reads is unchanged. Tests in `__tests__/effect-class.test.ts`.
- **Still to measure:** the interventions per day after a shadow day on the fixed build; the verdicts already stored keep the old targets.

### FIXED [outbound-drop+display-misclassify]: about 2 % of amygdala hook calls were refused as too large, and one refusal painted the panel "the chats reach nothing" (2026-10-02; bug id `amygdala-hook-413`)

- **Symptom:** after the 12:42 restart the amygdala status read "Hooks refused by the gateway (HTTP 413) · the chats reach nothing" while checks kept arriving.
- **Measured:** `hook-spool.jsonl` held 951 `refused` rows with status 413 (about 10 an hour since at least 2026-10-02 01:00) out of 51,939, mostly post-tool after a large `Read`.
- **Root cause (code):** `hooks/lib.mjs` posted the whole Claude Code payload; a big `tool_response` passed the gateway's 256 KB `MAX_BODY_BYTES` (`src/http.ts`), so the step was dropped. `buildStatus` (`src/status.ts`) treated any refusal in the last 5 minutes as total, even with checks arriving after it.
- **Fix:** `9cbbeed60df`: `fitPayload` cuts tool_response strings to 16 KB (tool_input to 64 KB only if still over budget; the local floor reads the full payload); a refusal followed by arriving checks shows as "some hook calls refused (HTTP n)" and is not red. Tests in `hooks.test.ts` and `status.test.ts`.

### FIXED [event-ordering+ui-state-clear]: a tab whose session never goes quiet showed no history after a reload — the AcmeVision parallel-worker tab (2026-10-02; bug id `busy-tab-history-never-loads`)

- **Symptom:** _"When switching from Acmevision to its slave, parallel worker, the chat history does not load at all."_ The worker tab's DOM snapshot (12:42) held only what had streamed in since the page reloaded at 12:36: two tool rows, the 🔄 restart notice and a turn-timing block. The gateway served its history fine: 34 rows (claude-cli imports), though one read took 24 s on a loaded box (load average 35).
- **Root cause:** two gates that each read "the page has content" as "the page has history". (1) `loadChat` defers its merge while the session has a live writer, because a page with no server rows is written FRESH, replacing the bubble the run is writing into. The worker runs turn after turn, so the deferral never ended. (2) The background prefetch `hydrateTab` skipped any tab with `messages.length > 0`; a busy tab's live bubbles arrive before the serial prefetch reaches it. Neither was new: the morning's UI pushes and the 12:38 gateway restart reloaded the page while the worker was busy, which is what exposed it.
- **Fix (`tinker-ui/src/history-paging.ts`, `tinker-ui/src/app.ts`):** `planHistoryAroundLooseRows` writes the reply AROUND the rows a server-row-less page holds, by time, with the older page's placement (`slotAroundLooseRows`), skipping the live run's rows as watched; nothing on the page moves. `loadChat` uses it under a live writer when the page holds no server rows (the full merge is still owed); `hydrateTab` skips only a page that holds server rows; `mergeHistoryIntoSavedPage` writes around a saved page of live rows instead of replacing it. Rule in `tinker-ui.md` §5.8W.
- **Tests:** `history-paging.test.ts` (placement above live bubbles, around a client note, a page with server rows left to the ordinary merge, unrecognisable rows not written; app.ts wiring of the three call sites read off the source). Real browser: `tinker-ui/e2e/chat-viewport/` check 6 (a viewed tab kept busy by streamed `agent` frames, then reloaded): develop's build showed 0 rows, the fix shows the history.

### FIXED [ui-state-clear]: the CALL TIMELINE went blank after a reload, while a new prompt was prepared, and after a turn with no call (2026-10-02; bug id `call-timeline-empty`)

- **Symptom:** _"The CALL TIMELINE panel sometimes stays empty, it should always show the last run instead."_
- **Root causes (code, reproduced in a real browser):** the panel drew only the NEWEST prompt's live-recorded calls (`CallTimelineStore.promptView`, `timedCalls` in `call-timeline-canvas.ts`), and three situations left that set empty. (1) The store lives in the page: after any reload (every rebuild pushes one) it held only the anatomy history rows, one per turn, which are never drawn because their per-call timing was never recorded, so the stage said "Prompt N is from history…" until the next prompt. (2) A new prompt's preparation tick restarted the view before any call existed ("Preparing prompt N…"), for good if that preparation never became a run. (3) A turn that ended without a model call left the view on an empty window.
- **Fix (`e55bb2aff54`):** `promptView` holds the last run with a live record while the newest prompt has none, and names the newer prompt in `PromptView.newer` (title: `prompt N preparing…` / `: no model call` / `: no per-call record`). `lastRunSnapshot` / `restoreRun` / `thinSamples` in `call-timeline.ts` and `call-timeline-persist.ts` keep each session's newest finished run in localStorage `tinker.callTimeline.lastRun`, saved 1.5 s after the session goes quiet and on `pagehide`, restored when `callTimelineStoreFor` creates the store. Rule and rejected alternatives in `context-window-panel.md` §5.3 ("The last run stays on screen").
- **Tests:** `call-timeline.test.ts` (10 new, all red against develop's store: hold while preparing, past a no-call turn, never a later history row; snapshot round trip draws the same rate columns; no snapshot of a running run; history loaded after a restore adds no second turn; a live run is never overwritten; malformed snapshots refused; thinning keeps every token and kind change), `call-timeline-persist.test.ts` (5). Real browser: `tinker-ui/e2e/call-timeline/` (mock gateway streams a two-call run, then a prompt with no call, then reloads): 6/6 on the fix, with the same 7,148 lane pixels painted before and after the reload; develop's build fails checks 2, 3 and 4 with 0 pixels painted.
- **Not covered:** a browser restart. The owner's Chrome profile wipes localStorage on a clean exit, so after a cold start the history note shows until each tab's next prompt; closing that needs a gateway-side per-call record (send, first token, end, prompt parts, an output profile), which does not exist: the `call` stream is not persisted, the LLM ledger has no first-token time and one row per turn on the cc-bridge lane, and the anatomy table holds one row per turn.

### FIXED [display-misclassify]: the MCP `message` tool reported "tool execution failed" for an attachment WhatsApp refuses by name (2026-10-02; bug id `tools-invoke-hides-media-refusal`)

- **Symptom:** sending an HTML page to a WhatsApp group through the Claude Code MCP `message` tool. A direct POST to `/tools/invoke` came back `500 {"message":"tool execution failed"}` after 10 s, while `openclaw message send` with the same file said at once: `LocalMediaAccessError: Host-local media sends only allow buffer-verified images, audio, video, PDF, and Office documents (got text/html)`.
- **Root cause (code):** `resolveToolInputErrorStatus` in `src/gateway/tools-invoke-http.ts` passed through only `ToolInputError` / `ToolAuthorizationError`; every other error became the opaque 500. The refusal reaches the handler as a `GatewayClientRequestError` whose message starts with `LocalMediaAccessError:`.
- **Fix:** `26e339a1860`: a `LocalMediaAccessError`, bare or wrapped that way, maps to 400 with its text. Real crashes stay an opaque 500. Test: `tools-invoke-http.test.ts` "returns a refused media attachment as a 400 with its reason" (red on develop: 500; green with the fix; file 26/26).
- **Not fixed, not reproduced:** the first MCP attempt at 15:32 hung until undici's headers timeout and never reached this handler (no `[tools-invoke]` log line); the gateway had sessions flagged stuck at 15:35. The same call at 15:43 answered in 10 s.

### FIXED [scope-mismatch]: a session reset before every turn showed only its current turn after a reload — the worker tab's earlier turns were in archives nothing served (2026-10-02; bug id `reset-archives-never-served`)

- **Symptom:** _"The parallel worker's chat history seems to not be loading."_ (17:00, after the `[event-ordering+ui-state-clear]` fix above.) The page showed everything `chat.history` served: 68 rows, all from 12:13:53, one prompt.
- **Measured:** the worker session `agent:main:tinker:muoi4s4c` keeps one transcript path (`86e0b6e0-….jsonl`) and was reset 6 times that day; each reset left `86e0b6e0-….jsonl.reset.<ISO time>`. Five of the seven archives' header session ids resolve through the tinker-bridge map (11,052 entries, every binding kept) to an existing claude-cli transcript; two (UUIDv7-style ids) resolve to none and serve their local rows only.
- **Root cause:** the scope mismatch is between what a reset preserves and what `chat.history` reads. The reset keeps the turns, renamed beside the live file; `chat.history` reads the live file only. The page kept earlier turns on screen only while it never reloaded. The worker was never wrong about its history: nothing served it.
- **Fix:** `tinker-ui.md` §5.8W "a session that is reset: its archives page in above". `chat.history {resetArchiveBefore: T}` (`src/gateway/chat-history-archive.ts`, `server-methods/chat.ts respondWithResetArchive`, schema `ChatHistoryArchive`, Swift models regenerated); the page pages archives in above the live transcript under `Session reset` dividers (`history-paging.ts`, `app.ts loadResetArchivePage`, divider a run boundary in `chat-units.ts`).
- **Found verifying it live (right after the 17:25:55 deploy):** the newest archive served 2 rows and no turns. Its header id (`01a0fc8b…`, UUIDv7-style) is not the store sessionId the bridge map knows. Fix: the archive's first tool-call ids are matched against the claude-cli transcripts last written during its span; on the seven real archives that names exactly one transcript each.
- **Found walking every archive live after that deploy:** each archive now found its CLI transcript, but served 7–15 rows. The import flood valve (`IMPORT_FLOOD_MAX_RATIO = 3`, `cli-session-history.ts`) cut them to 3× the local rows, and an archive holds only its turn's prompt and answer locally (journal: "2 local messages, truncating 215 imported"). The live worker never trips it during a turn because its local store is empty then (`local.total=0`). Fix: archive reads pass `floodValveLocalCount: 0`.
- **Third report (18:0x, the owner: _"the chat history is also gone … I want to be able to scroll up until the beginning, since I created that tab"_):** the worker had been reset again at 17:46, so its live transcript was EMPTY, and the page's archive paging required a server row on the page: nothing loaded (e2e check 8 reproduces it on develop: 0 dividers, 0 rows). Also measured: the first archive (09-30 21:40 → 10-01 21:06) spans three CLI sessions and 1,779 rows, but the merge imported at most two ids and the page took at most 1,000 rows; a first archive read took 10–60 s against a 60 s page timeout. Fix: every mapped CLI session imported, archives paged within (`archiveOffset`/`rowsBefore`), no server-row gate, 180 s timeout + loading marker, cached archive rows, head-only tool-call matching (`tinker-ui.md` §5.8W).
- **Walked live after that deploy (18:35:31 build):** 4,049 rows back to 09-30 21:40:50, the first archive in 3 pages, but the three archives found by tool-call matching came back empty: their CLI transcripts' first tool call sits 372–386 KB in, past the 256 KB head the match read. The match now reads each candidate in 1 MB chunks until its first hit.
- **Tests:** `src/gateway/chat-history-archive.test.ts`, `src/gateway/server-methods/chat.reset-archive.test.ts` (real handler, real store, the import merge through the bridge map, every mapped CLI session, tool-call matching, no flood valve, paging a long archive from its end), `tinker-ui/src/history-paging.test.ts`, `tinker-ui/src/chat-units.test.ts`. Real browser: `tinker-ui/e2e/chat-viewport/` check 7 (develop's build fails it).

### FIXED [observability+layout]: most of every call's prompt was drawn "unitemised" on the claude-code lane, and the call timeline was full of empty stretches and slivers (2026-10-02; bug id `call-timeline-unitemised`)

- **Symptom:** _"I see some sent information is unitemised, why is that? Can you fix it and assign it a bucket? Also, the graph has horizontal gaps where nothing is sent nor received, they should be cut out. Finally, ... some lines are very thin horizonally and vertically, so let's not be so rigorous with the time factor, and present a simplified graph where more area is painted."_
- **Root cause, unitemised (measured):** on cc-bridge the call timeline's only breakdown of a prompt was the gateway's anatomy row, which describes the gateway's MIRROR transcript, not what the claude CLI sends (F6's root). A Tinker chat's row itemised 126,558 tokens with tool results 0; the same turn's calls billed 200k–540k each. The rest was hatched. The CLI's own transcript itemises almost all of it (system prompt snapshot, instructions, skill listing, hook context, tool lines, messages, tool calls, tool results). At a session's first call the rest is 31.1k–36.4k on the Opus and Sonnet 5.x models, 9.7k–10.6k on Haiku 4.5 and Sonnet 4.6 (60 newest worker sessions): the CLI's built-in tool definitions, never written to the transcript. Later growth of the remainder is ceil(chars/3.5) undercounting code and JSON (billed growth ÷ estimated growth 1.6–2.0).
- **Root cause, gaps and slivers (code):** the 2026-10-01 rate chart drew tokens per second over time: tool runs and waits were empty stretches (folded only past 2 s), a quick call was a column a pixel wide, and a prefill spike set the top lane's scale.
- **Fix:** `dd62f8dd62f` the call contract's `usage` frame may carry `composition` (eight top-lane buckets, summing to the billed prompt); `80ee64e4d47` the tinker-bridge itemises each call from the CLI's transcript as the CLI sends it (`cli-context.ts`, incremental, first-call remainder → tool schemas, later remainder → conversation / tool results / user message); `7171ed9945b` the UI applies it to exactly that call, never lets an anatomy row replace it, and draws one equal-width column per call (stacked buckets on top, reply by kind below, 2 px floors, the moral code shortened last). Rules and rejected alternatives: `context-window-panel.md` §5.3.
- **Tests:** `call-telemetry.test.ts` (wire shape), `cli-context.test.ts` + `stream.call-telemetry.test.ts` (36, 18 of them red with the reader and wiring stubbed; the bridge folder 293/293), `call-timeline.test.ts` (columns, floors, moral-code rule, composition parsing/application/guard/reload). Real browser, `tinker-ui/e2e/call-timeline/` 8/8: lanes painted across 99.5 % of the width (rate chart: 23.9 % top, 89.1 % bottom), legend with no "Unitemised"; develop's build fails checks 5 and 6.
